# 16. Sūrat al-Naḥl

**128 verses.** It is widely reported that it was revealed in the Meccan period; it is also reported that the closing verses (126-128 in particular) were revealed in the Medinan period. It takes its name from the word **ٱلنَّحْل** (*al-naḥl* — **the honey bee**) in the **sixty-eighth verse**.

**A second name for the sūra is also transmitted in the classical sources: *sūrat al-niʿam* — the sūra of favours.** I transmit this naming as a report; **but the ground of the name is verifiable from the text itself**, and it is the backbone of this commentary as well.

## The structure of the sūra

| Block | Verses | Subject | What closes the block |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | 1-2 | The coming of the command, and the descent of the angels with the *rūḥ* | *Fa-ttaqūn* (2) |
| **II** | 3-18 | **The first enumeration of favours** — from the livestock to the star | *Wa in taʿuddū niʿmata'llāhi lā tuḥṣūhā* (18) |
| **III** | 19-40 | The unresponsiveness of those called, arrogance, denial of the hereafter | *Kun fa-yakūn* (40) |
| **IV** | 41-64 | The emigration, the humanity of the messengers, the prostration of the shadows, the scene of the infant daughter | *Hudan wa raḥmatan li-qawmin yuʾminūn* (64) |
| **V** | 65-83 | **The second enumeration of favours** — water, milk, the bee, spouses, houses, garments | *Wa aktharuhumu'l-kāfirūn* (83) |
| **VI** | 84-89 | The day of the witnesses, and the sending down of the Book as *tibyān* | *Wa bushrā li'l-muslimīn* (89) |
| **VII** | 90-105 | The command of justice, the covenant, the woman who unravels her thread, the objection "you are substituting" | *Wa ulāʾika humu'l-kādhibūn* (105) |
| **VIII** | 106-128 | The dispensation under compulsion, the parable of the secure town, lawful and forbidden, Abraham (Ibrāhīm), the method of calling, patience | *Wa'lladhīna hum muḥsinūn* (128) |

**The backbone of the sūra is an *enumeration*.** It begins at the fifth verse, is cut off by a single sentence at the eighteenth, opens again at the sixty-fifth, and closes at the eighty-first. **Everything that passes in between — argument, history, ruling, parable — has been placed between these two enumerations.**

### The list of favours — a full inventory drawn from the text

**The table below is the order of the things the sūra enumerates. The order is the order of the verses themselves; nothing has been added.**

| # | Verse | What is enumerated | The word in the verse |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **5** | **The livestock** — warmth, benefits, food | *al-anʿām · difʾ · manāfiʿ* |
| 2 | **6** | **The beauty taken from the same animals** — the evening return and the morning release | *jamāl · turīḥūna · tasraḥūn* |
| 3 | **7** | **The carrying of loads** — to a land you could not reach by your own strength | *athqālakum · bi-shiqqi'l-anfus* |
| 4 | **8** | **Horses, mules, donkeys** — to ride, and adornment; and what you do not know | *al-khayl · al-bighāl · al-ḥamīr · zīna* |
| 5 | **10** | **Water from the sky** — drink, and trees in which you pasture | *sharāb · shajar · tusīmūn* |
| 6 | **11** | **Crops, olives, date palms, grapes, all the fruits** | *al-zarʿ · al-zaytūn · al-nakhīl · al-aʿnāb* |
| 7 | **12** | **Night, day, sun, moon; the stars made subservient** | *sakhkhara · musakhkharātun bi-amrih* |
| 8 | **13** | **What is strewn over the earth, differing in colour** | *dharaʾa · mukhtalifan alwānuh* |
| 9 | **14** | **The sea** — fresh meat, ornament, ships that cleave it | *laḥman ṭariyyā · ḥilya · mawākhir* |
| 10 | **15** | **Mountains, rivers, roads** | *rawāsī · anhār · subul* |
| 11 | **16** | **Landmarks, and the star** | *ʿalāmāt · bi'l-najmi hum yahtadūn* |
| — | **17-18** | **The enumeration is cut off**: a question and a ruling | *A-fa-man yakhluqu ka-man lā yakhluq* · *lā tuḥṣūhā* |
| 12 | **65** | **Water from the sky** — the reviving of dead ground | *fa-aḥyā bihi'l-arḍa baʿda mawtihā* |
| 13 | **66** | **Milk** — from between the *farth* and the blood | *labanan khāliṣan sāʾighan* |
| 14 | **67** | **The fruit of date palms and vines** — *sakar* and good provision | *sakaran wa rizqan ḥasanā* |
| 15 | **68-69** | **The bee** — houses, paths, a drink of differing colours, healing | *awḥā · buyūtā · shifāʾun li'l-nās* |
| 16 | **72** | **Spouses, sons, grandchildren, wholesome provisions** | *azwāj · banīn · ḥafada · ṭayyibāt* |
| 17 | **78** | **Hearing, eyes, hearts** — when you knew nothing at all | *al-samʿ · al-abṣār · al-afʾida* |
| 18 | **79** | **Birds held up in the air** | *musakhkharātin fī jawwi'l-samāʾ* |
| 19 | **80** | **Houses, and tents of hide; wool, fur, hair** | *sakanā · buyūtā · aṣwāf · awbār · ashʿār* |
| 20 | **81** | **Shade, shelters in the mountains, two kinds of garment** | *ẓilāl · aknān · sarābīl* |
| — | **end of 81** | **The enumeration closes**: with a sentence of purpose | *Ka-dhālika yutimmu niʿmatahū ʿalaykum laʿallakum tuslimūn* |

### And how the list is cut off

**This is the most conspicuous structural sign in the sūra, and it can be verified from the text:** nowhere does the enumeration end of itself. **At the end of every section there comes either a question or a ruling.**

| Where | The sentence that cuts in | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| **end of 11** | *Inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin **yatafakkarūn*** | **A qualification** — it names whom it addresses |
| **end of 12** | *Li-qawmin **yaʿqilūn*** | **A qualification** |
| **end of 13** | *Li-qawmin **yadhdhakkarūn*** | **A qualification** |
| **17** | ***A-fa-man yakhluqu ka-man lā yakhluq*** | **A question** |
| **18** | ***Wa in taʿuddū niʿmata'llāhi lā tuḥṣūhā*** | **A ruling** — the enumeration is declared impossible |
| **53-55** | *Thumma idhā kashafa'l-ḍurra ʿankum idhā farīqun minkum bi-rabbihim yushrikūn* | **A diagnosis** |
| **end of 71** | ***A-fa-bi-niʿmati'llāhi yajḥadūn*** | **A question** |
| **end of 72** | *A-fa-bi'l-bāṭili yuʾminūna wa bi-niʿmati'llāhi hum **yakfurūn*** | **A question** |
| **end of 81** | *Ka-dhālika yutimmu niʿmatahū ʿalaykum laʿallakum **tuslimūn*** | **A purpose** — why the enumeration is made |
| **83** | *Yaʿrifūna niʿmata'llāhi **thumma yunkirūnahā*** | **A diagnosis** — the enumeration closes |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is these ten points of interruption:** having enumerated the favours, the sūra does not send the reader back to the head of the list. **Each time it ties the list to a question or to a ruling.** The work of the enumeration is therefore not to give information; it is **to open an accounting.**

**And the eighteenth verse declares the impossibility of the list itself:** *lā tuḥṣūhā* — "you cannot count them". **In the very act of making an enumeration, the sūra says the enumeration will not be finished.** This is treated in detail below.

---

## 16/1 — أَتَىٰٓ أَمْرُ ٱللَّهِ فَلَا تَسْتَعْجِلُوهُ سُبْحَٰنَهُۥ وَتَعَٰلَىٰ عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ

*Atā amru'llāhi fa-lā tastaʿjilūh, subḥānahū wa taʿālā ʿammā yushrikūn*

"**The command of God has come; do not seek to hasten it.** He is far removed from, and exalted above, what they associate with Him."

### Telling of a future event with a perfect verb

**The whole sentence gathers into two expressions, and the entire matter lies in them: *atā* (it has come) — a perfect verb; and immediately after it *fa-lā tastaʿjilūh* (do not seek to hasten it) — a prohibition which shows that it has not yet come.**

**That is, the verse forbids that haste be asked for over the very thing it says has come. Had it come, no haste could be asked for.**

**This structure was analysed in detail at two places in this commentary, and I take them as the ground here:**

- **At `054-kamer.md`, 54/1** (*iqtarabati'l-sāʿatu wa'nshaqqa'l-qamar*) the **two distinct tasks** of the perfect verb were tabulated: **the expression of certainty** (a future event whose occurrence is certain being told as though it had already happened) and **the narration of an event genuinely past.** **And this verse was already named there as the example of the first kind.**
- **At `021-enbiya.md`, 21/1** (*iqtaraba li'l-nāsi ḥisābuhum*) the same pattern was treated once more, and the sense of *iqtaraba* that comes from Form VIII — **"the closing of a distance"** — was recorded.

**I do not repeat it here. What belongs here is the comparison of the three verses — for the three are not doing the same work:**

| Place | Verb | Mood | What it conveys | The sentence beside it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **al-Qamar 54/1** | *Iqtaraba* — Form VIII | Perfect | **The motion of drawing near** — the distance is closing | *Wa'nshaqqa'l-qamar* — an event |
| **al-Anbiyāʾ 21/1** | *Iqtaraba* — Form VIII | Perfect | **Drawing near** — and ascribed to those whose reckoning it is | *Wa hum fī ghaflatin muʿriḍūn* — a state |
| **al-Naḥl 16/1** | ***Atā*** — **Form I** | Perfect | **Arrival** — no distance left, it has come | *Fa-lā tastaʿjilūh* — **a prohibition** |

**The difference must be recorded, and I give this as my own reading, its ground being the choice of verb:** al-Qamar and al-Anbiyāʾ convey **drawing near** — *iqtaraba*. **al-Naḥl goes past even drawing near and conveys *arrival*: *atā*.** Of the three verses this is the one that goes furthest. **And it is precisely the verse that goes furthest which, immediately afterwards, forbids haste.**

**The contrast lies inside the sentence, and the lexicographers dwell on it.** In rhetoric this usage is generally called **the highest degree of certainty**: the certainty of the event is so complete that the future tense fell short and the perfect was used.

**And the addressee of the prohibition is worth recording.** *Lā tastaʿjilū* — **plural.** It is not said who is hastening; **but those who hasten are not the ones who do not want it to come, they are the ones who do.** This demand will be named several times in the verses that follow.

### أَمْر — root: أ-م-ر

**The root has two branches, and the lexicographers record both:**

| Branch | Sense | Plural |
|---|---|---|
| **أَمْر** (command) | To command, to order | *awāmir* |
| **أَمْر** (affair, event) | A situation, a state, a thing that comes to pass | *umūr* |

**There is disagreement over the usage here, and it must be set out in a table:**

| View | What *amru'llāh* is | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The Hour** | *Istiʿjāl* (asking for haste) is used in the Qurʾān mostly of the Hour and of punishment |
| 2 | **Punishment, retribution** | Further on in the sūra what befell earlier peoples is recalled (26, 33-34) |
| 3 | **God's decree / the decision He has taken** | The widest sense of the word; in the next verse *min amrih* occurs from the same root |

**All three views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But an internal note must be entered:** **the second verse uses the same root once more** — *yunazzilu'l-malāʾikata bi'l-rūḥi **min amrih***. **Two consecutive verses thus carry the same word:** in the one a *command* that comes, in the other a *command* that descends.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is this repetition:** the sūra sets the command whose hastening is forbidden beside **another command that is in the course of descending.** **What is awaited has not yet come; but news of what ought to be awaited has long since arrived.**

### سُبْحَٰنَهُۥ وَتَعَٰلَىٰ عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ

**The sentence will occur twice in the sūra: in the first verse, and at the end of the third** (*taʿālā ʿammā yushrikūn*). **Only one verse stands between them.** This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.

**The root of *subḥān* (س-ب-ح) has been analysed at many places in this commentary** — `087-ala.md`, `036-yasin.md` 36/36, `059-hasr.md` and others: **the core sense is to swim away through empty space or through water; from there, *tanzīh* — holding God clear of what does not befit Him.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**تَعَٰلَىٰ** — root **ع-ل-و**: height. **That it comes from Form VI (*taʿālā*) is worth recording:** this form generally conveys reciprocity, but here it does the work the lexicographers call *mubālagha* (intensification) — **that the height is of Himself.**

---

## 16/2 — يُنَزِّلُ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةَ بِٱلرُّوحِ مِنْ أَمْرِهِۦ عَلَىٰ مَن يَشَآءُ مِنْ عِبَادِهِۦٓ أَنْ أَنذِرُوٓا۟ أَنَّهُۥ لَآ إِلَٰهَ إِلَّآ أَنَا۠ فَٱتَّقُونِ

*Yunazzilu'l-malāʾikata bi'l-rūḥi min amrihī ʿalā man yashāʾu min ʿibādih, an andhirū annahū lā ilāha illā anā fa-ttaqūn*

"**He sends down the angels with the *rūḥ* of His command upon whom He wills of His servants:** '**Warn: there is no god but I; so guard yourselves against Me.**'"

### The transition between the two verses

**The first verse announced a future event; the second announces a work now under way.**

**The tense of the verb shows it: *atā* (perfect) → *yunazzilu* (imperfect).** **And *yunazzilu* is Form II** — the lexicographers record that here this form conveys **repetition and gradualness** (piece by piece, many times over).

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the event whose certainty is announced is named once and in the perfect; **the descent of the warning, as a verb still in progress.** The mood of the sentences thus separates the character of the two events.

### بِٱلرُّوحِ — the scope of the word here

**There is disagreement over the word, and it must be set out in a table:**

| View | What *rūḥ* is here | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Revelation** | That at al-Shūrā 42/52 the Qurʾān is called *rūḥan min amrinā*; the same qualification as *min amrih* here |
| 2 | **Gabriel** | That the phrases *rūḥ al-qudus* and *al-rūḥ al-amīn* are used of the angel |
| 3 | **Prophethood** | The sense of the word as that which gives life; the reviving of dead hearts |

**The first reading has the strongest internal ground, and I record this as an observation:** the qualification *min amrihī* in the verse **coincides word for word with the expression at al-Shūrā 42/52.** **In `042-sura.md` it was tabulated that the root و-ح-ي is the densest root of that sūra and that the channels of revelation are enumerated at 42/51-52; I rely on that treatment.** **Even so I do not impose a preference: all three views are transmitted, and two of them do not exclude one another** — revelation is what the angel carries.

**عَلَىٰ مَن يَشَآءُ مِنْ عِبَادِهِ** — and the one chosen is called a **servant**. **Messengership is thus given as a task that arises from within servanthood, not as a station that takes a man outside it.** This line will be drawn once more, and openly, in the forty-third verse of the sūra.

### أَنْ أَنذِرُوٓا۟ — the content of the command

**The imperative comes in the plural: *andhirū* — "warn".** Yet the one upon whom the revelation descends is singular (*man yashāʾu*).

**The lexicographers explain this in two ways:** either it is addressed to the angels, or it is because the word *man* is singular in form and plural in sense. **Both explanations are transmitted.**

**And the content of the warning is a single sentence: *annahū lā ilāha illā anā*.** **Worth recording:** the warning is delivered not with a threat but **with a statement.** The threat comes only at the end of the sentence, and as a command: *fa-ttaqūn*.

**And the change of person is striking; I record it as an observation about the word order:** the sentence begins in the third person (*yunazzilu … min amrihī … ʿibādihī*), then **shifts into the first** (*illā **anā*** … *fa-ttaqū**ni***). **In the middle of a report, the speaker Himself steps in.** In Arabic this shift is called *iltifāt* (a shift in the direction of address), and the lexicographers record that it does the work of **gathering attention.**

---

## 16/3-4 — خَلَقَ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضَ بِٱلْحَقِّ تَعَٰلَىٰ عَمَّا يُشْرِكُونَ · خَلَقَ ٱلْإِنسَٰنَ مِن نُّطْفَةٍ فَإِذَا هُوَ خَصِيمٌ مُّبِينٌ

*Khalaqa'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa bi'l-ḥaqq, taʿālā ʿammā yushrikūn · Khalaqa'l-insāna min nuṭfatin fa-idhā huwa khaṣīmun mubīn*

"**He created the heavens and the earth in truth;** He is exalted above what they associate with Him. · **He created man from a drop; and behold, he is an open disputant.**"

### The two verses opening with the same verb

**Both verses begin with the same word: *khalaqa*.** And the difference between their two objects establishes the whole evidential order of the sūra:

| Verse | What is created | Its outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **3** | **The heavens and the earth** | *Bi'l-ḥaqq* — **with a purpose** |
| **4** | **Man** | ***Khaṣīmun mubīn*** — **an open disputant** |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two verses stand back to back:** the same verb, two objects; the one is named with its purpose, the other **with its conduct.** **The universe keeps faith with its creation; man begins to dispute the moment after his.**

### فَإِذَا هُوَ خَصِيمٌ مُّبِينٌ

**This sentence occurs once more in the Qurʾān, in almost the same words, and it was treated there in detail.**

**At `036-yasin.md`, 36/77** (*a-wa-lam yara'l-insānu annā khalaqnāhu min nuṭfatin fa-idhā huwa khaṣīmun mubīn*) the following were recorded, and **I rely on that treatment:**

- ***Idhā fujāʾiyya*** (the *idhā* that conveys suddenness): "and behold". **The sentence leaps over every step between the drop and the disputant.**
- ***Khaṣīm* is on the *faʿīl* pattern, and in Arabic this pattern conveys a settled, fixed quality.** **Man is thus named not as "someone who disputes" but as "a disputant".**
- **The limit set by the method (`USLUP.md`) concerning *nuṭfa*:** the word is not equated one to one with the terms of modern embryology; **the evidence is that the beginning of man is not in his own hands.**

**I do not repeat it here. What belongs here is the difference between the contexts of the two verses, and that difference is large:**

| | **Yā Sīn 36/77** | **al-Naḥl 16/4** |
|---|---|---|
| The head of the sentence | *A-wa-lam **yara**'l-insān* — **a question** | *Khalaqa'l-insān* — **a statement** |
| Who is speaking | What man **has not seen** is said | What the Creator **has done** is said |
| Where it stands | At the **end** of the sūra — after the evidence | At the **beginning** of the sūra — **before** the evidence |
| What follows it | *Wa ḍaraba lanā mathalan wa **nasiya** khalqah* | ***Wa'l-anʿāma khalaqahā lakum*** — the enumeration begins |

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the position of the two verses within their sūras:** Yā Sīn uses this sentence as a **conclusion** — man's stance is named after all the evidence has been given. **al-Naḥl uses the same sentence as a *beginning*.** The diagnosis is entered first, the enumeration made afterwards.

**And this has a consequence for the sūra:** the ruling *lā tuḥṣūhā* (you cannot count them) that will come in the eighteenth verse rests upon the diagnosis of the fourth. **The one who cannot count and the one who disputes are the same person.**

### خَصِيم and the diagnosis of disputation

**The root خ-ص-م:** to contend, to be an adversary, to go to law. *Khuṣūma* — litigation; *khaṣm* — the opposing party.

**And this diagnosis must be read together with another root in this commentary. At `018-kehf.md`, 18/54, it was said of man:**

> *"Wa kāna'l-insānu **akthara shayʾin jadalā**"* — "Man is, more than anything else, contentious."

**What was recorded in the al-Kahf section was this, and I rely on it:** **the root ج-د-ل was the whole backbone of `040-gafir.md`**, and it was tabulated there that what is condemned is **disputation without ground** (*bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum*); **at `002-bakara.md`, 2/197, the concrete sense of the root — to twist a rope tight — was analysed.** And there was one further note in al-Kahf: **the tendency is identified (54), then a limit is set (22).**

**I set the three verses side by side, because the three give the same diagnosis in three different words:**

| Place | Word | Root | Pattern / form | What it says |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **al-Naḥl 16/4** | *Khaṣīmun mubīn* | خ-ص-م | *Faʿīl* — a settled attribute | **The attribute won at once by what came out of a drop** |
| Yā Sīn 36/77 | *Khaṣīmun mubīn* | خ-ص-م | *Faʿīl* | **The attribute after the evidence has been seen** |
| al-Kahf 18/54 | *Akthara shayʾin **jadalā*** | ج-د-ل | A specifying accusative — with *akthara* | **More than anything else** — comparative |

**And this must be recorded, because it bears on the close of the sūra:** having named man a **disputant** in the fourth verse, al-Naḥl teaches that same man the **method** of disputation in the hundred and twenty-fifth: *wa **jādilhum** bi'llatī hiya aḥsan.*

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two verses stand at the two ends of the sūra:** the sūra neither forbids disputation nor praises it. **In the fourth verse it identifies it as a fact; in the hundred and twenty-fifth it binds it to a form.** **This is the line recorded in `058-mucadele.md`, where these two verses were already named side by side:** *jadal* is **an act that takes its value from its subject and its manner.**

---

## 16/5 — وَٱلْأَنْعَٰمَ خَلَقَهَا لَكُمْ فِيهَا دِفْءٌ وَمَنَٰفِعُ وَمِنْهَا تَأْكُلُونَ

*Wa'l-anʿāma khalaqahā, lakum fīhā difʾun wa manāfiʿu wa minhā taʾkulūn*

"**And the livestock He created: in them there is warmth for you, and benefits; and of them you eat.**"

### The enumeration begins here

**And the construction of the opening must be recorded.** The sentence does not say *khalaqa'l-anʿāma lakum*. It says: *wa'l-anʿāma khalaqahā* — **the object is brought forward, and then the verb takes it up again with a pronoun.**

**In Arabic this is called *ishtighāl*, and the lexicographers record that it conveys *ihtimām* (giving prominence).** **The first item of the enumeration is thus brought forward at the level of word order too.**

**And the choice of that first item is worth recording:** the enumeration begins not from the sky and not from the sea but **from just outside the door of the house.**

### دِفْء — root: د-ف-أ

**The concrete sense of the root: to grow warm, warmth.** *Difāʾ* — the thing that warms; *dafiya* — he grew warm.

**The lexicographers explain the word here specifically as clothing made from wool, hair and hide — that is, not the heat the animal gives off but the warming thing got from the animal.** **And this explanation has an internal ground:** in the eighty-first verse *sarābīla **taqīkumu'l-ḥarr*** (garments that guard you from the heat) will occur, and in the eightieth *wa min **aṣwāfihā** wa **awbārihā** wa **ashʿārihā*** (from their wool, their fur and their hair).

**What the sūra says in a single word in the fifth verse it thus repeats, unfolded, in the eightieth and eighty-first.** This is a weave that can be verified from the text.

### Three benefits, three distinct levels

**The verse enumerates three things, and their order is meaningful:**

| Order | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Difʾ* | **Warmth** — staying alive |
| 2 | *Manāfiʿ* | **Benefits** — left indefinite, plural |
| 3 | *Wa minhā taʾkulūn* | **Food** |

**The middle item is worth recording: *manāfiʿ* is plural and its content is not given.** **Even in its first item, then, the list is left open within itself.** This is a small rehearsal of what will be done openly in the eighth verse with *wa yakhluqu mā lā taʿlamūn*.

**At `047-muhammed.md`, 47/12, it was already recorded that the word *anʿām* is tied to the list of favours in this sūra;** I rely on that treatment.

---

## 16/6 — وَلَكُمْ فِيهَا جَمَالٌ حِينَ تُرِيحُونَ وَحِينَ تَسْرَحُونَ

*Wa lakum fīhā jamālun ḥīna turīḥūna wa ḥīna tasraḥūn*

"**And in them there is beauty for you, when you bring them home at evening and when you drive them out at morning.**"

### I treat this verse in detail

**The preceding verse enumerated three benefits: warmth, benefits, food. This verse adds a fourth thing, and what it adds is not a benefit.**

***Jamāl* — beauty.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two verses stand back to back: having enumerated utility, the sūra does not stop; it sets beauty beside utility.** And it does so with a separate sentence, a separate word and a separate note of time.

**Three things must be recorded besides:**

**One — the beauty comes with the qualification *lakum* (for you).** The verse does not say "these animals are beautiful". It says: **"in them there is beauty for you."** Beauty is given not as a property of the object but as **a relation established between the beholder and the object.**

**Two — the beauty is bound to two *moments*.** It is stated not as a general condition but for **two definite times**:

| Verb | Root | Time | The scene |
|---|---|---|---|
| **تُرِيحُونَ** (*turīḥūn*) | ر-و-ح | **Towards evening** | The **return** of the herd from pasture |
| **تَسْرَحُونَ** (*tasraḥūn*) | س-ر-ح | **Morning** | The **releasing** of the herd to pasture |

***Rawāḥ* — to come back towards evening; *al-murāḥ* is the place where the animals pass the night.** **At `030-rum.md`, 30/17-18, the enumeration of the times of day and the building of the pair *ʿishāʾ* / *ibkār* were treated;** the two times here are of the same family, but the scale is different: there the times were enumerated for **glorification**, here for **looking**.

***Sarḥ* — to let loose, to set free.** In the root there is **the untying of a bond**; *sarraḥahū* — he set it loose, let it go.

**Three — the order in the wording is reversed, and this is striking.** The natural order would be to begin with morning and end with evening. **The verse says first *turīḥūn* (the evening return), then *tasraḥūn* (the morning release).**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the order itself:** the return is named before the release. **And of the two scenes it is the return that fills the eye more** — the herd comes in a body, with dust, with sound, in the evening light. **The morning release is a scattering.** In naming the beauty the verse thus puts **what gathers** first and **what scatters** second.

**I do not impose a preference, and I do not claim that this is a rule of rhetoric; the order itself is a fact verifiable from the text, the reading of it is mine.**

### The placing of beauty beside utility — my own reading

**I open this item separately, because it bears on the whole logic of the sūra's enumeration.**

**When both the function and the look of a thing are enumerated in the Qurʾān, the two are generally not joined in one sentence; they are named separately.** So it is in this sūra:

| Verse | Function | Look / adornment |
|---|---|---|
| **5-6** | *Difʾ · manāfiʿ · taʾkulūn* | ***Jamāl*** (6) |
| **8** | *Li-tarkabūhā* — to ride | ***Zīna*** — adornment |
| **14** | *Laḥman ṭariyyā* — fresh meat | ***Ḥilya*** — ornament |
| **80-81** | *Sakanā · buyūtā · taqīkum* — shelter and protection | *Athāthan wa matāʿan* — furnishing and equipment |

**The same pattern in all four places: first what works, then what shows.** **This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.**

**And I record this as my own reading:** in describing a favour the sūra **does not take sufficiency as its measure.** An animal could already have been enumerated because it is eaten and carries loads; the verse enumerates the scene of the evening return as well. **A sea could have been enumerated because it gives fresh meat; the verse enumerates the ornament as well.**

**The list, then, does not hold needs alone — it holds what is not needed as well.** And part of the force of the ruling *lā tuḥṣūhā* (you cannot count them) in the eighteenth verse comes from this: **the boundary of what is to be counted is not the boundary of staying alive.**

**The ground of this reading is that twofold structure in four verses; I do not rest it on any transmitted report besides.**

### What this says to the present

**The verse records how a society looks at the means of its own livelihood.** The animal is named here not only as a unit of production but also as **something looked at.**

**I record this as an observation:** there is a difference between measuring a creature solely by what it yields and taking something from the sight of it as well — **and by putting the two on the same list the verse does not abolish that difference; on the contrary, it makes it visible.**

---

## 16/7 — وَتَحْمِلُ أَثْقَالَكُمْ إِلَىٰ بَلَدٍ لَّمْ تَكُونُوا۟ بَٰلِغِيهِ إِلَّا بِشِقِّ ٱلْأَنفُسِ إِنَّ رَبَّكُمْ لَرَءُوفٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*Wa taḥmilu athqālakum ilā baladin lam takūnū bālighīhi illā bi-shiqqi'l-anfus, inna rabbakum la-raʾūfun raḥīm*

"**And they carry your loads to a land you could not reach save by straining your very selves.** Your Lord is truly most kind, most merciful."

### بِشِقِّ ٱلْأَنفُسِ — root: ش-ق-ق

**The root was treated in full in `084-insikak.md`, and a summary was given at `054-kamer.md`, 54/1. I rely on that treatment.** What is needed here from those notes is this:

> **The concrete sense of the root is to split, to cleave in two.** ***مَشَقَّة* (*mashaqqa*) is hardship — on the lexicographers' explanation, that which splits a man.**

**The construction here must be recorded:** *bi-shiqqi'l-anfus* — **"with the splitting of the selves".**

**The verse does not say "with difficulty". Nor does it use the word *mashaqqa*.** It chooses the **most concrete form** of the root, and it names the object too: *anfus* — the selves, their own persons.

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the concreteness of the word:** the cost of the journey is pictured not as time or money spent but as **a piece torn from the person himself.**

### The logic of the sentence

**And the equation the verse sets up is worth recording:**

| Without the animal | With the animal |
|---|---|
| *Lam takūnū bālighīhi* — **you cannot reach it** | *Taḥmilu athqālakum* — **they carry your loads** |
| *Illā bi-shiqqi'l-anfus* — **save by splitting your selves** | (no cost is named) |

**The favour is thus described not as "a thing given" but as a cost lifted.**

**I give this as an observation, and it matters for the sūra's logic of enumeration:** some of the items on the list are things given (milk, fruit, shade), and some are **costs not paid.** The second kind is the harder to notice — for a hardship that is absent is invisible.

### إِنَّ رَبَّكُمْ لَرَءُوفٌ رَّحِيمٌ

**The verse closes with two names, and the lexicographers record the difference between them:**

| Name | Root | The lexicographers' distinction |
|---|---|---|
| **رَءُوف** | ر-أ-ف | **Tenderness** — seeing pain and shrinking from it; finer, more particular |
| **رَحِيم** | ر-ح-م | **Mercy** — encompassing, issuing in an effect |

**The lexicographers record that *raʾfa* is sharper and narrower than *raḥma*; while *raḥma* can reach the good and the wicked alike.** **I transmit this distinction as a relation the lexicographers set up, not as a settled ruling.**

**And that these two names are invoked here is worth recording:** the subject of the sentence is a pack animal. **The names come after something as everyday as that.**

---

## 16/8 — وَٱلْخَيْلَ وَٱلْبِغَالَ وَٱلْحَمِيرَ لِتَرْكَبُوهَا وَزِينَةً وَيَخْلُقُ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ

*Wa'l-khayla wa'l-bighāla wa'l-ḥamīra li-tarkabūhā wa zīnah, wa yakhluqu mā lā taʿlamūn*

"**And horses, mules and donkeys — for you to ride, and as adornment. And He creates what you do not know.**"

### Three animals, two purposes

**The sentence enumerates two purposes, and the two come in different grammatical forms:**

| Purpose | Expression | Form |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Li-tarkabūhā* | ***Lām* + verb** — for you to ride |
| 2 | *Wa zīnatan* | **A noun in the accusative** — as adornment |

**The lexicographers dwell on the case of the second word** (whether it is a causal object, or governed by an elided verb). **But in point of meaning there is no difference, and what must be recorded is this:** the work done in the sixth verse with *jamāl* (beauty) is repeated here with *zīna* (adornment).

**The lexicographers give the difference between the two words thus, and I transmit it:** *jamāl* is beauty **residing in the thing itself**; *zīna* is an adornment **added, laid over it.** **In the sixth verse, then, the sight of the animal is named; here, the appearance the animal lends its owner.** I give this distinction as a tendency the lexicographers record, not as an absolute rule.

### وَيَخْلُقُ مَا لَا تَعْلَمُونَ — the leaving open of the list

**This is one of the most striking sentences in the sūra, and it must be handled with care.**

**The verse enumerated three animals. Then it did not close the list.**

**This structure was treated in detail in this commentary, and I rely on it: at `036-yasin.md`, 36/36** (*subḥāna'lladhī khalaqa'l-azwāja kullahā mimmā tunbitu'l-arḍu wa min anfusihim **wa mimmā lā yaʿlamūn***) the following was recorded:

> **"The third item comes in the same grammatical structure as the first two — the same *min*, the same *mā*. It is enumerated as a part of the list, with full standing. But its content is not given."**
>
> **"The verse has deliberately left the third item unfilled. To fill it with the knowledge of any one period breaks the work the verse plainly does — for the moment it is filled, the item ceases to be 'what they do not know'."**

**And in the Yā Sīn section this verse (al-Naḥl 16/8) was already named. I close the ring from this end.**

**The difference that belongs here must be recorded, and the two verses are not doing the same thing:**

| | **Yā Sīn 36/36** | **al-Naḥl 16/8** |
|---|---|---|
| The kind of sentence | ***Glorification*** — *subḥāna'lladhī* | **Enumeration** — a list of favours |
| Who does not know | *Lā **yaʿlamūn*** — **third person** | *Lā **taʿlamūn*** — **the one addressed: you** |
| What is left open | The third source of the **pairs** created | The continuation of the **mounts** created |
| The tense of the verb | *Khalaqa* — perfect | ***Wa yakhluqu*** — **imperfect** |

**The difference of tense is the most important, and I record this as my own reading:** in Yā Sīn the creating is named as **done and finished**; **in al-Naḥl, *wa yakhluqu* — it is still going on.** **The list is therefore left open here not merely from a want of knowledge but because the work continues.**

**And the difference of person must be recorded second:** in Yā Sīn those who do not know are "they" — a group being described. **In al-Naḥl the one who does not know is the addressee himself: "you."** The sentence states the limit of the reader's own knowledge.

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`)

**This sentence is one of the places often used for claims of scientific miracle:** filling the phrase "things you do not know" with the vehicles known today, with technology, or with some other phenomenon is a widespread interpretation.

**This commentary does not take that road, and the reason is the same as the one written in `036-yasin.md`:** the moment the item is filled it ceases to be "what you do not know". **The force of the sentence comes not from its content but from its having been left empty.**

**I add this as my own reading, and my ground is the enumeration as a whole:** the eighteenth verse will say *lā tuḥṣūhā* (you cannot count them). **The eighth verse shows how that ruling is possible:** the list does not close, that it might be counted.

---

## 16/9 — وَعَلَى ٱللَّهِ قَصْدُ ٱلسَّبِيلِ وَمِنْهَا جَآئِرٌ وَلَوْ شَآءَ لَهَدَىٰكُمْ أَجْمَعِينَ

*Wa ʿala'llāhi qaṣdu'l-sabīli wa minhā jāʾir, wa law shāʾa la-hadākum ajmaʿīn*

"**Upon God rests the pointing of the straight way; and among the ways there is one that swerves.** Had He willed He would have guided you all."

### A sentence about the way in the middle of the enumeration

**It must be recorded:** the eighth verse enumerated the mounts, the tenth will enumerate water. **A sentence about the *way* has come in between.**

**I give this as my own reading; its ground is the position of the verse:** where mounts are spoken of it is natural that the road be spoken of; **but the verse leaves the physical road and passes over to direction.** The enumeration slides from the place to be reached to **the bearing to be held.** **And in the fifteenth verse *subul* (ways) will come back, this time in the physical sense.** The sūra uses the same word on two planes.

### قَصْدُ ٱلسَّبِيلِ — root: ق-ص-د

**The concrete sense of the root: to head straight in one direction; and to be middling, balanced.** *Qaṣd* — heading towards, intent; *muqtaṣid* — one who holds the middle way; *iqtiṣād* — moderation. **The root occurred at `031-lokman.md`, 31/19 (*wa'qṣid fī mashyik* — be moderate in your walking) and at `031-lokman.md`, 31/32 (*fa-minhum muqtaṣid*).**

**The construction is read in two ways, and both are transmitted:**

| Reading | Sense |
|---|---|
| 1 | **"The straight one of the ways"** — a genitive with the force of an adjective |
| 2 | **"The directing towards the way"** — with the force of a verbal noun; the act of pointing |

**I do not impose a preference.**

**وَمِنْهَا جَآئِرٌ** — *jāʾir*, from the root **ج-و-ر**: **to swerve, to bend aside, to depart from what is right.** *Jawr* — injustice.

**And the pronoun must be recorded: *minhā* — "of them".** The verse does not say openly what the pronoun refers to; the lexicographers record that it is **the ways**, and that this is understood from the word *sabīl* carrying a plural sense as a generic noun.

**The word order of the sentence sets up a balance:** the pointing of the straight way **rests upon God** (*wa ʿala'llāh*), while the swerving way is named **without an agent** (*wa minhā jāʾir*). **The one is taken upon Himself; of the other it is only said that it exists.**

### وَلَوْ شَآءَ لَهَدَىٰكُمْ أَجْمَعِينَ

***Law* — a condition contrary to fact.** The sentence tells of something that did not happen.

**The same structure will come once more in the sūra, in the thirty-fifth verse** (*law shāʾa'llāhu mā ʿabadnā min dūnihī min shayʾ*) — **but there it is the addressees who frame the sentence, and they frame it as an excuse.** **This is an overlap verifiable within the sūra, and it will be treated at the thirty-fifth verse.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the comparison of these two occurrences:** the same sentence, once in the mouth of the text and once in the mouth of the addressee. **The text says it as a piece of *knowledge*; the addressee says it as a *defence*.** The difference between them lies not in the truth of the sentence but in who uses it and for what.

---

## 16/10-11 — هُوَ ٱلَّذِىٓ أَنزَلَ مِنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ مَآءً لَّكُم مِّنْهُ شَرَابٌ وَمِنْهُ شَجَرٌ فِيهِ تُسِيمُونَ · يُنۢبِتُ لَكُم بِهِ ٱلزَّرْعَ وَٱلزَّيْتُونَ وَٱلنَّخِيلَ وَٱلْأَعْنَٰبَ وَمِن كُلِّ ٱلثَّمَرَٰتِ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يَتَفَكَّرُونَ

*Huwa'lladhī anzala mina'l-samāʾi māʾan, lakum minhu sharābun wa minhu shajarun fīhi tusīmūn · Yunbitu lakum bihi'l-zarʿa wa'l-zaytūna wa'l-nakhīla wa'l-aʿnāba wa min kulli'l-thamarāt, inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin yatafakkarūn*

"**It is He who sends down water from the sky: from it you drink, and from it come the trees in which you pasture your beasts.** With it He brings forth for you the crops, the olive, the date palm, the vine and fruits of every kind. **In that there is a sign for a people who think.**"

### The two shares of the water

**The sentence divides the water in two and gives it with two *min*s:**

| Share | Expression | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Lakum minhu **sharāb*** | **To man** — drink |
| 2 | *Wa minhu **shajarun fīhi tusīmūn*** | **To the animal** — pasture |

**Worth recording:** the verse names man's share first, but it enumerates the animal's share in the same sentence. **And both are tied to the same water.**

**تُسِيمُونَ** — root **س-و-م**: **to send an animal out to pasture and let it graze freely.** *Sāʾima* — an animal turned out to pasture, not given fodder. **It is recorded that the word occurs in this form only here in the Qurʾān.**

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** in the sixth verse *tasraḥūn* (you drive them out), here *tusīmūn* (you pasture them). **The two verbs tell of the same act from two different sides:** the one, the **sight** of the driving out; the other, its **yield.**

### Four crops and one scope

**The eleventh verse enumerates four crops, then opens the scope:**

| Order | Crop | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *al-Zarʿ* | **Sown crops** — grain, the staple food |
| 2 | *al-Zaytūn* | **The olive** — oil |
| 3 | *al-Nakhīl* | **The date palm** |
| 4 | *al-Aʿnāb* | **The vine** |
| — | *Wa min kulli'l-thamarāt* | **And of fruits of every kind** |

**The same pattern again: things are enumerated, then the list is left open.** The work done in the eighth verse with *wa yakhluqu mā lā taʿlamūn* is done here with *wa min kulli'l-thamarāt*. **This is a repetition that can be verified from the text.**

**نَخِيل وَأَعْنَاب** — **the pair date palm and vine is named together regularly in the Qurʾān**, and **this pair was treated at `023-muminun.md`, 23/19;** it was recorded there that the two plants are **dependent on water in an arid climate** and **can be stored.** **The same pair occurred at `036-yasin.md`, 36/34.** **I rely on those treatments.**

**And this pair will come back once more in the sūra, in the sixty-seventh verse** — this time with the crop in its **processed form** (*tattakhidhūna minhu sakaran wa rizqan ḥasanā*). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

### لِّقَوْمٍ يَتَفَكَّرُونَ — the series of closings begins

**From the eleventh verse on, the enumeration is divided into sections by closing sentences, and each closing uses a different verb:**

| Verse | Closing | Verb |
|---|---|---|
| **11** | *Li-qawmin **yatafakkarūn*** | **To think** |
| **12** | *Li-qawmin **yaʿqilūn*** | **To reason** |
| **13** | *Li-qawmin **yadhdhakkarūn*** | **To remember / to take heed** |
| **65** | *Li-qawmin **yasmaʿūn*** | **To hear** |
| **67** | *Li-qawmin **yaʿqilūn*** | **To reason** |
| **69** | *Li-qawmin **yatafakkarūn*** | **To think** |
| **79** | *Li-qawmin **yuʾminūn*** | **To believe** |

**Seven closings, five distinct verbs.** **This does the same work as the closings of the ***wa min āyātihī*** series tabulated at `030-rum.md`, 30/20-25** — there four closings addressed four distinct capacities (to think, to know, to hear, to reason). **I rely on that treatment and record the difference:**

| | **al-Rūm 30/20-25** | **al-Naḥl 16/11-79** |
|---|---|---|
| What opens the pattern | ***Wa min āyātihī*** — a repeated opening | **No opening formula** — each verse begins differently |
| Number of closings | 4 (inside the series) | **7 (spread across the sūra)** |
| The shape of the series | **Closed** — 20 and 25 frame it | **Open** — from 11 to 79 |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is how the two sūras use the formula:** al-Rūm builds a series and frames it; **al-Naḥl scatters its closings through the sūra.** **In al-Rūm the signs are gathered into a single section; in al-Naḥl they are worked into the weave of the sūra.**

---

## 16/12-13 — وَسَخَّرَ لَكُمُ ٱلَّيْلَ وَٱلنَّهَارَ وَٱلشَّمْسَ وَٱلْقَمَرَ وَٱلنُّجُومُ مُسَخَّرَٰتٌۢ بِأَمْرِهِۦٓ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَٰتٍ لِّقَوْمٍ يَعْقِلُونَ · وَمَا ذَرَأَ لَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مُخْتَلِفًا أَلْوَٰنُهُۥٓ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يَذَّكَّرُونَ

*Wa sakhkhara lakumu'l-layla wa'l-nahāra wa'l-shamsa wa'l-qamar, wa'l-nujūmu musakhkharātun bi-amrih, inna fī dhālika la-āyātin li-qawmin yaʿqilūn · Wa mā dharaʾa lakum fi'l-arḍi mukhtalifan alwānuh, inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin yadhdhakkarūn*

"**He has made the night, the day, the sun and the moon subservient to you; and the stars are made subservient by His command.** In that there are signs for a people who reason. · **And what He has strewn for you over the earth, of differing colours.** In that there is a sign for a people who take heed."

### سَخَّرَ — root: س-خ-ر

**The root has two branches, and the lexicographers record both:** **to make subservient, to set to a task** (*taskhīr*); and **to mock** (*sukhriyya*). **The shared idea, on the lexicographers' explanation, is the using of a thing outside its own will.**

**The root was treated at `031-lokman.md`, 31/20 and 31/29** (*sakhkhara lakum mā fi'l-samāwāti wa mā fi'l-arḍ*); **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is the splitting of the sentence in two, and this must be recorded:**

| Part | Expression | Form |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Sakhkhara **lakum** al-layla wa'l-nahāra wa'l-shamsa wa'l-qamar* | **A verbal sentence** — with the qualification *lakum* |
| 2 | *Wa'l-nujūmu **musakhkharātun bi-amrih*** | **A nominal sentence** — **no** *lakum* qualification |

**The difference is plain and can be verified from the text:** the night, the day, the sun and the moon are made subservient **"for you"**. **For the stars this qualification is not entered; they are named only as "made subservient by His command".**

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the absence of the *lakum* qualification in the second sentence:** the verse does not divide what serves man from what does not — **but it does mark off man's share separately.** The stars too are within the order; but their order is not reduced to man's service.

**And in the sixteenth verse this will be completed: *wa bi'l-najmi hum yahtadūn*** — there the face the star turns towards man will be named. **This is a completion verifiable within the sūra.**

**A note on the variant readings:** it is transmitted that in some readings *wa'l-nujūmu musakhkharātun* is read *wa'l-nujūma musakhkharātin* — on that reading the clause ceases to be a nominal sentence and attaches to the preceding verb. **In point of meaning the difference is that the distinction recorded above falls away.** **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference, and I state that my reading above rests on the widespread reading.**

### ذَرَأَ — root: ذ-ر-أ

**The concrete sense of the root: to spread as one scatters seed, to distribute by multiplying.** *Dhurriyya* — offspring; the lexicographers record that it is attached to the same root. **The root occurred at `067-mulk.md`, 67/24 (*huwa'lladhī dharaʾakum fi'l-arḍ*).**

### مُخْتَلِفًا أَلْوَٰنُهُۥ — the fourth link

**This construction was treated in detail in this commentary. At `035-fatir.md`, 35/27-28, it occurred three times and was tabulated there:**

| Domain | Expression | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Plant** | *Thamarātin mukhtalifan alwānuhā* | Fāṭir 35/27 |
| **Stone** | *Judadun bīḍun wa ḥumrun mukhtalifun alwānuhā* | Fāṭir 35/27 |
| **Living creature** | *Mina'l-nāsi wa'l-dawābbi wa'l-anʿāmi mukhtalifun alwānuhū* | Fāṭir 35/28 |
| **Everything strewn on the earth** | ***Wa mā dharaʾa lakum fi'l-arḍi mukhtalifan alwānuh*** | **al-Naḥl 16/13** |

**This is the fourth link, and its difference must be recorded:** in Fāṭir the construction was repeated separately for **three distinct domains** — plant, stone, living creature. **In al-Naḥl it is given in a single sentence, with no division: *mā dharaʾa lakum fi'l-arḍ* — "what He has strewn for you over the earth."**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the scope of the two expressions:** Fāṭir enumerates by **separating**, al-Naḥl by **gathering.** **And there is one further qualification in al-Naḥl's sentence: *lakum*.** In Fāṭir the diversity was a matter for observation; **in al-Naḥl it is an item of favour.** The same fact, entered in two different columns.

**And at `030-rum.md`, 30/22, the same diversity was treated by way of tongue and colour, and compared there with Fāṭir's list of colours and with `049-hucurat.md`, 49/13.** **I rely on that treatment and add one further link here.**

---

## 16/14 — وَهُوَ ٱلَّذِى سَخَّرَ ٱلْبَحْرَ لِتَأْكُلُوا۟ مِنْهُ لَحْمًا طَرِيًّا وَتَسْتَخْرِجُوا۟ مِنْهُ حِلْيَةً تَلْبَسُونَهَا وَتَرَى ٱلْفُلْكَ مَوَاخِرَ فِيهِ وَلِتَبْتَغُوا۟ مِن فَضْلِهِۦ وَلَعَلَّكُمْ تَشْكُرُونَ

*Wa huwa'lladhī sakhkhara'l-baḥra li-taʾkulū minhu laḥman ṭariyyan wa tastakhrijū minhu ḥilyatan talbasūnahā, wa tara'l-fulka mawākhira fīhi wa li-tabtaghū min faḍlihī wa laʿallakum tashkurūn*

"**And it is He who made the sea subservient to you: that you might eat fresh meat from it and bring out of it ornaments to wear. And you see the ships cleaving through it** — that you might seek of His bounty, and that you might give thanks."

### An exact overlap with Fāṭir

**The first half of this sentence occurred at `035-fatir.md`, 35/12, in almost the same words:** *wa min kullin taʾkulūna laḥman ṭariyyan wa tastakhrijūna ḥilyatan talbasūnahā.* **I rely on that treatment.**

**And what was recorded in the Fāṭir section can be carried over here:** the two seas **are not equal**, yet benefit is drawn **from both** — the verse named the shared benefit after stating the inequality.

**The difference here must be recorded, and the two verses are not doing the same work:**

| | **Fāṭir 35/12** | **al-Naḥl 16/14** |
|---|---|---|
| The work of the sentence | **Comparison** — *wa mā yastawi'l-baḥrān* | **Enumeration** — an item on the list of favours |
| Number of seas | **Two** — sweet and salt | **One** — *al-baḥr*, generic |
| What is added | *Wa tara'l-fulka fīhi mawākhir* | The word ***mawākhir*** is **peculiar to al-Naḥl** |

### مَوَاخِر — root: م-خ-ر

**The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root thus: to cleave the water, to go forward cleaving, and to make a sound in doing so.** *Makhr* — the ship's cleaving of the water; *mākhira* — that which cleaves.

**The picture in the word is worth recording:** the verse does not name the ship as "floating" or as "going". It names it as **cleaving.**

**And I give this as an observation:** in the seventh verse it was said that man travels by splitting his own self (*bi-shiqqi'l-anfus*). **Here the ship that cleaves the sea is named.** What the two verses have in common is the idea of **cleaving**; but the one takes place in the man himself, the other in the water. **I record this not as a claim of kinship between roots — the roots differ (ش-ق-ق and م-خ-ر) — but as a likeness of picture.**

### Four purposes, two forms

**The verse enumerates four purposes:**

| Purpose | Expression | Form |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Li-taʾkulū laḥman ṭariyyā* | ***Lām*** + verb |
| 2 | *Wa tastakhrijū ḥilyatan* | Attached to the preceding |
| 3 | *Wa **li**-tabtaghū min faḍlih* | ***Lām*** + verb — **repeated** |
| 4 | *Wa **laʿalla**kum tashkurūn* | ***Laʿalla*** — the particle of hope, of purpose |

**Worth recording: in the third purpose the *lām* is repeated.** The lexicographers record that this repetition serves to re-establish the connection after **the sentence that comes between** (*wa tara'l-fulka mawākhira fīh*).

**And the form of the last purpose changes: *laʿalla*.** **This sets the fourth item apart from the rest** — the first three are **outcomes**, the fourth **the return that is looked for.**

**The same distinction was treated at `030-rum.md`, 30/46:** the last link of the *wa min āyātihī* series ended with *wa laʿallakum tashkurūn*, and it was recorded there — **the series is built out of verbs of understanding, and the link that stands apart ends with a verb of response.** **I rely on that treatment and record that the same structure is found here within a single verse.**

---

## 16/15-16 — وَأَلْقَىٰ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ رَوَٰسِىَ أَن تَمِيدَ بِكُمْ وَأَنْهَٰرًا وَسُبُلًا لَّعَلَّكُمْ تَهْتَدُونَ · وَعَلَٰمَٰتٍ وَبِٱلنَّجْمِ هُمْ يَهْتَدُونَ

*Wa alqā fi'l-arḍi rawāsiya an tamīda bikum wa anhāran wa subulan laʿallakum tahtadūn · Wa ʿalāmāt, wa bi'l-najmi hum yahtadūn*

"**And He cast firm mountains into the earth, lest it sway with you, and rivers and roads** — that you might find your way. · **And landmarks. And by the star they find their way.**"

### وَأَلْقَىٰ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ رَوَٰسِىَ أَن تَمِيدَ بِكُمْ

**This sentence occurred at `031-lokman.md`, 31/10, in exactly the same words, and was treated there. I rely on that treatment.** The notes there were these:

- **رَوَٰسِى — root ر-س-و: to drop anchor, to be held fast.** **The root's connection with a ship's anchoring was recorded at `050-kaf.md`, 50/7.**
- **أَن تَمِيدَ بِكُمْ — *mayd*: to sway, to be tossed about.** **The lexicographers use the word especially of seasickness and of the rolling of a ship.**
- **And the note required by the method (`USLUP.md`) entered in Luqmān holds here too, and I repeat it:** no conclusion about modern geology, about isostasy or about gravitation is drawn from this. **The function of the verse is to say that an order visible to the eye stands without any visible support.**

**The difference that belongs here must be recorded, and the two verses do not stand in the same context:**

| | **Luqmān 31/10** | **al-Naḥl 16/15** |
|---|---|---|
| What precedes the sentence | *Khalaqa'l-samāwāti bi-ghayri ʿamadin tarawnahā* — **pillars you do not see** | *Sakhkhara'l-baḥr* — **the sea** |
| What follows the sentence | *Wa baththa fīhā min kulli dābba* — **living creatures** | *Wa anhāran wa subulā* — **rivers and roads** |
| How the block ends | *Fa-arūnī mādhā khalaqa'lladhīna min dūnih* — **a challenge** | *Laʿallakum tahtadūn* — **finding one's way** |

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is how the two blocks close:** Luqmān ties the mountains to a **challenge** — "what have you created?" **al-Naḥl ties the same mountains to a sentence about *finding one's way*.** **The same fact is thus enumerated in the one as evidence, in the other as a possibility opened.**

### أَنْهَٰرًا وَسُبُلًا — and a return to the eighth verse

**It must be recorded: in the ninth verse the word *sabīl* occurred in the sense of a bearing** (*wa ʿala'llāhi qaṣdu'l-sabīl*). **Here the same root comes back in the physical sense: *subulā* — roads.**

**And in both places the closing is tied to the same root:** *la-hadākum* in the ninth verse, *laʿallakum tahtadūn* in the fifteenth, *hum yahtadūn* in the sixteenth. **The root ه-د-ي occurs four times in three verses.**

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and I record this as my own reading:** the sūra tells of finding one's way on two planes at once — **finding the road across the earth, and finding the straight way.** The two planes are named with the same root, and no boundary is set between them.

### وَعَلَٰمَٰتٍ وَبِٱلنَّجْمِ هُمْ يَهْتَدُونَ

**The sixteenth verse is among the shortest in the sūra, and it says two things.**

**عَلَٰمَٰت** — root **ع-ل-م**: to know. *ʿAlāma* — **the mark by which a thing is known.** **That the word comes from the root of knowing is worth recording:** a mark is not knowledge itself; **it is what leads to knowledge.**

**The lexicographers dwell on what the *ʿalāmāt* are here:** the mountains, stones, hills and stars that give bearing on a journey. **No definite list is given, and I do not give one either.**

**وَبِٱلنَّجْمِ هُمْ يَهْتَدُونَ** — and the wording shifts. **The sentence passes into the third person: *hum yahtadūn* — "they find their way".**

**Yet the preceding verse was in the second person: *laʿallakum tahtadūn* — "that you might find your way."**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse names the finding of one's way by the star not as something **commanded of the addressee** but as something **actually done.** **What is recorded is that a possibility is in use.**

**And the word is singular: *bi'l-**najm***.** The lexicographers take it as a generic noun — not "by the star" but "by the stars". **But another reason for its coming in the singular is transmitted:** a pointing to one particular star used in finding direction. **Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And a ring closes with the twelfth verse:** there the stars were named as *musakhkharātun bi-amrih*, and **no qualification belonging to man was entered.** **Here that qualification comes.** **This is a completion verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/17-18 — أَفَمَن يَخْلُقُ كَمَن لَّا يَخْلُقُ أَفَلَا تَذَكَّرُونَ · وَإِن تَعُدُّوا۟ نِعْمَةَ ٱللَّهِ لَا تُحْصُوهَآ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَغَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*A-fa-man yakhluqu ka-man lā yakhluq, a-fa-lā tadhakkarūn · Wa in taʿuddū niʿmata'llāhi lā tuḥṣūhā, inna'llāha la-ghafūrun raḥīm*

"**Is He who creates like one who does not create? Will you still not take heed?** · **If you set out to count God's favour you could not count it.** God is truly most forgiving, most merciful."

### The enumeration is cut off here

**A list that has run for thirteen verses is stopped by two sentences: a question and a ruling.**

**The question ties the whole list to a single comparison.** *A-fa-man **yakhluqu** ka-man **lā yakhluq*** — and the verb is **imperfect**: "the one who is creating" set against "the one who does not create".

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence uses no name. It says neither "God" nor gives the other side a name. **There is only a verb and its negative.** The comparison is thus built not on identities but on **the work done.**

**And the word *man* must be recorded: in Arabic *man* is used of beings possessed of reason.** The sentence therefore puts its question **counting the other side as persons** — on the most favourable terms available to them. **Even so the outcome does not change.**

### وَإِن تَعُدُّوا۟ نِعْمَةَ ٱللَّهِ لَا تُحْصُوهَا

**This is one of the most striking sentences in the sūra, and it comes directly after a full enumeration.**

**The difference between the two verbs carries the whole weight of the sentence, and the lexicographers dwell on it:**

| Verb | Root | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| **تَعُدُّوا۟** | ع-د-د | **To count** — to name one by one, to make an inventory |
| **تُحْصُوا۟** | ح-ص-ي | **To reckon up (*iḥṣāʾ*)** — to grasp the number exactly, to exhaust it, to enter it on record |

**The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root ح-ص-ي thus: *ḥaṣā* — a pebble.** In the old manner of counting one counted with pebbles; **from this comes the sense "a complete count, an exhaustive inventory".** **The word occurred at `072-cin.md`, 72/28 (*wa aḥṣā kulla shayʾin ʿadadā*) and at `019-meryem.md`, 19/94 (*laqad aḥṣāhum wa ʿaddahum ʿaddā* — the two verbs side by side).**

**The difference must be recorded, and the meaning of the verse rests on it:** in saying "you cannot count them" the verse does not say that counting **cannot be begun.** **It says: if you set about counting (*in taʿuddū*), you cannot finish (*lā tuḥṣūhā*).**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that two distinct verbs were chosen:** the verb of the conditional clause is *taʿuddū*, the verb of the answering clause *tuḥṣū*. **Had the verse forbidden counting altogether it could have used both verbs from the same root.** That it keeps the distinction shows **that it does not annul the act of counting itself.**

**And this has a direct consequence for the sūra:** the sūra has in fact been counting for thirteen verses. **The verse therefore halts at a point the very work it has been doing, but does not cancel it.**

### نِعْمَة — its coming in the singular

**The word is singular: *niʿmata'llāh*, "God's favour" — not the plural *niʿam*.**

**The lexicographers explain it as a generic noun: it conveys not number but kind.** **And I give this as an observation:** to say of a singular word that it cannot be counted raises the tension of the sentence — **not even a single thing can be counted.**

**And the sūra will use this word insistently:** the construction *niʿmata'llāh* occurs five times in the sūra (18, 53, 71, 72, 83), and once more in the form *niʿmatahū* (81). **This is a countable fact within the sūra and will be tabulated at the end.**

### إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَغَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ — the choice of closing

**And the closing of the verse is striking.**

**The expected closing would have been a sentence about thanks, or a rebuke. The verse names forgiveness.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two sentences stand back to back:** the return for a favour that cannot be counted cannot itself be rendered in full. **Instead of saying that the return cannot be made, the verse says that the failure to make it is forgiven.** **The sentence closes, within the same verse, the account it opened.**

**I do not rest this reading on a transmitted report; my ground is the order of the two sentences.**

---

## 16/19-21 — وَٱللَّهُ يَعْلَمُ مَا تُسِرُّونَ وَمَا تُعْلِنُونَ · وَٱلَّذِينَ يَدْعُونَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ لَا يَخْلُقُونَ شَيْـًٔا وَهُمْ يُخْلَقُونَ · أَمْوَٰتٌ غَيْرُ أَحْيَآءٍ وَمَا يَشْعُرُونَ أَيَّانَ يُبْعَثُونَ

*Wa'llāhu yaʿlamu mā tusirrūna wa mā tuʿlinūn · Wa'lladhīna yadʿūna min dūni'llāhi lā yakhluqūna shayʾan wa hum yukhlaqūn · Amwātun ghayru aḥyāʾ, wa mā yashʿurūna ayyāna yubʿathūn*

"**And God knows what you conceal and what you make known.** · **And those they call upon besides God create nothing; they themselves are created.** · **They are dead, not living; nor do they know when they will be raised.**"

### The question of the seventeenth verse is answered

**The seventeenth verse had asked: *a-fa-man yakhluqu ka-man lā yakhluq.* The twentieth gives the answer, and in giving it uses the same root.**

| Verse | Expression | Form |
|---|---|---|
| **17** | *A-fa-man **yakhluqu** ka-man **lā yakhluq*** | **A question** — active verb, two sides |
| **20** | *Lā **yakhluqūna** shayʾan wa hum **yukhlaqūn*** | **The answer** — active and **passive** side by side |

**The construction of the answer must be recorded:** the verse does not say only "they cannot create". **It turns the same root into the passive and adds: *wa hum yukhlaqūn* — "they themselves are created."**

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** the position claimed and the position in fact are set against each other **by the two voices of a single root.** **The other side is found in exactly the reverse form of its own claim.**

### أَمْوَٰتٌ غَيْرُ أَحْيَآءٍ — a qualification that looks superfluous

**To say *amwāt* (dead) would have sufficed. The verse adds *ghayru aḥyāʾ* (not living).**

**The lexicographers count additions of this kind as *taʾkīd* (emphasis).** **But one further explanation belonging here must be recorded, and I give it as my own reading:** in Arabic *mawt* is used also of what has never lived — **an inanimate object is *mayyit* too.** The qualification *ghayru aḥyāʾ* marks off this second sense: **what is at issue is not a thing that has died but a thing that has never been alive.**

**And at `035-fatir.md`, 35/13-14, the same subject was closed off in three stages** (they do not hear → were they to hear they could not answer → in the end they reject). **I rely on that treatment and record the difference here:** Fāṭir was treating the impossibility of **communication**; al-Naḥl, the character of **existence.**

**وَمَا يَشْعُرُونَ أَيَّانَ يُبْعَثُونَ** — and the pronoun must be recorded. *Yubʿathūn* — when "**they will be raised**". **The sentence thus says that the objects called upon are themselves inside a process of being raised.**

**The lexicographers give the reference of the pronoun in two ways:** either those called upon, or those who call. **Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

---

## 16/22-23 — إِلَٰهُكُمْ إِلَٰهٌ وَٰحِدٌ فَٱلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ قُلُوبُهُم مُّنكِرَةٌ وَهُم مُّسْتَكْبِرُونَ · لَا جَرَمَ أَنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَعْلَمُ مَا يُسِرُّونَ وَمَا يُعْلِنُونَ إِنَّهُۥ لَا يُحِبُّ ٱلْمُسْتَكْبِرِينَ

*Ilāhukum ilāhun wāḥid, fa'lladhīna lā yuʾminūna bi'l-ākhirati qulūbuhum munkiratun wa hum mustakbirūn · Lā jarama anna'llāha yaʿlamu mā yusirrūna wa mā yuʿlinūn, innahū lā yuḥibbu'l-mustakbirīn*

"**Your god is one god.** As for those who do not believe in the hereafter, **their hearts are in denial and they make themselves great.** · **Beyond doubt God knows what they conceal and what they make known. He does not love those who make themselves great.**"

### The diagnosis: the problem is not knowledge

**The verse says two things about those who deny the hereafter, and the two lie on different planes:**

| Expression | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| *Qulūbuhum **munkira*** | **Within** | A heart that does not accept |
| *Wa hum **mustakbirūn*** | **Without** | The conduct of self-magnifying |

**And the order of the two clauses must be recorded:** first the heart, then the conduct. **The verse ranks conduct as an outcome of the heart.**

**I give this as an observation, and its ground is the order of the clauses:** the verse does not name denial as a want of knowledge. **Both diagnoses have to do not with knowledge but with disposition.**

### لَا جَرَمَ — the form itself

***Lā jarama* occurs in five places in the Qurʾān, and the lexicographers differ over the origin of the form.**

| View | How *lā jarama* is built |
|---|---|
| 1 | The negative *lā* + *jarama* (to cut) — "certain, unavoidable" |
| 2 | A frozen formula of oath or emphasis — "beyond doubt, assuredly" |
| 3 | A statement of necessity, like *lā budda* |

**In point of meaning all three come out at the same place: certainty.** **I do not impose a preference.**

**And this construction will occur twice in the sūra: here and in the sixty-second verse** (*lā jarama anna lahumu'l-nār*). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

### The repetition of the nineteenth verse

**It must be recorded: the sentence *yaʿlamu mā tusirrūna wa mā tuʿlinūn* occurred in the nineteenth verse. In the twenty-third it returns in almost the same words — the only difference being the change of person:**

| Verse | Expression | Person |
|---|---|---|
| **19** | *Mā **tu**sirrūna wa mā **tu**ʿlinūn* | **Second person** — you |
| **23** | *Mā **yu**sirrūna wa mā **yu**ʿlinūn* | **Third person** — they |

**This is a repetition that can be verified from the text. And I record this as my own reading:** the same sentence is said first to the general addressee, then to a particular group. **The ruling is laid down first for everyone, then applied to those who hold a certain stance.**

---

## 16/24-25 — وَإِذَا قِيلَ لَهُم مَّاذَآ أَنزَلَ رَبُّكُمْ قَالُوٓا۟ أَسَٰطِيرُ ٱلْأَوَّلِينَ · لِيَحْمِلُوٓا۟ أَوْزَارَهُمْ كَامِلَةً يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ وَمِنْ أَوْزَارِ ٱلَّذِينَ يُضِلُّونَهُم بِغَيْرِ عِلْمٍ أَلَا سَآءَ مَا يَزِرُونَ

*Wa idhā qīla lahum mādhā anzala rabbukum qālū asāṭīru'l-awwalīn · Li-yaḥmilū awzārahum kāmilatan yawma'l-qiyāmati wa min awzāri'lladhīna yuḍillūnahum bi-ghayri ʿilm, alā sāʾa mā yazirūn*

"**And when it is said to them, "What has your Lord sent down?", they say: "Tales of the ancients."** · **So that on the Day of Resurrection they may carry their own burdens in full, and a part of the burdens of those they lead astray without knowledge. Beware — evil is what they take upon themselves!**"

### The form of the question and of the answer

**The question is a question of information: *mādhā anzala rabbukum* — "What has your Lord sent down?"**

**The answer, however, does not answer the question.** *Asāṭīru'l-awwalīn* — "tales of the ancients." **What is given is not an account of what was sent down but a verdict on where it came from.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence does not even say "nothing was sent down". **It accepts the question and changes its object.** **And in the thirtieth verse the same question will be put once more, this time to another group — and the answer will be wholly different.** The sūra gives the same question to two different mouths. **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**أَسَٰطِير** — the plural of *usṭūra*, root **س-ط-ر**: **a line, writing, to set out in rows.** *Misṭar* — the instrument used for ruling lines. **The word means "a thing written, set out in lines"; from this comes the sense "old story, tale".**

**There is an irony in the root of the word, and I record this as an observation about the language:** the objection names what has been sent down **"a thing written".** **Yet the subject of the objection is a text that has been sent down.** Both sides use the same family of words; where they part is over the source of the text.

### لِيَحْمِلُوٓا۟ أَوْزَارَهُمْ كَامِلَةً — and the principle that no burden is carried for another

**There is a matter here that must be handled with care, because at first sight the verse appears to contradict a principle recorded many times over in this commentary.**

**The principle is this, and it was treated at many places:** *wa lā taziru wāziratun wizra ukhrā* — **"no bearer of burdens bears the burden of another."** **It was treated in detail at `035-fatir.md`, 35/18, and at `017-isra.md`, 17/15.**

**This verse, however, says that those who lead others astray will carry a part of the burdens of those they lead astray. How is the contradiction resolved?**

**The answer lies in the verse's own words, and two of them are decisive:**

| Word | What it does |
|---|---|
| ***Awzārahum* + كَامِلَةً** | **Their own burdens — in full.** Their own share is not diminished at all |
| *Wa **min** awzāri'lladhīna yuḍillūnahum* | ***Min*** — **"a part of".** Not the whole |

**And the burden of those led astray is not lightened:** the verse does not say that their burden is taken away. **What it says is that the one who leads astray is loaded in addition, as a consequence of his own act.**

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is these two words (*kāmilatan* and *min*):** what is carried is not another's burden — **it is the return for the act of leading astray itself.** **The principle is thus not broken; leading astray is counted as an act in its own right.**

**And the qualification *bi-ghayri ʿilm* is worth recording:** the leading astray is described as done **without knowledge.** **The same construction occurred at `031-lokman.md`, 31/6 (*li-yuḍilla ʿan sabīli'llāhi bi-ghayri ʿilm*);** I rely on that treatment.

---

## 16/26-27 — قَدْ مَكَرَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِمْ فَأَتَى ٱللَّهُ بُنْيَٰنَهُم مِّنَ ٱلْقَوَاعِدِ فَخَرَّ عَلَيْهِمُ ٱلسَّقْفُ مِن فَوْقِهِمْ وَأَتَىٰهُمُ ٱلْعَذَابُ مِنْ حَيْثُ لَا يَشْعُرُونَ · ثُمَّ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ يُخْزِيهِمْ وَيَقُولُ أَيْنَ شُرَكَآءِىَ ٱلَّذِينَ كُنتُمْ تُشَٰٓقُّونَ فِيهِمْ

*Qad makara'lladhīna min qablihim fa-ata'llāhu bunyānahum mina'l-qawāʿidi fa-kharra ʿalayhimu'l-saqfu min fawqihim wa atāhumu'l-ʿadhābu min ḥaythu lā yashʿurūn · Thumma yawma'l-qiyāmati yukhzīhim wa yaqūlu ayna shurakāʾiya'lladhīna kuntum tushāqqūna fīhim*

"**Those before them plotted too; God came at their building from the foundations, and the roof fell in upon them from above, and the punishment came at them from where they did not perceive it.** · **Then on the Day of Resurrection He will disgrace them and say: 'Where are My associates, over whom you took the opposing side?'**"

### Three verbs, three directions

**The scene is divided into three movements, and the three come from different directions:**

| Verb | Direction | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| *Fa-atā … mina'l-**qawāʿid*** | **From below** | From the foundations |
| *Fa-**kharra** ʿalayhimu'l-saqfu min **fawqihim*** | **From above** | The roof upon their heads |
| *Wa atāhumu'l-ʿadhābu **min ḥaythu lā yashʿurūn*** | **From where it was not reckoned** | Left unnamed |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** unlike the first two, the third direction is **not a place.** The verse leaves one direction unclosed — and that direction is the one the plot could not take into account.

**This picture was named at `047-muhammed.md`, 47/10;** I rely on that treatment.

**بُنْيَٰن and قَوَاعِد** — *bunyān*, root **ب-ن-ي**: a building. *Qawāʿid*, the plural of *qāʿida*, root **ق-ع-د**: **that which sits, that which is set down; a foundation.** **That the word comes from the verb "to sit" is worth recording: the foundation is where the building sits upon the ground.**

**مَكَرَ** — root **م-ك-ر**: the core the lexicographers give is **to work at something in secret and by indirect means.** **The root was treated in detail at `027-neml.md`, 27/50-51 (*wa makarū makran wa makarnā makran*);** I rely on that treatment.

### تُشَٰٓقُّونَ فِيهِمْ

**The verb is Form III from the root ش-ق-ق: *mushāqqa* — to stand on opposite sides of one another, to set up an opposing front.**

**The picture in the root must be recorded, and it was analysed in `084-insikak.md`:** *shiqq* — one of the two pieces into which a thing is split. **So *mushāqqa* means "to stand in the other half".**

**And it must be recorded that this is the root's second occurrence in the sūra:** *bi-shiqqi'l-anfus* (the splitting of the selves) occurred in the seventh verse. **Both occurrences carry the picture of splitting; the one in the body, the other in the ranks.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.

---

## 16/28-29 — ٱلَّذِينَ تَتَوَفَّىٰهُمُ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ ظَالِمِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ فَأَلْقَوُا۟ ٱلسَّلَمَ مَا كُنَّا نَعْمَلُ مِن سُوٓءٍۭ بَلَىٰٓ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلِيمٌۢ بِمَا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ · فَٱدْخُلُوٓا۟ أَبْوَٰبَ جَهَنَّمَ خَٰلِدِينَ فِيهَا فَلَبِئْسَ مَثْوَى ٱلْمُتَكَبِّرِينَ

*Alladhīna tatawaffāhumu'l-malāʾikatu ẓālimī anfusihim, fa-alqawu'l-salama mā kunnā naʿmalu min sūʾ, balā inna'llāha ʿalīmun bimā kuntum taʿmalūn · Fa'dkhulū abwāba jahannama khālidīna fīhā, fa-la-biʾsa mathwa'l-mutakabbirīn*

"**Those whom the angels take while they are wronging themselves surrender: 'We were doing no evil.' No indeed! God knows what you were doing.** · **So enter the gates of Hell, to remain in them. Evil is the abode of those who make themselves great!**"

### فَأَلْقَوُا۟ ٱلسَّلَمَ

**To *cast down the salam* — to lay surrender out in the open, to give oneself up.** **Root س-ل-م: to be sound, to be without flaw, to surrender.**

**And the choice of verb is worth recording: *alqaw* — "they cast, they threw down".** The same verb was used of the mountains in the fifteenth verse (*wa **alqā** fi'l-arḍi rawāsiya*). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra; but the agent changes between the two uses: there it is God who casts, here it is man who throws down.**

**مَا كُنَّا نَعْمَلُ مِن سُوٓءٍ** — and even after the surrender a defence follows. **The order of the sentence must be recorded:** first the surrender, then the denial. **The two movements do not normally stand together.**

**I give this as an observation:** the verse shows that surrender is not acceptance — **even when the resistance is over, the defence continues.**

**And the answer is a single word: *balā* — "no, it is not so".** In Arabic *balā* is a particle used specifically **to refute a negative sentence**; *naʿam* (yes) would have confirmed the negation. **The claim is refuted in one syllable.**

**مَثْوَى** — root **ث-و-ي**: to stay in a place, to lodge. *Mathwā* — the place where one stays. **The word occurred at `047-muhammed.md`, 47/12 (*wa'l-nāru mathwan lahum*).**

---

## 16/30-32 — وَقِيلَ لِلَّذِينَ ٱتَّقَوْا۟ مَاذَآ أَنزَلَ رَبُّكُمْ قَالُوا۟ خَيْرًا لِّلَّذِينَ أَحْسَنُوا۟ فِى هَٰذِهِ ٱلدُّنْيَا حَسَنَةٌ وَلَدَارُ ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ خَيْرٌ وَلَنِعْمَ دَارُ ٱلْمُتَّقِينَ · جَنَّٰتُ عَدْنٍ يَدْخُلُونَهَا تَجْرِى مِن تَحْتِهَا ٱلْأَنْهَٰرُ لَهُمْ فِيهَا مَا يَشَآءُونَ · ٱلَّذِينَ تَتَوَفَّىٰهُمُ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ طَيِّبِينَ يَقُولُونَ سَلَٰمٌ عَلَيْكُمُ ٱدْخُلُوا۟ ٱلْجَنَّةَ بِمَا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ

*Wa qīla lilladhīna'ttaqaw mādhā anzala rabbukum qālū khayrā, lilladhīna aḥsanū fī hādhihi'l-dunyā ḥasana, wa la-dāru'l-ākhirati khayr, wa la-niʿma dāru'l-muttaqīn · Jannātu ʿadnin yadkhulūnahā tajrī min taḥtiha'l-anhāru lahum fīhā mā yashāʾūn · Alladhīna tatawaffāhumu'l-malāʾikatu ṭayyibīna yaqūlūna salāmun ʿalaykumu'dkhulu'l-jannata bimā kuntum taʿmalūn*

"**And when it is said to those who guard themselves, "What has your Lord sent down?", they say: "Good."** For those who do good in this world there is good; and the abode of the hereafter is better. How excellent is the abode of those who guard themselves! · **Gardens of ʿAdn which they enter, with rivers running beneath them; there they have whatever they wish.** · **Those whom the angels take while they are pure, saying: "Peace be upon you! Enter the Garden for what you were doing."**"

### The same question, two different mouths

**This is one of the plainest symmetries in the sūra, and it must be set out in a table:**

| | **Verse 24** | **Verse 30** |
|---|---|---|
| The question | *Mādhā anzala rabbukum* | ***The same question, word for word*** |
| To whom it is put | *Lahum* — unnamed | *Lilladhīna'ttaqaw* — **to those who guard themselves** |
| The answer | *Asāṭīru'l-awwalīn* — **tales of the ancients** | ***Khayrā*** — **good** |
| The kind of answer | **A verdict on the source** | **A verdict on the content** |
| The length of the answer | Two words | **One word** |

**This is a repetition that can be verified from the text. And I record this as my own reading:** both answers are short, but **they answer different things.** The first answers the question "who sent it down" — though that was not the question asked. **The second answers the question that was asked, "what has He sent down": *khayrā* — good.**

**The difference is thus given, before any difference of belief, as a difference in *hearing the question*.**

### Two death scenes

**And the sūra builds one further symmetry. The twenty-eighth and thirty-second verses begin with the same sentence:**

| | **Verse 28** | **Verse 32** |
|---|---|---|
| The opening | *Alladhīna **tatawaffāhumu'l-malāʾika*** | ***The same*** |
| The note of state | ***Ẓālimī anfusihim*** — wronging themselves | ***Ṭayyibīn*** — pure |
| Who speaks first | **The dying** — *mā kunnā naʿmalu min sūʾ* | **The angels** — *salāmun ʿalaykum* |
| The command | *Fa'dkhulū abwāba **jahannam*** | *Udkhulu'l-**janna*** |
| The ground given | (none is given) | ***Bimā kuntum taʿmalūn*** |

**A contrast in five lines, and all five can be verified from the text.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the third line:** in the first scene it is **the dying** who speak first — and what they say is a defence. **In the second it is the angel who speaks first — and what he says is a greeting of peace.** Who speaks first determines the whole of the scene.

**طَيِّبِين** — root **ط-ي-ب**: pleasant, clean, agreeable to the heart. **The root was treated at `061-saff.md`, 61/12, where it was recorded that the word is used of food, of speech and of life;** I rely on that treatment. **And the word will occur once more in the sūra, in the seventy-second verse, of provisions** (*wa razaqakum mina'l-ṭayyibāt*).

**عَدْن** — root **ع-د-ن**: to stay on in a place, to settle. *Maʿdan* — the place where a substance lies settled; **from the same root.** **So *jannātu ʿadn* means "the gardens where one stays".**

---

## 16/33-34 — هَلْ يَنظُرُونَ إِلَّآ أَن تَأْتِيَهُمُ ٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ أَوْ يَأْتِىَ أَمْرُ رَبِّكَ كَذَٰلِكَ فَعَلَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِمْ وَمَا ظَلَمَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ وَلَٰكِن كَانُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُمْ يَظْلِمُونَ · فَأَصَابَهُمْ سَيِّـَٔاتُ مَا عَمِلُوا۟ وَحَاقَ بِهِم مَّا كَانُوا۟ بِهِۦ يَسْتَهْزِءُونَ

*Hal yanẓurūna illā an taʾtiyahumu'l-malāʾikatu aw yaʾtiya amru rabbik, ka-dhālika faʿala'lladhīna min qablihim, wa mā ẓalamahumu'llāhu wa lākin kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn · Fa-aṣābahum sayyiʾātu mā ʿamilū wa ḥāqa bihim mā kānū bihī yastahziʾūn*

"**Do they look for anything but that the angels should come to them, or that the command of your Lord should come?** Those before them did the same. **God did not wrong them; it was themselves they were wronging.** · **The evils of what they had done struck them, and the very thing they mocked closed in around them.**"
