### A return to the first verse

**It must be recorded: the construction *amru rabbik* is the same as the *amru'llāh* of the sūra's first verse.**

**The first verse had said: *atā amru'llāhi fa-lā tastaʿjilūh* — "it has come; do not seek to hasten it."**
**The thirty-third says: *aw yaʾtiya amru rabbik* — "or that the command of your Lord should come."**

| Verse | Verb | Mood | Who is speaking |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Atā* | **Perfect** | **The text** — a declaration of certainty |
| **33** | *Yaʾtiya* | **Imperfect** (subjunctive) | **The text** — describing what they wait for |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and I record this as my own reading:** what the first verse forbade them to hasten is named in the thirty-third as **the one thing they wait for.** **The sūra thus explains, thirty-two verses later, why that prohibition was laid down:** if no other sign is accepted, that is all that remains.

**هَلْ يَنظُرُونَ إِلَّآ** — *naẓar* here has the sense of waiting for. **And the restriction built with *illā* must be recorded:** the verse does not say "this is what they wait for"; it says **"they wait for nothing but this."**

### حَاقَ — root: ح-ي-ق

**The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root thus: a thing turning back and coming down upon its owner, closing around him.** *Ḥāqa bihī* — it befell him, it wrapped itself about him. **In the Qurʾān the word occurs almost everywhere together with mockery or with plotting.**

**And the construction of the sentence is worth recording:** *ḥāqa bihim mā kānū bihī **yastahziʾūn*** — what closes in on them is **the very thing they mocked.** **The punishment is not fetched from outside; the thing mocked comes back.**

**The same sentence is repeated at several places in the Qurʾān and was treated in this commentary: the root was analysed at `040-gafir.md`, 40/45, and the same point of word order was recorded at `039-zumer.md`, 39/48, and at `021-enbiya.md`, 21/41.** **I rely on those treatments.**

---

## 16/35 — وَقَالَ ٱلَّذِينَ أَشْرَكُوا۟ لَوْ شَآءَ ٱللَّهُ مَا عَبَدْنَا مِن دُونِهِۦ مِن شَىْءٍ نَّحْنُ وَلَآ ءَابَآؤُنَا وَلَا حَرَّمْنَا مِن دُونِهِۦ مِن شَىْءٍ كَذَٰلِكَ فَعَلَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِمْ فَهَلْ عَلَى ٱلرُّسُلِ إِلَّا ٱلْبَلَٰغُ ٱلْمُبِينُ

*Wa qāla'lladhīna ashrakū law shāʾa'llāhu mā ʿabadnā min dūnihī min shayʾin naḥnu wa lā ābāʾunā wa lā ḥarramnā min dūnihī min shayʾ, ka-dhālika faʿala'lladhīna min qablihim, fa-hal ʿala'l-rusuli illa'l-balāghu'l-mubīn*

"**Those who associate said: 'Had God willed, neither we nor our fathers would have served anything besides Him, nor would we have forbidden anything without Him.'** Those before them did the same. **Is anything laid upon the messengers but plain delivery?**"

### The sentence of the ninth verse comes back

**In the ninth verse the text had said: *wa law shāʾa la-hadākum ajmaʿīn* — "had He willed He would have guided you all."**

**In the thirty-fifth the same sentence, in the same form, comes from the mouth of the addressee: *law shāʾa'llāhu mā ʿabadnā min dūnihī min shayʾ.***

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and it was pointed to at the ninth verse. The comparison can now be made:**

| | **Verse 9** | **Verse 35** |
|---|---|---|
| Who says it | **The text** | **Those who associate** |
| The form | *Law shāʾa la-hadākum* | *Law shāʾa'llāhu mā ʿabadnā* |
| Its direction | **Positive** — guidance could have been given | **Negative** — we would not have served |
| Its work | **A piece of knowledge** | **A defence** |
| What comes after it | The enumeration continues | ***Fa-hal ʿala'l-rusuli illa'l-balāgh*** |

**And the answer the verse gives must be recorded, because it does not reject the claim.**

**The answer is this: *fa-hal ʿala'l-rusuli illa'l-balāghu'l-mubīn* — "is anything laid upon the messengers but plain delivery?"**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the answer itself:** the verse does not say that the sentence "had God willed" is untrue — **indeed it said something like it itself in the ninth verse.** What it says is **that the sentence cannot be used as a means of unloading responsibility.**

**What is rejected, then, is not the proposition but the work the proposition is put to.**

**The same defence occurs word for word at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/20** (*law shāʾa'l-raḥmānu mā ʿabadnāhum*) and was treated there: **it was recorded that the claim is framed without knowledge (*in hum illā yakhruṣūn*).** **I rely on that treatment.**

### ٱلْبَلَٰغُ ٱلْمُبِينُ — the limit of responsibility

**This construction was treated at many places in this commentary:** **at `046-ahkaf.md`, 46/35 (*balāgh*), at `035-fatir.md`, 35/19-23, at `039-zumer.md`, 39/41 (*wa mā anta ʿalayhim bi-wakīl*), at `030-rum.md`, 30/52-53.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here is that the construction has a second occurrence in the sūra:** it will come once more in the eighty-second verse (*fa-innamā ʿalayka'l-balāghu'l-mubīn*). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and both stand immediately after an objection.**

---

## 16/36 — وَلَقَدْ بَعَثْنَا فِى كُلِّ أُمَّةٍ رَّسُولًا أَنِ ٱعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَٱجْتَنِبُوا۟ ٱلطَّٰغُوتَ فَمِنْهُم مَّنْ هَدَى ٱللَّهُ وَمِنْهُم مَّنْ حَقَّتْ عَلَيْهِ ٱلضَّلَٰلَةُ فَسِيرُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ فَٱنظُرُوا۟ كَيْفَ كَانَ عَٰقِبَةُ ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ

*Wa laqad baʿathnā fī kulli ummatin rasūlan ani'budu'llāha wa'jtanibu'l-ṭāghūt, fa-minhum man hada'llāhu wa minhum man ḥaqqat ʿalayhi'l-ḍalāla, fa-sīrū fi'l-arḍi fa'nẓurū kayfa kāna ʿāqibatu'l-mukadhdhibīn*

"**We have surely raised up in every community a messenger: 'Serve God, and keep clear of the ṭāghūt.'** Among them were some whom God guided, and some upon whom error was fixed. **So travel through the earth and see what became of those who cried lies.**"

### The messenger's message is two sentences

**And the verb forms of the two differ — this must be recorded:**

| Command | Form | Its direction |
|---|---|---|
| ***Uʿbudu'llāh*** | **Imperative** — do | **Positive** |
| ***Ijtanibu'l-ṭāghūt*** | **Imperative** — Form VIII, *ijtināb* | **Keep clear of** |

**اجْتَنِبُوا۟** — root **ج-ن-ب**: **side, flank.** *Janb* — the flank; *jānib* — the side. **In Form VIII (*ijtanaba*) it means "not to take a thing to one's side, to draw away from it".**

**The picture in the word is worth recording, and I give this as an observation about the language:** the verse does not say "tear it down" or "reject it". **It says "keep it away from your side."** The command is thus not a command to destroy but **a command about distance.**

**طَٰغُوت** — root **ط-غ-ي**: **to overstep the bound, to overflow.** *Ṭughyān* — excess; *ṭāghī* — one who oversteps. ***Ṭāghūt* is intensive in form, and the lexicographers record that the word is used for both singular and plural.** **The root was treated at `069-hakka.md`, 69/5 (*fa-uhlikū bi'l-ṭāghiya*) and at `079-naziat.md`, 79/17 (*innahū ṭaghā*).**

### The work this verse does in the sūra

**It must be recorded: the verse gives a second answer to the defence of the thirty-fifth verse, and this answer is historical.**

**The defence was: "had God willed we would not have served them." The answer: to every community a messenger was sent.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that the two verses stand back to back:** the verse does not debate the existence of the will as a theory. **It announces as a fact that the knowledge was delivered.** **And the principle recorded at `017-isra.md`, 17/15, is stated here from the other side:** there it was *wa mā kunnā muʿadhdhibīna ḥattā nabʿatha rasūlā* (We do not punish until We have raised up a messenger); **here it is announced that the messenger has been sent.** **The same principle was recorded at `028-kasas.md`, 28/59.**

**فَسِيرُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ** — **this proof was treated at many places in this commentary:** **`030-rum.md` 30/9 and 30/42, `035-fatir.md` 35/44, `040-gafir.md` 40/21 and 40/82, `047-muhammed.md` 47/10.** **I do not repeat it here.** **And what was recorded there holds here too: the proof is fetched not from a distant history but from the ruins that lie along the road.**

---

## 16/37-39 — إِن تَحْرِصْ عَلَىٰ هُدَىٰهُمْ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يَهْدِى مَن يُضِلُّ وَمَا لَهُم مِّن نَّٰصِرِينَ · وَأَقْسَمُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ جَهْدَ أَيْمَٰنِهِمْ لَا يَبْعَثُ ٱللَّهُ مَن يَمُوتُ بَلَىٰ وَعْدًا عَلَيْهِ حَقًّا وَلَٰكِنَّ أَكْثَرَ ٱلنَّاسِ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ · لِيُبَيِّنَ لَهُمُ ٱلَّذِى يَخْتَلِفُونَ فِيهِ وَلِيَعْلَمَ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوٓا۟ أَنَّهُمْ كَانُوا۟ كَٰذِبِينَ

*In taḥriṣ ʿalā hudāhum fa-inna'llāha lā yahdī man yuḍillu wa mā lahum min nāṣirīn · Wa aqsamū bi'llāhi jahda aymānihim lā yabʿathu'llāhu man yamūt, balā waʿdan ʿalayhi ḥaqqan wa lākinna akthara'l-nāsi lā yaʿlamūn · Li-yubayyina lahumu'lladhī yakhtalifūna fīhi wa li-yaʿlama'lladhīna kafarū annahum kānū kādhibīn*

"**However eager you are for their guidance, God does not guide the one He lets go astray; and they have no helpers.** · **They swore by God with the utmost of their oaths: 'God will not raise up one who dies.' No indeed! It is a promise binding upon Him, in truth — but most people do not know.** · **That He may make clear to them that over which they differ, and that those who disbelieved may know that they were liars.**"

### تَحْرِصْ — root: ح-ر-ص

**The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root thus: to be excessively eager to obtain a thing; and *ḥarasa'l-thawb* — to rub a cloth too hard in the washing and so wear it out.**

**This second picture is worth recording, and I give it as an observation about the language:** in the root there is the idea that **an excess of wanting does damage.** **And this is exactly what the verse says:** eagerness does not change the outcome. **The same line was treated at `035-fatir.md`, 35/8 (*fa-lā tadhhab nafsuka ʿalayhim ḥasarāt*);** I rely on that treatment.

### وَأَقْسَمُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ جَهْدَ أَيْمَٰنِهِمْ

***Jahda aymānihim* — "with the utmost force of their oaths".** **Root ج-ه-د: to spend strength, to go to the end of one's capacity. The root was treated at `029-ankebut.md`, 29/6 and 29/69;** I rely on that treatment.

**The construction occurs in several places in the Qurʾān, and the same form was present at `035-fatir.md`, 35/42.**

**And the work it does here must be recorded:** what is sworn to is a **denial** — *lā yabʿathu'llāhu man yamūt.* **A form of affirmation of the highest degree is thus used to prop up a negation.**

**I give this as an observation:** the verse shows that the **direction** of certainty can change. The same word (*balā*) refuted a denial in the twenty-eighth verse as well; **here it refutes an oath.**

### لِيُبَيِّنَ لَهُمُ ٱلَّذِى يَخْتَلِفُونَ فِيهِ — the ground of the raising

**The thirty-ninth verse says why the raising takes place, and the ground it gives is worth recording:**

| Purpose | Expression | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Li-yubayyina lahumu'lladhī **yakhtalifūna** fīh* | **To those who differ** — a making clear |
| 2 | *Wa li-yaʿlama'lladhīna kafarū **annahum kānū kādhibīn*** | **To those who disbelieved** — knowledge of their own condition |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the word of the first purpose:** the verse names the raising first of all not as **punishment** but as ***tabyīn*** — a making clear. **The ending of the disagreement is counted as the first work of the scene.**

**The root ب-ي-ن is one of the sūra's backbones and we shall return to it in the verses ahead:** *li-tubayyina li'l-nās* in the forty-fourth verse, *tibyānan li-kulli shayʾ* in the eighty-ninth. **All three occurrences can be verified from the text.**

---

## 16/40 — إِنَّمَا قَوْلُنَا لِشَىْءٍ إِذَآ أَرَدْنَٰهُ أَن نَّقُولَ لَهُۥ كُن فَيَكُونُ

*Innamā qawlunā li-shayʾin idhā aradnāhu an naqūla lahū kun fa-yakūn*

"**When We will a thing, Our word to it is only that We say to it, 'Be', and it is.**"

### The closing of the block

**The verse is the last answer to the denial of the thirty-eighth verse (*lā yabʿathu'llāhu man yamūt*). And the answer is not a proof but a description.**

**The construction of the sentence must be recorded, and the lexicographers dwell on it:**

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| ***Innamā*** | **Restriction (*ḥaṣr*)** — "only, no more than" |
| *Qawlunā li-shayʾin **idhā aradnāh*** | The condition: the will **first** |
| *An naqūla lahū **kun*** | **A single word** |
| *Fa-**yakūn*** | **The outcome** — from the same root |

**And what draws the attention is that there is no intermediary at all between *kun* and *yakūn*: *fa* — immediately after.**

**The form occurs in eight places in the Qurʾān and was treated in detail in this commentary at `036-yasin.md`, 36/82 (*innamā amruhū idhā arāda shayʾan an yaqūla lahū kun fa-yakūn*).** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference that belongs here must be recorded, and the wording of the two verses parts at one point:**

| | **Yā Sīn 36/82** | **al-Naḥl 16/40** |
|---|---|---|
| The key word | *Innamā **amruhū*** — **His command** | *Innamā **qawlunā*** — **Our word** |
| Person | **Third** — *amruhū*, *arāda* | **First person plural** — *qawlunā*, *aradnā* |
| Its place in the sūra | **Among the last verses** — a closing | **The end of a block** — a transition |

**And one further note:** *amru'llāh* occurred in the first verse. **In the fortieth the same idea is given with the word *qawl*.** This is an overlap verifiable within the sūra.

**A note on the variant readings:** it is transmitted that in some readings *fa-yakūn* is read *fa-yakūna* (subjunctive); on that reading the clause is coordinated with *an naqūla*. **It is recorded that there is no essential change in meaning.**

---

## 16/41-42 — وَٱلَّذِينَ هَاجَرُوا۟ فِى ٱللَّهِ مِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا ظُلِمُوا۟ لَنُبَوِّئَنَّهُمْ فِى ٱلدُّنْيَا حَسَنَةً وَلَأَجْرُ ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ أَكْبَرُ لَوْ كَانُوا۟ يَعْلَمُونَ · ٱلَّذِينَ صَبَرُوا۟ وَعَلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ يَتَوَكَّلُونَ

*Wa'lladhīna hājarū fi'llāhi min baʿdi mā ẓulimū la-nubawwiʾannahum fi'l-dunyā ḥasana, wa la-ajru'l-ākhirati akbar, law kānū yaʿlamūn · Alladhīna ṣabarū wa ʿalā rabbihim yatawakkalūn*

"**Those who emigrated for God's sake after they had been wronged — We shall surely settle them well in this world; and the reward of the hereafter is greater, if only they knew.** · **They are those who were patient and who rely upon their Lord.**"

### هَاجَرُوا۟ — root: ه-ج-ر

**The concrete sense of the root: to part from, to abandon, to cut a tie.** *Hajr* — abandonment; *hijra* — departure from one's homeland. **That it comes from Form III (*hājara*) is worth recording:** this form conveys reciprocity, and the lexicographers record here the sense that **while a man leaves a thing, the thing leaves him as well.**

**And the qualification is important: *min baʿdi mā ẓulimū* — "after they had been wronged".** **The departure is thus named not as a choice but as a consequence.**

**لَنُبَوِّئَنَّهُمْ** — root **ب-و-أ**: **to settle in a place, to give a position.** *Mubawwaʾ* — the place in which one is settled. **The *lām* at the head of the verb and the *nūn* at its end (the *nūn* of emphasis) must be recorded: the sentence is reinforced twice over.**

**And the same verb occurred in the same form at `029-ankebut.md`, 29/58** (*la-nubawwiʾannahum mina'l-jannati ghurafā*), **and there too it was followed by *alladhīna ṣabarū wa ʿalā rabbihim yatawakkalūn*.** **The two verses are almost identical in structure, and this is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

**The difference must be recorded:**

| | **al-ʿAnkabūt 29/58-59** | **al-Naḥl 16/41-42** |
|---|---|---|
| Where they are settled | ***Mina'l-jannati ghurafā*** — chambers in the Garden | ***Fi'l-dunyā ḥasana*** — **in this world** |
| The note about the hereafter | The Garden is already what is described | *Wa la-ajru'l-ākhirati **akbar*** — added separately |
| The shared closing sentence | *Alladhīna ṣabarū wa ʿalā rabbihim yatawakkalūn* | ***The same*** |

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the difference between the two verses:** al-Naḥl begins by giving the return **in this world** and adds the hereafter separately. **The cost of the emigration is thus not tied to a deferred return alone.**

---

## 16/43-44 — وَمَآ أَرْسَلْنَا مِن قَبْلِكَ إِلَّا رِجَالًا نُّوحِىٓ إِلَيْهِمْ فَسْـَٔلُوٓا۟ أَهْلَ ٱلذِّكْرِ إِن كُنتُمْ لَا تَعْلَمُونَ · بِٱلْبَيِّنَٰتِ وَٱلزُّبُرِ وَأَنزَلْنَآ إِلَيْكَ ٱلذِّكْرَ لِتُبَيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ مَا نُزِّلَ إِلَيْهِمْ وَلَعَلَّهُمْ يَتَفَكَّرُونَ

*Wa mā arsalnā min qablika illā rijālan nūḥī ilayhim fa'sʾalū ahla'l-dhikri in kuntum lā taʿlamūn · Bi'l-bayyināti wa'l-zubur, wa anzalnā ilayka'l-dhikra li-tubayyina li'l-nāsi mā nuzzila ilayhim wa laʿallahum yatafakkarūn*

"**And before you We sent none but men to whom We gave revelation. If you do not know, ask the people of the reminder.** · **With the clear proofs and the scriptures. And We have sent down to you the reminder, that you may make clear to people what has been sent down to them, and that they may reflect.**"

### The context — what the verse answers

**Torn from its context, this verse comes to say something altogether different. The context must therefore be set out first and openly.**

**The first half of the sentence is this: *wa mā arsalnā min qablika illā rijālan nūḥī ilayhim* — "and before you We sent none but human beings to whom We gave revelation."**

**There is, then, an objection in view, and the subject of the objection is this: that the messenger is a human being.** This objection is recorded at many places in the Qurʾān and was treated in this commentary: **at `021-enbiya.md`, 21/3 and 21/7-8** (*hal hādhā illā basharun mithlukum*; *wa mā jaʿalnāhum jasadan lā yaʾkulūna'l-ṭaʿām*), **at `046-ahkaf.md`, 46/9** (*mā kuntu bidʿan mina'l-rusul*), **at `035-fatir.md`, 35/4.**

**And the command *fa'sʾalū ahla'l-dhikr* is the second half of the answer given to that objection: "if you do not know that the earlier messengers too were human beings, ask those who do know."**

**Insisting on this context is obligatory, because torn from it the verse can be presented as a general and unlimited *principle of consulting authority*.** **The text does not say that.** What the verse commands to be asked about is definite: **the nature of the earlier messengers.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the structure of the sentence itself:** the command *fa'sʾalū* is joined by *fāʾ* to the preceding clause — it is therefore **a clause of consequence**, not an independent principle. **And the conditional clause narrows it further: *in kuntum lā taʿlamūn* — "if you do not know."**

### أَهْلَ ٱلذِّكْرِ — who is meant

**The same verse occurs word for word at `021-enbiya.md`, 21/7, and the disagreement was tabulated there. I rely on that treatment and bring the table over here:**

| View | Who the *ahl al-dhikr* are | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The people of the earlier scriptures** | The matter at issue is what the earlier messengers were; it is they who know this |
| 2 | **Those who possess knowledge, generally** | The wording allows it; *dhikr* means "what is known, what is remembered" |
| 3 | **Here *dhikr* is the Qurʾān; its people are those who know it** | The next verse says *wa anzalnā ilayka'l-**dhikr*** |

**I transmit the disagreement; I do not impose a preference.**

**But it must be recorded that the third view has an internal ground, and this one is peculiar to al-Naḥl:** **in the forty-fourth verse the same word is used of the Book that is sent down.** **There was a similar movement in al-Anbiyāʾ** — there, three verses later, it said *dhikrukum*, and in the al-Anbiyāʾ section this was recorded: **"ask (7) → if you do not know (7) → you already have it in your hand (10)."**

**In al-Naḥl the same movement is completed in two verses:**

| Verse | *Dhikr* | In whose hand |
|---|---|---|
| **43** | *Ahla'l-**dhikr*** | **Others** — those who are to be asked |
| **44** | *Wa anzalnā ilayka'l-**dhikr*** | **The addressee himself** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading:** the command does not set up a standing dependence. **What is commanded to be asked about is placed, immediately afterwards, into the addressee's own hand.**

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`)

**This verse has been used in very different places and to very different ends throughout history.** **In this commentary nothing is loaded onto the verse beyond what its wording and its context give.**

**What the verse lays down in its own wording is this, and it can be gathered into three items:**

| # | Item | Its warrant in the text |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The subject of the asking is definite** | That the earlier messengers were human — *illā rijālan nūḥī ilayhim* |
| 2 | **The condition for asking is not knowing** | *In kuntum lā taʿlamūn* |
| 3 | **Whom one is to ask is disputed** | The three views above |

**Inferences that go beyond these three items — on what subject one is to ask whom, how an authority in knowledge is to be constituted, how binding it is — come not from the wording of the verse but from later debates. This commentary does not enter that debate, and no legal ruling is issued.**

### رِجَالًا — a note about the word

***Rijāl* — men.** **Some exegetes have drawn a ruling from this word; others record that the word is used here for the contrast *not an angel, but a human being*** — because what the sentence is about is not the messenger's sex but his kind. **I transmit both explanations and lay down no ruling in this commentary;** the objection the verse answers is, in the text's own words, that the messenger is **a mortal man**.

### ٱلْبَيِّنَٰتِ وَٱلزُّبُر

**زُبُر** — the plural of *zabūr*, root **ز-ب-ر**: **to write, to incise in stone.** *Zabara'l-kitāb* — he wrote it. **In the root there is hardness and permanence; *zubratu'l-ḥadīd* — a lump of iron.**

**The same pair occurred at `035-fatir.md`, 35/25** (*jāʾathum rusuluhum bi'l-bayyināti wa bi'l-zuburi wa bi'l-kitābi'l-munīr*) — **there it was a threesome, here a pair.** This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.

### لِتُبَيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ

**The root ب-ي-ن occurs at three different places in the sūra and in three different tasks, and this is one of the sūra's structural signs:**

| Verse | Expression | Who makes clear | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| **39** | *Li-**yubayyina** lahumu'lladhī yakhtalifūna fīh* | **God** | What is disputed — **in the hereafter** |
| **44** | *Li-**tubayyina** li'l-nāsi mā nuzzila ilayhim* | **The Prophet** | What has been sent down — **in this world** |
| **92** | *Wa la-**yubayyinanna** lakum yawma'l-qiyāmati mā kuntum fīhi takhtalifūn* | **God** | What is disputed — **in the hereafter, to the second person** |
| **89** | *Wa nazzalnā ʿalayka'l-kitāba **tibyānan** li-kulli shayʾ* | **The Book itself** | Everything |

**All four occurrences can be verified from the text. And I record this as my own reading:** the work of making clear is distributed among three distinct subjects — **the Book, the messenger, and finally God.** **And the order is worth recording: the Book makes clear, the messenger makes clear, and what is left over is made clear in the hereafter.** A place is thus shown where disagreement will be closed, if it does not close in this world.

---

## 16/45-47 — أَفَأَمِنَ ٱلَّذِينَ مَكَرُوا۟ ٱلسَّيِّـَٔاتِ أَن يَخْسِفَ ٱللَّهُ بِهِمُ ٱلْأَرْضَ أَوْ يَأْتِيَهُمُ ٱلْعَذَابُ مِنْ حَيْثُ لَا يَشْعُرُونَ · أَوْ يَأْخُذَهُمْ فِى تَقَلُّبِهِمْ فَمَا هُم بِمُعْجِزِينَ · أَوْ يَأْخُذَهُمْ عَلَىٰ تَخَوُّفٍ فَإِنَّ رَبَّكُمْ لَرَءُوفٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*A-fa-amina'lladhīna makaru'l-sayyiʾāti an yakhsifa'llāhu bihimu'l-arḍa aw yaʾtiyahumu'l-ʿadhābu min ḥaythu lā yashʿurūn · Aw yaʾkhudhahum fī taqallubihim fa-mā hum bi-muʿjizīn · Aw yaʾkhudhahum ʿalā takhawwuf, fa-inna rabbakum la-raʾūfun raḥīm*

"**Do those who plotted evil feel safe that God will not make the earth swallow them, or that the punishment will not come at them from where they do not perceive it?** · **Or that He will not seize them as they go to and fro — and they cannot thwart Him.** · **Or that He will not seize them amid a mounting fear. Your Lord is truly most kind, most merciful.**"

### Four possibilities, increasing in subtlety

**The verse enumerates four possibilities, and their ordering must be recorded:**

| Order | Possibility | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Yakhsifa'llāhu bihimu'l-arḍ* | **The earth swallowing them** — sudden, total |
| 2 | *Yaʾtiyahumu'l-ʿadhābu min ḥaythu lā yashʿurūn* | **From where it is not reckoned** — sudden, unlooked for |
| 3 | *Yaʾkhudhahum **fī taqallubihim*** | **At their business** — inside daily life |
| 4 | *Yaʾkhudhahum **ʿalā takhawwuf*** | **Upon fear** — slow, by diminishing |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is this ordering:** the list runs **from the most sudden to the most gradual.** **The first two happen in an instant; the third while they are on the move; the fourth is spread over time.**

**And this must be recorded:** *wa atāhumu'l-ʿadhābu min ḥaythu lā yashʿurūn* had already occurred in the twenty-sixth verse — **about a people of the past.** **Here the same sentence comes back as a possibility.** This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and **it turns an example into a warning.**

### تَقَلُّب and تَخَوُّف

**تَقَلُّب** — root **ق-ل-ب**: to turn, to overturn. **A verbal noun of Form V: *taqallub*, to turn about, to pass from one state into another.** **The lexicographers record here in particular the going to and fro on the roads for trade.**

**تَخَوُّف** — root **خ-و-ف**: fear. **There is disagreement over the sense of the word here, and it must be set out in a table:**

| Reading | What *ʿalā takhawwuf* is | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **To be seized while in fear** | The common sense of the root |
| 2 | **To be seized by diminishing** — *takhawwuf*, "to reduce" | It is transmitted that in some Arab dialects the verb *takhawwafa* is used in the sense "he reduced it, ate it away at the edge" |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The second reading sits better with the four-step ordering above** — for what is expected at the end of the list is gradualness. **I record this as an observation, not as a preference.**

### فَإِنَّ رَبَّكُمْ لَرَءُوفٌ رَّحِيمٌ

**And the block closes once more with the same two names — the same closing as in the seventh verse.**

| Verse | Closing | What comes before it |
|---|---|---|
| **7** | *Inna rabbakum la-**raʾūfun raḥīm*** | **A favour** — the beast that carries loads |
| **47** | *Fa-inna rabbakum la-**raʾūfun raḥīm*** | **Four possibilities of punishment** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, its ground being these two contexts:** the same two names, the one after a favour, the other after a threat. **The verse thereby says that the deferral too comes from the same attribute** — for not one of the four possibilities has come to pass.

---

## 16/48-50 — أَوَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ إِلَىٰ مَا خَلَقَ ٱللَّهُ مِن شَىْءٍ يَتَفَيَّؤُا۟ ظِلَٰلُهُۥ عَنِ ٱلْيَمِينِ وَٱلشَّمَآئِلِ سُجَّدًا لِّلَّهِ وَهُمْ دَٰخِرُونَ · وَلِلَّهِ يَسْجُدُ مَا فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَمَا فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ مِن دَآبَّةٍ وَٱلْمَلَٰٓئِكَةُ وَهُمْ لَا يَسْتَكْبِرُونَ · يَخَافُونَ رَبَّهُم مِّن فَوْقِهِمْ وَيَفْعَلُونَ مَا يُؤْمَرُونَ

*A-wa-lam yaraw ilā mā khalaqa'llāhu min shayʾin yatafayyaʾu ẓilāluhū ʿani'l-yamīni wa'l-shamāʾili sujjadan li'llāhi wa hum dākhirūn · Wa li'llāhi yasjudu mā fi'l-samāwāti wa mā fi'l-arḍi min dābbatin wa'l-malāʾikatu wa hum lā yastakbirūn · Yakhāfūna rabbahum min fawqihim wa yafʿalūna mā yuʾmarūn*

"**Have they not looked at the things God has created? Their shadows turn to the right and to the left, prostrating themselves to God, and they are lowly.** · **To God prostrates whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth of living creatures, and the angels; and they do not make themselves great.** · **They fear their Lord above them, and they do what they are commanded.**"

### The proof from the shadow — a comparison with al-Furqān

**The proof from the shadow was treated in detail in this commentary: at `025-furkan.md`, 25/45-46** (*a-lam tara ilā rabbika kayfa madda'l-ẓilla wa law shāʾa la-jaʿalahū sākinā, thumma jaʿalna'l-shamsa ʿalayhi dalīlā, thumma qabaḍnāhu ilaynā qabḍan yasīrā*). **I rely on that treatment and summarise what was recorded there:**

> **"A shadow is a thing with no existence of its own. It cannot be held and cannot be measured; it exists only by the existence of something else. And it is precisely this that the verse makes into a proof."**
>
> **"What one expects is that the shadow should be the proof of the sun. The verse says the reverse: the sun is the proof of the shadow. When a shadow is to be read, the place to look is not the shadow itself — it is its source."**

**Now I compare the two verses, because they put the same fact to two different uses:**

| | **al-Furqān 25/45-46** | **al-Naḥl 16/48** |
|---|---|---|
| The verb | *Madda'l-ẓill* — **He stretched it out**; *qabaḍnāhu* — **We drew it in** | *Yatafayyaʾu* — **it turns to and fro** |
| The tense | **Perfect** — an ordering already made | **Imperfect** — **it goes on** |
| The agent | **God** — who stretches the shadow out and draws it in | **The shadow itself** — *ẓilāluhū* is the subject |
| What the proof is about | **The existence of an order** — *jaʿala'l-shamsa ʿalayhi dalīlā* | **Submission** — *sujjadan li'llāhi wa hum dākhirūn* |
| The word | ***ظِلّ* (ẓill)** — shadow in general | ***تَفَيُّؤ* (tafayyuʾ)** — the turning shadow |
| What is asked | *A-lam **tara*** — singular, the addressee | *A-wa-lam **yaraw*** — plural, third person |

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the difference of agent:** in al-Furqān the shadow is **passive** — it is stretched out and drawn in. **In al-Naḥl the shadow is the *agent*:** it turns of itself, it prostrates of itself. **The same fact is given in the one as the object of an ordering, in the other as the subject of an obedience.**

### تَفَيَّؤُ — root: ف-ي-ء

**The choice of this word is the most striking thing in the verse, and the root was analysed in detail at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/9. I rely on that treatment and bring its notes over here:**

> **"Root: ف-ي-ء. The primary sense the lexicographers record: to turn, to turn back. And the concrete picture in the root is this: *al-fayʾ* is the shadow that turns back after the sun has passed the zenith."**

**And the table of distinctions drawn up in al-Ḥujurāt works directly here:**

| Word | Root | Which shadow |
|---|---|---|
| **ظِلّ (ẓill)** | ظ-ل-ل | Shadow in general; and in particular the shadow that **shortens** from morning to noon |
| **فَيْء (fayʾ)** | ف-ي-ء | The shadow that **turns back and lengthens** after noon |

**And the picture recorded in the al-Ḥujurāt section was this:** in the morning the shadow is long; as the sun rises it shortens; at noon it all but vanishes; **then it turns back** and lengthens until evening.

**What belongs here must be recorded, and the verse uses the two words together:** *ya**tafayyaʾu** **ẓilā**luhū.* **The verb is from the root ف-ي-ء, its subject from the root ظ-ل-ل.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** two roots come together in one sentence. **The verse says "shadow", and names the work the shadow does with the verb *to turn*.** A motionless object is thereby made **a moving agent.**

**And it was recorded that in al-Ḥujurāt the verb *tafīʾa* was chosen for a moral turning — the attacking party's "returning to its place".** **In al-Naḥl the same verb tells of a turning that takes place in nature.**

| Place | The verb *fayʾ* | What turns |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ḥujurāt 49/9** | *Ḥattā **tafīʾa** ilā amri'llāh* | **The attacking party** — to God's command |
| **al-Naḥl 16/48** | *Ya**tafayyaʾu** ẓilāluhū* | **The shadow** — to the right and to the left |

**The same root: in the one a turning that is asked for, in the other a turning that takes place.** **This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.** **And the idea recorded in al-Ḥujurāt of "returning to one's natural condition" stands, in al-Naḥl's usage, in the word's own home:** the shadow turns not under compulsion but **of itself, by the course of the sun.**

### عَنِ ٱلْيَمِينِ وَٱلشَّمَآئِلِ — singular and plural

**There is an asymmetry in the wording, and the lexicographers dwell on it:** *al-yamīn* is **singular**, *al-shamāʾil* **plural.**

| Explanation | Ground |
|---|---|
| 1 | ***Yamīn* here is a generic noun;** it comes in the singular but carries a plural sense |
| 2 | **The right is one direction, the left sides are many** — a body has one right, but its shadow may fall in more than one direction |
| 3 | **Agreement with the verse-ending** — the word was chosen to suit the sound of the sentence |

**All three explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **But the fact itself can be verified from the text and must be recorded.**

### سُجَّدًا لِّلَّهِ — the scope of the prostration

**And a note of method is needed here.**

**The verse ascribes prostration to the shadow. This is a pattern treated at many places in this commentary: the ascribing of an act to beings that are inanimate or without will.**

**The forty-ninth verse gives the scope openly:** *wa li'llāhi yasjudu mā fi'l-samāwāti wa mā fi'l-arḍi **min dābbatin wa'l-malāʾika***.

**And the word order must be recorded:** the angels are enumerated separately — *wa'l-malāʾika*. **Yet "what is in the heavens" would already have covered them.**

**The lexicographers count this separate enumeration as *takhṣīṣ* (naming a thing specially).** **And I give this as an observation:** the angels are named together with the note that follows immediately upon them, *wa hum lā yastakbirūn* (they do not make themselves great). **The reason they are enumerated separately is therefore the attribute that follows.**

**دَآبَّة** — root **د-ب-ب**: **a living thing that moves upon the ground.** **The root was treated at `045-casiye.md`, 45/4;** I do not repeat it here.

### وَهُمْ دَٰخِرُونَ — root: د-خ-ر

**The word was treated at `040-gafir.md`, 40/60, and I rely on that treatment.** What was recorded there was this:

> **"دَاخِرِين — root د-خ-ر: to be made small, to bow the neck, to be brought low. The word is the exact opposite of pride, and it is set against it in the same sentence."**

**And this note from Ghāfir works here as well — and even more plainly:**

| Place | Of whom *dākhirīn* is said | Its opposite |
|---|---|---|
| **Ghāfir 40/60** | *Alladhīna **yastakbirūna** ʿan ʿibādatī sa-yadkhulūna jahannama **dākhirīn*** | *Yastakbirūn* — **in the same sentence** |
| **al-Naḥl 16/48-49** | The shadows and those who prostrate — *wa hum **dākhirūn*** | *Wa hum **lā yastakbirūn*** (49) — **in the next verse** |

**The same two words, set against each other in two different sūras.** **This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

**And the difference must be recorded; I give it as my own reading:** in Ghāfir being *dākhir* is a **punishment** — the end of the one who makes himself great. **In al-Naḥl it is a *state*** — the attribute of what is already so. **The same word, then, is in the one a position one is brought down to, in the other a position one already occupies.**

### يَخَافُونَ رَبَّهُم مِّن فَوْقِهِمْ

**The lexicographers dwell on the construction: *min fawqihim* — "from above them", or "who is above them".**

| Reading | What it attaches to |
|---|---|
| 1 | ***Rabbahum min fawqihim*** — they fear their Lord, who is above them |
| 2 | ***Yakhāfūna … min fawqihim*** — the direction from which the fear comes |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the closing of the verse must be recorded: *wa yafʿalūna mā yuʾmarūn* — "and they do what they are commanded."**

**I give this as an observation, and it bears on the sūra as a whole:** the forty-eighth verse enumerated the shadows, the forty-ninth living creatures and angels. **The fiftieth ties their common attribute to a single act: obeying the command.** **And the sūra had already named, in its fourth verse, the one being that does not obey that command: *khaṣīmun mubīn*.**

---

## 16/51-53 — وَقَالَ ٱللَّهُ لَا تَتَّخِذُوٓا۟ إِلَٰهَيْنِ ٱثْنَيْنِ إِنَّمَا هُوَ إِلَٰهٌ وَٰحِدٌ فَإِيَّٰىَ فَٱرْهَبُونِ · وَلَهُۥ مَا فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَلَهُ ٱلدِّينُ وَاصِبًا أَفَغَيْرَ ٱللَّهِ تَتَّقُونَ · وَمَا بِكُم مِّن نِّعْمَةٍ فَمِنَ ٱللَّهِ ثُمَّ إِذَا مَسَّكُمُ ٱلضُّرُّ فَإِلَيْهِ تَجْـَٔرُونَ

*Wa qāla'llāhu lā tattakhidhū ilāhayni'thnayn, innamā huwa ilāhun wāḥid, fa-iyyāya fa'rhabūn · Wa lahū mā fi'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi wa lahu'l-dīnu wāṣibā, a-fa-ghayra'llāhi tattaqūn · Wa mā bikum min niʿmatin fa-mina'llāh, thumma idhā massakumu'l-ḍurru fa-ilayhi tajʾarūn*

"**God said: 'Do not take two gods; He is only one god. So fear Me, and Me alone.'** · **To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and the earth, and to Him belongs religion, unceasingly. Will you then guard yourselves against any but God?** · **Every favour you have is from God. Then, when hardship touches you, it is to Him that you cry aloud.**"

### إِلَٰهَيْنِ ٱثْنَيْنِ — a number that looks superfluous

***Ilāhayni* is already dual. After it *ithnayni* (two) has been added.**

**The lexicographers count this as emphasis. But one further explanation belonging here must be recorded, and I give it as an observation:** the stress falls not on **two** but on **more than one.** **And the answering clause bears this out: *innamā huwa ilāhun wāḥid* — here too the adjective *wāḥid* (one) has been added separately.**

**On both sides, then, the sentence states the number outright. It is a symmetry of word order.**

### وَاصِبًا — root: و-ص-ب

**Two explanations of the sense of the root are transmitted:**

| Explanation | Sense | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Continuous, unbroken** | *Waṣaba* — it continued; *wāṣib* — perpetual |
| 2 | **Obligatory, that to which submission is due** | *Waṣab* — weariness, toil; from this, "what is burdensome" |

**The majority of the lexicographers give the first sense.** **I transmit both explanations; I do not impose a preference.**

**The word occurs in this form only here in the Qurʾān.**

### تَجْـَٔرُونَ — root: ج-أ-ر

**The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root thus: the long, pain-drawn bellowing of an animal.** *Juʾār* — the cry of cattle and of like beasts.

**The choice of the word is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:** the verse does not say *tadʿūn* (you call, you pray). **It chooses the sound that comes before speech.** The turning in the moment of distress is thus named not as a considered prayer but as **a sound that escapes without thought.**

**The word occurs at few places in the Qurʾān; it is recorded that in this form it is found here and in the twenty-third sūra (al-Muʾminūn 23/64).**

**The same cycle was treated at many places in this commentary: `029-ankebut.md` 29/65, `030-rum.md` 30/33 and 30/49-51, `031-lokman.md` 31/32.** **I rely on those treatments.** **In the al-Rūm section this cycle was tabulated: hardship → turning → mercy → association.** **al-Naḥl builds the same cycle between its fifty-third and fifty-fifth verses.**

---

## 16/54-56 — ثُمَّ إِذَا كَشَفَ ٱلضُّرَّ عَنكُمْ إِذَا فَرِيقٌ مِّنكُم بِرَبِّهِمْ يُشْرِكُونَ · لِيَكْفُرُوا۟ بِمَآ ءَاتَيْنَٰهُمْ فَتَمَتَّعُوا۟ فَسَوْفَ تَعْلَمُونَ · وَيَجْعَلُونَ لِمَا لَا يَعْلَمُونَ نَصِيبًا مِّمَّا رَزَقْنَٰهُمْ تَٱللَّهِ لَتُسْـَٔلُنَّ عَمَّا كُنتُمْ تَفْتَرُونَ

*Thumma idhā kashafa'l-ḍurra ʿankum idhā farīqun minkum bi-rabbihim yushrikūn · Li-yakfurū bimā ātaynāhum, fa-tamattaʿū fa-sawfa taʿlamūn · Wa yajʿalūna li-mā lā yaʿlamūna naṣīban mimmā razaqnāhum, ta'llāhi la-tusʾalunna ʿammā kuntum taftarūn*

"**Then, when He removes the hardship from you, at once a party among you associates others with their Lord.** · **So as to be ungrateful for what We have given them. Take your enjoyment, then; you will come to know.** · **And out of the provision We have given them they set aside a share for what knows nothing. By God, you shall surely be questioned about what you were inventing.**"

### Two *idhā*s — how the cycle is built

**There are two *idhā*s in the sentence, and they do different work:**

| *Idhā* | Its work | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Condition** — "when…" | *Idhā kashafa'l-ḍurra ʿankum* |
| 2 | ***Idhā fujāʾiyya*** — "and behold" | *Idhā farīqun minkum … yushrikūn* |

***Idhā fujāʾiyya* was treated at many places in this commentary: `036-yasin.md` 36/77 and `030-rum.md` 30/20.** **And the same particle was used in the sūra's fourth verse** (*fa-idhā huwa khaṣīmun mubīn*). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the same particle is used in the sūra's fourth verse for the passage **from creation to disputation**, and in the fifty-fourth for the passage **from rescue to association.** **Both passages are given in the same way, with the steps between them leapt over.**

**فَرِيقٌ مِّنكُم** — and the scope must be recorded. **The verse does not say "all of you": *farīqun minkum* — "a party among you".** **And the same care was recorded at `029-ankebut.md`, 29/47;** I rely on that treatment. **Per the method (`USLUP.md`) the same line is kept here: the ruling is tied not to a group but to a stance.**

### لِمَا لَا يَعْلَمُونَ

**The construction is read in two ways, and the disagreement must be recorded:**

| Reading | Who does not know | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The things for which the share is set aside** — "those that know nothing" | It coincides with *amwātun ghayru aḥyāʾ* in the twentieth verse |
| 2 | **Those who set the share aside** — "for things they do not know" | They do not know what it is they are setting a share aside for |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.** **The internal ground of the first reading is the twentieth verse** (*wa mā yashʿurūna ayyāna yubʿathūn*); I record this as an observation.

**تَٱللَّهِ** — **the oath particle *tāʾ*.** In Arabic there are three oath particles (*wāw*, *bāʾ*, *tāʾ*), and the lexicographers record that the *tāʾ* is the least used of them and that it mostly conveys **wonder and astonishment.** **And in the Qurʾān an oath with *tāʾ* comes only with the name *Allāh*.**

---

## 16/57-59 — وَيَجْعَلُونَ لِلَّهِ ٱلْبَنَٰتِ سُبْحَٰنَهُۥ وَلَهُم مَّا يَشْتَهُونَ · وَإِذَا بُشِّرَ أَحَدُهُم بِٱلْأُنثَىٰ ظَلَّ وَجْهُهُۥ مُسْوَدًّا وَهُوَ كَظِيمٌ · يَتَوَٰرَىٰ مِنَ ٱلْقَوْمِ مِن سُوٓءِ مَا بُشِّرَ بِهِۦٓ أَيُمْسِكُهُۥ عَلَىٰ هُونٍ أَمْ يَدُسُّهُۥ فِى ٱلتُّرَابِ أَلَا سَآءَ مَا يَحْكُمُونَ

*Wa yajʿalūna li'llāhi'l-banāti subḥānahū wa lahum mā yashtahūn · Wa idhā bushshira aḥaduhum bi'l-unthā ẓalla wajhuhū muswaddan wa huwa kaẓīm · Yatawārā mina'l-qawmi min sūʾi mā bushshira bih, a-yumsikuhū ʿalā hūnin am yadussuhū fi'l-turāb, alā sāʾa mā yaḥkumūn*

"**They assign daughters to God — glory be to Him — and to themselves what they crave.** · **When one of them is given news of a girl, his face turns black and he is choked with rage.** · **He hides himself from the people because of the evil of the news given him: is he to keep her in humiliation, or bury her in the dust? Beware — evil is the judgement they make!**"

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`), to be entered at the outset

**In treating these three verses one thing must be kept clear from the start, and the same note was entered at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/17-18:**

**The verse lays down no ruling about women. The verse reports the stance of a society towards a female child, and shows the contradiction inside that stance.**

**And the evidence for this within the text is the wording of the verse. It is set out below.**

### How the contradiction is built — the fifty-seventh verse

**The sentence is made of two halves, and the contrast between them is built by the choice of words:**

| Half | Expression | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Wa yajʿalūna **li'llāhi'l-banāt*** | **To God** — daughters |
| 2 | *Wa **lahum** mā **yashtahūn*** | **To themselves** — what they crave |

**The verb in the second half must be recorded: *yashtahūn* — root ش-ه-و: appetite, desire, the craving of the self.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is this verb:** in the second half the verse does not say "sons". **It says "what they crave".** The measure is thus named not as a fact but **as an appetite.** **With this word the sentence says that the preference rests not on a judgement but on a craving.**

**And *subḥānahū* comes in between — between the two halves.** **I give this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence of *tanzīh* is placed immediately after the ascription, before the comparison is made. **The sentence thus rejects one half before it sets the two halves side by side.**

### A comparison of three verses

**This scene occurs in three places in the Qurʾān, and all three were treated in this commentary. The comparison was already made at `081-tekvir.md`, 81/8-9 (*mawʾūda*), and at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/17 (*bimā ḍaraba li'l-raḥmāni mathalā*). I rely on those treatments and complete the table from here, from the place where the wording is plainest:**

| | **al-Naḥl 16/57-59** | **al-Takwīr 81/8-9** | **al-Zukhruf 43/17** |
|---|---|---|---|
| The centre of the scene | **The father** — his reaction and his hesitation | **The child** — the weight laid upon her | **The father** — his contradiction |
| The key word | ***يَدُسُّ* (yadussu)** — to bury in secret | ***مَوْءُودَة* (mawʾūda)** — one upon whom a weight was laid | ***bimā ḍaraba … mathalā*** — the thing he ascribes |
| The picture in the root | **To push a thing secretly inside another** | **A weight coming down upon one** | (not a root but a construction) |
| Form | **A verb** — the father the agent | **A passive participle** — the name of the victim | **A relative clause** — the ascription |
| The word "girl" | ***Bi'l-unthā*** — **said outright** | ***Mawʾūda*** — feminine, but "girl" is not said | **Not said at all** |
| The work of the verse | **Diagnosis** — the motive is named | **Reckoning** — the question is put to the victim | **The showing of a contradiction** |
| The closing | *Alā **sāʾa mā yaḥkumūn*** | *Bi-ayyi dhanbin **qutilat*** | *A-fa-man yunashshaʾu fi'l-ḥilya…* |

**The three verses take hold of the same fact from three different sides, and this table is the completed form of what was drawn up in `081-tekvir.md` and `043-zuhruf.md`.**

**And I record this as my own reading, its ground being the fifth line:** the one place where the word is said outright is al-Naḥl. **al-Takwīr names the victim but does not say "girl"; al-Zukhruf does not say it at all, and leaves the reader alone with his own ascription.** **al-Naḥl builds the scene in full plainness, using the word itself.** Read together, the three verses show the same event told **from three distances**: from closest up (al-Naḥl), from the victim's place (al-Takwīr), and from inside the mind (al-Zukhruf).

### يَدُسُّهُۥ فِى ٱلتُّرَابِ — root: د-س-س

**The root was analysed in detail at `091-sems.md`, 91/10, and this verse was named there. I rely on that treatment and take over its notes:**

> **"The concrete sense of the root: to push a thing secretly inside another, to hide it, to bury it."**
> **"دَسِيسَة (*dasīsa*) — a hidden trap, a stratagem. In Turkish the borrowed word is still in everyday use."**
> **"The verb دَسَّ, in the Qurʾān's own usage, means to put a living being under the earth."**

**And in the al-Shams section this connection was recorded: the root occurs in only two places in the Qurʾān — here and at al-Shams 91/10 (*wa qad khāba man dassāhā*).** **The verb used of the man who buries his own soul is thus the very verb of this verse.**

**What belongs here is the comparison of this verb with the other, and the verse offers two options:**

| Option | Verb | Root | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | *A-**yumsikuhū** ʿalā hūn* | م-س-ك | **To keep** — but with the qualification *hūn* |
| 2 | *Am **yadussuhū** fi'l-turāb* | د-س-س | **To push it secretly into the dust** |

**هُون** — root **ه-و-ن**: **lightness, worthlessness, humiliation.** *Hawn* — ease; *hūn / hawān* — humiliation. **The same root opens in two opposite directions, and the lexicographers record it:** a thing's being light is either ease or worthlessness.

**And the side of the two options that must be recorded is this:** the verse **offers no good option.** The first is "to keep" — but *ʿalā hūn*, in humiliation. **Both of the ways offered are evil, and the verse closes on both with a sentence of judgement: *alā sāʾa mā yaḥkumūn*.**

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the qualification *ʿalā hūn*:** what the verse condemns is not the act of burying alone. **How the keeping is done is condemned as well.** **The diagnosis therefore begins before the killing — in the counting of a child as a burden and a shame.**

### ظَلَّ وَجْهُهُۥ مُسْوَدًّا وَهُوَ كَظِيمٌ

**This sentence occurred word for word at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/17, and was treated there in detail. I rely on that treatment and summarise its notes:**

> **"ظَلَّ is a defective verb: to be in a state through the day, to remain in that state. The sentence therefore does not say 'his face turned black'; it says 'his face remained blackened'. It conveys duration."**
>
> **"مُسْوَدًّا — root س-و-د, an active participle of Form IX. In Arabic Form IX (*ifʿilāl*) is used almost only for colours and defects, and the form conveys a colour's settling upon a person."**
>
> **"كَظِيم — root ك-ظ-م. *Kaẓama'l-qirba* — he filled a waterskin and tied its mouth. *Kaẓīm* is on the *faʿīl* pattern and denotes one who ties his own mouth shut; *makẓūm* (al-Qalam 68/48) denotes one stopped up from outside."**

**I do not repeat it here. Two things belong here.**

**One — the difference between *bi'l-unthā* and *bimā ḍaraba*.** **In al-Zukhruf the word did not occur at all; here it does.** In the al-Zukhruf section it was recorded: *"The word — 'girl' — does not occur in the sentence. In its place the man's own ascription is used as a definition."* **al-Naḥl does not do this.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the wording of the two verses:** al-Zukhruf hides the word in order to show the contradiction. **al-Naḥl says the word in order to tell what happened.** The work of the two verses differs, and so does their wording.

**Two — the tension produced by the verb *bushshira*.** **It was recorded in `043-zuhruf.md`:** *bushshira* is the verb for announcing good news; the reaction that follows is the reaction to bad news. **In naming the event, then, the verse declares its side: what has happened is glad tidings.**

**And al-Naḥl does this once more, with the same root and more sharply: *min sūʾi mā bushshira bih* — "because of the evil of what he was given glad tidings of".**

**This construction must be recorded, and it was named in `043-zuhruf.md` as well:** *sūʾ* (evil) and *bushshira* (he was given glad tidings) **stand side by side within a single expression.** **The two words give each other the lie.**

**I give this as my own reading; its ground is that juxtaposition:** the evil lies not in the event but in **the appraisal.** The verse goes on calling the thing "glad tidings" and does not ascribe the evil to it — *sūʾi **mā bushshira bih***, "the evil of what he was given glad tidings of", is the evil as it stands in the man's own eyes. **The sentence transmits an appraisal; it does not lay down a ruling.**

### يَتَوَٰرَىٰ مِنَ ٱلْقَوْمِ — root: و-ر-ي

**The root: to hide oneself, to take cover behind something.** *Warāʾ* — what is behind; *tawārā* — he drew back, he hid himself.

**And the object of the verb must be recorded:** *mina'l-**qawm*** — "from the people". **The party he hides from is his own community.**

**I give this as an observation, and it agrees with the historical frame recorded in `081-tekvir.md`:** it was recorded there that this practice was **not widespread, but known and capable of being condoned.** **The wording of the verse supports this:** had the practice been counted ordinary by the society, there would have been no need to hide. **The man hides from a shame — and the verse says what the shame is: that a girl has been given to him.**

### The historical frame

**The historical frame of this subject was given in detail in `081-tekvir.md`: the two distinct motives the Qurʾān names (shame and the fear of poverty), the need to avoid both exaggerations about how widespread the practice was, and the fact that it was not the characteristic of one people.** **I rely on that treatment and do not repeat it here.**

**Only this need be added here:** **these three verses of al-Naḥl are the plainest textual source for the "shame" motive within that frame.** al-Isrāʾ 17/31 and al-Anʿām 6/151 say *"your children"* and do not specify sex; **this verse, by saying *bi'l-unthā*, names a distinct fact.**

### أَلَا سَآءَ مَا يَحْكُمُونَ

**The closing comes with a verb of *giving judgement*: *yaḥkumūn*.**

**And this must be recorded: what was done in the fifty-seventh verse was also a judgement — assigning daughters to God and to themselves what they crave.** **What is done in the fifty-ninth is also a judgement — whether to keep or to bury.**

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the verb itself:** the verse names in a single word what it has been describing for three verses: **the giving of judgement.** And the judgement given is wrong in two places at once — both in what is ascribed above and in what is decided at home. **The same measure is at work in two different places.**

---

## 16/60-62 — لِلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ مَثَلُ ٱلسَّوْءِ وَلِلَّهِ ٱلْمَثَلُ ٱلْأَعْلَىٰ وَهُوَ ٱلْعَزِيزُ ٱلْحَكِيمُ · وَلَوْ يُؤَاخِذُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلنَّاسَ بِظُلْمِهِم مَّا تَرَكَ عَلَيْهَا مِن دَآبَّةٍ وَلَٰكِن يُؤَخِّرُهُمْ إِلَىٰٓ أَجَلٍ مُّسَمًّى · وَيَجْعَلُونَ لِلَّهِ مَا يَكْرَهُونَ وَتَصِفُ أَلْسِنَتُهُمُ ٱلْكَذِبَ أَنَّ لَهُمُ ٱلْحُسْنَىٰ لَا جَرَمَ أَنَّ لَهُمُ ٱلنَّارَ وَأَنَّهُم مُّفْرَطُونَ

*Lilladhīna lā yuʾminūna bi'l-ākhirati mathalu'l-sawʾ, wa li'llāhi'l-mathalu'l-aʿlā, wa huwa'l-ʿazīzu'l-ḥakīm · Wa law yuʾākhidhu'llāhu'l-nāsa bi-ẓulmihim mā taraka ʿalayhā min dābbatin wa lākin yuʾakhkhiruhum ilā ajalin musammā · Wa yajʿalūna li'llāhi mā yakrahūna wa taṣifu alsinatuhumu'l-kadhiba anna lahumu'l-ḥusnā, lā jarama anna lahumu'l-nāra wa annahum mufraṭūn*

"**To those who do not believe in the hereafter belongs the likeness of evil; and to God belongs the highest likeness.** He is the Mighty, the Wise. · **Were God to take people to task for their wrongdoing He would not leave a single creature upon the earth; but He defers them to an appointed term.** · **They assign to God what they themselves dislike, and their tongues frame the lie that the best is theirs. Beyond doubt the Fire is theirs, and they are sent on ahead.**"

### وَلَوْ يُؤَاخِذُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلنَّاسَ — an exact overlap with Fāṭir

**This verse occurs at `035-fatir.md`, 35/45, in almost the same words, and was treated there in detail as the closing verse of that sūra. I rely on that treatment.** What was recorded there:

> **"عَلَىٰ ظَهْرِهَا — 'upon its back'. The verse does not say what the pronoun refers to; the lexicographers record that it is the earth."**
> **"In telling of the deferral of the punishment the scope is not kept to man — *min dābba*, every living thing upon the earth."**
> **"إِلَىٰٓ أَجَلٍ مُّسَمًّى — to a term that has been named. The same construction was treated at `046-ahkaf.md`, 46/3, and at `039-zumer.md`, 39/42."**

**The difference that belongs here must be recorded, and it is a single word:**

| | **Fāṭir 35/45** | **al-Naḥl 16/61** |
|---|---|---|
| The cause | *Bimā **kasabū*** — for what they have **earned** | *Bi-**ẓulmihim*** — for their **wrongdoing** |
| The pronoun | *ʿAlā **ẓahrihā*** — "upon its back" | *ʿAlayhā* — "upon it" |
| Its place in the sūra | **The last verse** — a closing | **In the middle** — inside a block |

**I record this as an observation:** Fāṭir uses a general word for what is earned; **al-Naḥl, in the middle of a sūra and directly after the scene of the infant daughter, narrows the word and says *wrongdoing*.** **The context explains the choice of word.**

### مُّفْرَطُون — root: ف-ر-ط

**The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root thus: to go ahead, to go in front; and to neglect a thing and leave it behind.** *Faraṭ* — the scout who goes ahead of the caravan to find water.

**There is disagreement over the reading and the sense of the word here, and it must be set out in a table:**

| Reading | Word | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Mufraṭūn* (with *fatḥa* on the *rāʾ*) | **Sent on ahead, dispatched first to the Fire** — from the picture of the *faraṭ* |
| 2 | *Mufriṭūn* (with *kasra* on the *rāʾ*) | **Those who overstep** — from the root *ifrāṭ* |
| 3 | *Mufarriṭūn* | **Those who neglect, who leave things behind** — from the root *tafrīṭ* |

**All three readings are transmitted in the sources on the variant readings, and the sense changes accordingly. I do not impose a preference.**

**And the first reading has a certain internal appeal, which I record as an observation:** an irony is set up between the concrete picture in the root (**the scout of the caravan**) and the subject of the sentence (**the going to the Fire**) — **to be in front is not, this time, a distinction.**

---

## 16/63-64 — تَٱللَّهِ لَقَدْ أَرْسَلْنَآ إِلَىٰٓ أُمَمٍ مِّن قَبْلِكَ فَزَيَّنَ لَهُمُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنُ أَعْمَٰلَهُمْ فَهُوَ وَلِيُّهُمُ ٱلْيَوْمَ وَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ · وَمَآ أَنزَلْنَا عَلَيْكَ ٱلْكِتَٰبَ إِلَّا لِتُبَيِّنَ لَهُمُ ٱلَّذِى ٱخْتَلَفُوا۟ فِيهِ وَهُدًى وَرَحْمَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يُؤْمِنُونَ

*Ta'llāhi laqad arsalnā ilā umamin min qablika fa-zayyana lahumu'l-shayṭānu aʿmālahum fa-huwa waliyyuhumu'l-yawma wa lahum ʿadhābun alīm · Wa mā anzalnā ʿalayka'l-kitāba illā li-tubayyina lahumu'lladhi'khtalafū fīhi wa hudan wa raḥmatan li-qawmin yuʾminūn*

"**By God, We sent messengers to communities before you; but Satan made their deeds seem fair to them. Today too he is their patron, and for them there is a painful punishment.** · **We sent down the Book to you only that you might make clear to them that over which they differed, and as guidance and mercy for a people who believe.**"

### فَزَيَّنَ لَهُمُ ٱلشَّيْطَٰن — the naming of the agent

**The root ز-ي-ن and the verb *tazyīn* were treated at many places in this commentary, and a pattern was recorded there:** **at `035-fatir.md`, 35/8, and at `040-gafir.md`, 40/37, the verb came in the passive — the one who made things seem fair was not named.**

**Here the agent is named: *fa-zayyana lahumu'l-**shayṭān***.**

**This is a difference that must be recorded, and I give it as an observation:** the same verb comes in some places without an agent and in others with one. **And even where the agent is named, man's responsibility is not lifted — for the verse says *fa-huwa waliyyuhum*: the patronage is a relationship that has been accepted.**

### The sentence of the thirty-ninth verse comes back

**It must be recorded: the sentence *li-yubayyina lahumu'lladhī yakhtalifūna fīh* occurred in the thirty-ninth verse — there the subject was God and the time was the hereafter.**

**In the sixty-fourth the same sentence returns with the subject changed: *li-tubayyina lahumu'lladhi'khtalafū fīh* — the subject is the Prophet, the time this world.**

| Verse | Verb | Subject | Tense | When |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **39** | *Li-**yu**bayyina* | **God** | *Yakhtalifūna* — **imperfect**, still going on | **In the hereafter** |
| **64** | *Li-**tu**bayyina* | **The Prophet** | *Ikhtalafū* — **perfect**, done | **In this world** |

**The difference of tense must be recorded, and I give it as my own reading:** in the thirty-ninth verse the disagreement is something **still going on**; in the sixty-fourth something **already over.** **The Book, then, is for making clear a disagreement that ought to have been settled; the hereafter, for closing the one that goes on.**

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and the root's series in the sūra has five links** (39, 44, 64, 89, 92) — **all five from the root ب-ي-ن, and all five tying the work of *making clear* to a different subject or to a different time.**

---

## 16/65 — وَٱللَّهُ أَنزَلَ مِنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ مَآءً فَأَحْيَا بِهِ ٱلْأَرْضَ بَعْدَ مَوْتِهَآ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يَسْمَعُونَ

*Wa'llāhu anzala mina'l-samāʾi māʾan fa-aḥyā bihi'l-arḍa baʿda mawtihā, inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin yasmaʿūn*

"**God sent down water from the sky and with it revived the earth after its death.** In that there is a sign for a people who hear."

### The second enumeration begins

**And the opening is a repetition of an item from the first enumeration: the tenth verse too said *anzala mina'l-samāʾi māʾan*.**

**The difference must be recorded:**

| | **Verse 10** | **Verse 65** |
|---|---|---|
| The work of the water | *Lakum minhu **sharāb*** — drink, pasture | *Fa-**aḥyā** bihi'l-arḍa baʿda mawtihā* — **reviving** |
| The addressee | *Lakum* — **for you** | **no** *lakum* |
| The closing | (none — the series goes on) | *Li-qawmin **yasmaʿūn*** |

**I record this as my own reading; its ground is the difference between the two verses:** in the first enumeration the water was named as a **benefit**; in the second as a **proof.** **The same fact is thus enumerated twice, and in two different tasks.**

**And the proof from *iḥyāʾ baʿda'l-mawt* was treated at many places in this commentary:** **`035-fatir.md` 35/9 (*ka-dhālika'l-nushūr*), `050-kaf.md` 50/11 (*ka-dhālika'l-khurūj*), `041-fussilet.md` 41/39, `030-rum.md` 30/50.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here must be recorded:** in al-Naḥl **no link to the resurrection is added** to this proof. Fāṭir says *ka-dhālika'l-nushūr*, Qāf says *ka-dhālika'l-khurūj*; **al-Naḥl says only *la-āyatan li-qawmin yasmaʿūn*.**

**I give this as an observation:** here the sūra ties the proof not to resurrection but to **hearing.** **And this fits the series of the sūra's closing verbs — the fifth verb enters here.**

---

## 16/66 — وَإِنَّ لَكُمْ فِى ٱلْأَنْعَٰمِ لَعِبْرَةً نُّسْقِيكُم مِّمَّا فِى بُطُونِهِۦ مِنۢ بَيْنِ فَرْثٍ وَدَمٍ لَّبَنًا خَالِصًا سَآئِغًا لِّلشَّٰرِبِينَ

*Wa inna lakum fi'l-anʿāmi la-ʿibra, nusqīkum mimmā fī buṭūnihī min bayni farthin wa damin labanan khāliṣan sāʾighan li'l-shāribīn*

"**In the livestock too there is a lesson for you: from what is in their bellies, from between the *farth* and the blood, We give you pure milk to drink, going down easily for those who drink it.**"

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`), to be entered at the outset

**This verse is one of the places most often used for claims of scientific miracle. This commentary does not take that road.**

**The reason is an explicit clause of the method:** *"No hunting for scientific miracles. No modern knowledge is forced onto a verse."*

**And I add two practical reasons, the same as those written at `054-kamer.md`, 54/1:**

**One.** The words of the verse were chosen not as the description of a physiology but as **the description of a kitchen.** It is shown below: the words are not the laboratory's but those of a man who slaughters an animal and opens its belly.

**Two**, and more important: **if you tie the truth of a text to a current scientific interpretation, then when the interpretation changes you have brought the text down along with it.** And the verse's own sentence does not ask for this — what it says is given below, in its own words.

### عِبْرَة — root: ع-ب-ر

**The concrete sense of the root: to cross from one bank to the other.** *ʿUbūr* — a crossing; *maʿbar* — a ford; *ʿibāra* — the speech that carries a meaning from one mind to another; *taʿbīr* (the interpretation of dreams) — to pass from the image to the meaning.

**The choice of the word must be recorded, and it shows what the verse is doing:** the verse does not say *āya* (sign), it says ***ʿibra***. **What is asked for, then, is a crossing from the visible to the unseen.**

**I give this as my own reading; its ground is the root itself:** the word names not a place to stay but **a place to cross.** **And all the detail of the verse — the *farth*, the blood, the milk — are the steps of that crossing, not its destination.**

### مِنۢ بَيْنِ فَرْثٍ وَدَمٍ

**فَرْث** — root **ف-ر-ث**: the sense the lexicographers give is **the digested fodder in an animal's stomach.** The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.

**And the construction must be recorded: *min bayni farthin wa dam* — "from between the *farth* and the blood".**

**I give this as an observation:** the verse enumerates three things in succession and gives the middle one a position. **The words are what could be seen in the belly of a slaughtered animal** — the fodder in the stomach, the blood, and the milk. **The description thus stands at the edge of what can be observed.**

**And there is no claim about a mechanism beyond that in the verse.** The verse gives no name of an organ and describes no process. **All it says is where the thing that comes out comes from.**

### خَالِصًا — root: خ-ل-ص

**The concrete sense of the root: a thing's separating out of a mixture and becoming pure.** *Khāliṣ* — unmixed; *ikhlāṣ* — the making of religion unmixed. **The root was the backbone of `039-zumer.md` and was treated there in detail;** I do not repeat it here.

**And the work of the word here must be recorded:** the verse enumerated two mixed things (the *farth* and the blood), then names what comes out ***khāliṣ***. **The word is thus placed not as a statement of chemistry but as the counterpart of the two words before it.**

**I give this as my own reading, and its ground is the order of the three words:** the *farth* and the *dam* are mixed; the *laban* is **unmixed.** What the sentence does is to say that a separation takes place in between — not how it takes place.

### سَآئِغًا — root: س-و-غ

**The word was treated at `035-fatir.md`, 35/12, and I rely on that treatment.** What was recorded there was this:

> **"سَآئِغ — root س-و-غ: to pass easily down the throat. The word describes the drinkability of water by way of *ease of swallowing* — that is, not a chemical description but a bodily experience."**

**And this note holds exactly for this verse too. The two occurrences can be set side by side:**

| Place | What *sāʾigh* qualifies | Is its opposite present in the verse |
|---|---|---|
| **Fāṭir 35/12** | *ʿAdhbun furātun **sāʾighun** sharābuh* — **sweet water** | **Yes** — *milḥun ujāj* (salt, bitter) |
| **al-Naḥl 16/66** | *Labanan khāliṣan **sāʾighan** li'l-shāribīn* | **No** — no opposite is set |

**The difference must be recorded, and I give it as an observation:** in Fāṭir the word was part of a **comparison**; **in al-Naḥl it stands alone.** **And al-Naḥl adds a qualification: *li'l-shāribīn* — "for those who drink".** The ease is described from the drinker's side.

**The principle recorded in the Fāṭir section holds here too, and I repeat it: the verse's description is not chemical but bodily.** **This is linguistic evidence enough not to turn the verse into a text of physiology.**

---

## 16/67 — وَمِن ثَمَرَٰتِ ٱلنَّخِيلِ وَٱلْأَعْنَٰبِ تَتَّخِذُونَ مِنْهُ سَكَرًا وَرِزْقًا حَسَنًا إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يَعْقِلُونَ

*Wa min thamarāti'l-nakhīli wa'l-aʿnābi tattakhidhūna minhu sakaran wa rizqan ḥasanā, inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin yaʿqilūn*

"**And from the fruits of the date palm and the vine you take both *sakar* and a good provision.** In that there is a sign for a people who reason."

### سَكَر — the sense of the word, and the disagreement

**Root: س-ك-ر.** **The lexicographers give the concrete sense of the root thus: to cut off the flow of a thing, to block it.** *Sakara'l-nahra* — he dammed the river; **from this, *sakr* — the blocking of the mind's way, drunkenness.** **The same picture in the root is seen elsewhere in the Qurʾān as well: *sukkirat abṣārunā* — "our eyes have been sealed over" (al-Ḥijr 15/15).**

**There is disagreement over the sense of the word here, and it must be set out in a table:**

| View | What *sakar* is | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **An intoxicating drink** | The commonest sense of the root; and the verse's setting it against *rizqan ḥasanā* |
| 2 | **Processed products such as vinegar, syrup, stewed fruit** | Some lexicographers record *sakar* in the sense "what is made from fruit" |
| 3 | **What is eaten, what is made use of** | It is transmitted that in some dialects *sakar* has the sense "food" |

**All three views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what must be recorded in point of word order is this:** the verse separates two things with a *wāw*: *sakaran* **and** *rizqan ḥasanā.* **The two are therefore not the same thing.** **And only the second is given an adjective: *ḥasan* (good).**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is this asymmetry of adjectives:** that no adjective is given to the first shows that the sentence sets no value upon it. **The second is expressly described as "good".** **The sentence enumerates two products but does not put them on a level.**

**No legal ruling is issued.** In the classical sources the relation of this verse to the ruling on wine has been debated at length; **this commentary does not enter that debate.** **What the verse does in its wording is to separate two products and to call only one of them "good".**

### A repetition within the sūra

**In the eleventh verse *al-nakhīl* and *al-aʿnāb* had already been enumerated — in their raw state.** **In the sixty-seventh the same pair comes back with its processed product.**

| Verse | Expression | Its state |
|---|---|---|
| **11** | *Yunbitu lakum bihi'l-zarʿa wa'l-zaytūna wa'l-**nakhīla** wa'l-**aʿnāb*** | **Brought forth** — in nature |
| **67** | *Wa min thamarāti'l-**nakhīli** wa'l-**aʿnābi** tattakhidhūna minh* | **Processed** — by human hands |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, its ground being the change of verb:** in the eleventh verse the verb is *yunbitu* — **God brings forth.** In the sixty-seventh, *tattakhidhūna* — **you take.** **The second enumeration thus takes the human hand into the list as well.**

**And this also shows how an item of the enumeration can be misused:** two things come out of the same fruit, and the two are not equal. **The enumeration records how what is given is used as well.**

---

## 16/68-69 — وَأَوْحَىٰ رَبُّكَ إِلَى ٱلنَّحْلِ أَنِ ٱتَّخِذِى مِنَ ٱلْجِبَالِ بُيُوتًا وَمِنَ ٱلشَّجَرِ وَمِمَّا يَعْرِشُونَ · ثُمَّ كُلِى مِن كُلِّ ٱلثَّمَرَٰتِ فَٱسْلُكِى سُبُلَ رَبِّكِ ذُلُلًا يَخْرُجُ مِنۢ بُطُونِهَا شَرَابٌ مُّخْتَلِفٌ أَلْوَٰنُهُۥ فِيهِ شِفَآءٌ لِّلنَّاسِ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَةً لِّقَوْمٍ يَتَفَكَّرُونَ

*Wa awḥā rabbuka ila'l-naḥli ani'ttakhidhī mina'l-jibāli buyūtan wa mina'l-shajari wa mimmā yaʿrishūn · Thumma kulī min kulli'l-thamarāti fa'slukī subula rabbiki dhululā, yakhruju min buṭūnihā sharābun mukhtalifun alwānuhū fīhi shifāʾun li'l-nās, inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin yatafakkarūn*

"**Your Lord revealed to the honey bee: 'Take houses in the mountains, in the trees, and in the trellises men put up.** · **Then eat of every kind of fruit, and enter the ways of your Lord in submission.' From their bellies comes a drink of differing colours, in which there is healing for people.** In that there is a sign for a people who think."

### وَأَوْحَىٰ رَبُّكَ إِلَى ٱلنَّحْلِ — the word *waḥy* used of the bee

**This is where the sūra takes its name, and I treat this verse in detail, because the use of the word gives direct information about the Qurʾān's vocabulary.**

**Root: و-ح-ي.** **The root was analysed in detail at `053-necm.md`, 53/4, and the same analysis was relied upon at `042-sura.md`, 42/3. I rely on that treatment and bring its definition over here:**

> **"The concrete sense of the root: swift and hidden communication. The definition the lexicographers give joins two features of the word — *speed* and *concealment*. To convey by a sign, by a whisper, by the short way."**
>
> **"There is no 'sacredness' at the centre of the word; there is speed and concealment."** (The same note was entered in `099-zilzal.md`.)

**And in the al-Najm section the field of the root's use was tabulated, and this verse was already named there. I complete the table from here:**

| Place | To whom / from whom | How the *waḥy* takes place |
|---|---|---|
| **Maryam 19/11** | Zechariah (Zakariyyā) → his people | **Conveying by a sign** — in the days when he could not speak |
| **al-Naḥl 16/68** | God → **the bee** | **A creature's knowing how it is to behave** |
| **al-Anʿām 6/112** | Satans → one another | **The whispering of gilded speech** |
| **al-Shūrā 42/51** | God → man | **Three channels: revelation, from behind a veil, a messenger** |
| **al-Najm 53/4** | God → the Prophet | *Waḥyun yūḥā* — verbal noun + verb, reinforced |

**The conclusion recorded in the al-Najm section can be carried straight over here, and I repeat it:** **these usages show that the root does not carry the sense "prophethood". The root names only the manner of the communication: swift, direct, without an intermediary, most often by an unseen route.**

**Now I add what belongs here, and I record it as my own reading, its ground being the primary sense of the root:**

**The two features of the root — *speed* and *concealment* — are exactly met in the case of the bee.**

| The feature of the root | Its counterpart in the bee |
|---|---|
| **Concealment** | The communication itself is invisible. **What** the bee knows is seen; **how** it knows is not |
| **Speed** | No process of learning, no trial and error is observed in between. The behaviour is there ready-made |
| **Directness** | No teacher, no book, no messenger comes to the bee |

**The word, then, is used not in a figure but in its own primary sense.** **And this is a fact about language that must be recorded:** because in English the word "revelation" is used only in the setting of prophecy, this verse reads oddly in English. **In Arabic it does not** — for the word names the **character** of the communication, not the one it is addressed to.

**And I go no further than this: the verse names where the bee's knowledge comes from; it does not describe how it works.**

### أَنِ ٱتَّخِذِى — the wording of the commands

**And there is a striking feature in the wording: the imperatives are in the feminine singular.**

| Command | Form | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| *Ittakhidhī* | **Feminine singular imperative** | Take |
| *Kulī* | **Feminine singular imperative** | Eat |
| *Uslukī* | **Feminine singular imperative** | Enter, travel |

***al-Naḥl* is a generic noun in Arabic that may be treated as masculine or as feminine; the verse chooses the feminine inflection.** The lexicographers put this down to the word's being a generic noun and to the prevalence of the feminine usage.

**And the verb's pronoun is singular, but the next sentence passes into the plural:** *yakhruju min **buṭūnihā*** — "from **their** bellies". **The lexicographers explain this by the distinction between the form and the sense of a generic noun.**

**And one further note: the order of the commands builds an order of life.**

| Order | Command | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Ittakhidhī … buyūtā* | **Shelter** — the house first |
| 2 | *Kulī min kulli'l-thamarāt* | **Feeding** |
| 3 | *Uslukī subula rabbiki dhululā* | **The road** — the return |
| — | *Yakhruju min buṭūnihā sharāb* | **The outcome** — not a command but a statement |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** three commands are given, and the fourth sentence is not a command. **The coming forth of the honey is not commanded — it is announced as the outcome of the three commands.**

### مِمَّا يَعْرِشُونَ — what man builds

**عَرْش**, root **ع-ر-ش**: **to raise, to put up a roof, to build a trellis.** *ʿArsh* — a raised throne; *ʿarīsh* — a vine trellis.

**And the pronoun must be recorded:** *yaʿrishūn* — **plural, third person, human beings.** **The third kind of house is thus what men build.**

**I give this as an observation:** the verse enumerates three shelters — mountain, tree, **and human construction.** **Man is not outside the order the verse sets out; he is named as an item within it.**

### فَٱسْلُكِى سُبُلَ رَبِّكِ ذُلُلًا

**سَلَكَ** — root **س-ل-ك**: **to enter upon a way, to be threaded into something.** *Silk* — a string, to thread on a string.

**ذُلُلًا** — root **ذ-ل-ل**: **to submit, to be docile.** The plural of *dhalūl*; **it is used of a tame, broken-in animal** — *dābbatun dhalūl*, a beast easy to ride.

**Two readings of the word's case are transmitted, and the disagreement must be recorded:**

| Reading | Whose state *dhululan* describes | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The state of the ***subul*** | **The ways are made submissive** — smoothed for the bee |
| 2 | **The state of the bee** | **The bee submitting** — it enters in obedience |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And a connection can be made with `049-hucurat.md`, and I give it as an observation:** *dākhirūn* (bowed low) occurred in the forty-eighth verse — of the shadows and of those who prostrate. **Here *dhulul* occurs — of the bee.** **Both words carry the same idea, and the sūra sets them twenty verses apart.**

### شَرَابٌ مُّخْتَلِفٌ أَلْوَٰنُهُۥ — the fourth link

**The construction *mukhtalifun alwānuhū* occurs for the second time in the sūra** (in the thirteenth verse *wa mā dharaʾa lakum fi'l-arḍi mukhtalifan alwānuh* occurred). **And at `035-fatir.md`, 35/27-28, it had been repeated three times.**

**I complete the table as the fourth link:**

| # | Place | Expression | Whose colours |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fāṭir 35/27 | *Thamarātin mukhtalifan alwānuhā* | **Plant** |
| 2 | Fāṭir 35/27 | *Judadun bīḍun wa ḥumrun mukhtalifun alwānuhā* | **Stone** |
| 3 | Fāṭir 35/28 | *Mina'l-nāsi wa'l-dawābbi wa'l-anʿāmi mukhtalifun alwānuhū* | **Living creature** |
| **4** | **al-Naḥl 16/13** | *Wa mā dharaʾa lakum fi'l-arḍi mukhtalifan alwānuh* | **Everything strewn on the earth** |
| **5** | **al-Naḥl 16/69** | *Sharābun mukhtalifun alwānuhū* | ***What a living creature produces*** |

**And the fifth link stands apart from the rest. I record this as my own reading, its ground being the list itself:** in the first four links the diversity is **in the created thing itself** — fruit, stone, living creature, what is strewn on the earth. **In the fifth link the diversity is in what a living creature *produces*.**

**The construction thus passes from direct creation to an intermediary.** **And the conclusion recorded at `035-fatir.md`, 35/28 — three distinct domains, one shared attribute: diversity — goes one step further here: in products as well.**

**And in Fāṭir this threesome was followed by *innamā yakhsha'llāha min ʿibādihi'l-ʿulamāʾ* — the conclusion of a list of observations.** **In al-Naḥl what follows is *li-qawmin yatafakkarūn*.** **The two sūras tie the same fact to two different capacities: the one to knowing, the other to thinking.**

### فِيهِ شِفَآءٌ لِّلنَّاسِ

**Care is needed here, and per the method (`USLUP.md`) the limit must be set openly.**
