**شِفَآء** — root **ش-ف-ي**: **to be healed; and to come to the edge, to the brink of a thing.** *Shafā* — an edge, a brink (in the Qurʾān, *shafā ḥufratin* — the brink of a pit). **The lexicographers join the two branches:** *shifāʾ* is to come to the **edge** of an illness and to pass beyond it.

**The wording of the verse is this, and nothing is added to it: *fīhi shifāʾun li'l-nās* — "in it there is healing for people."**

**Three things must be recorded in the wording, and all three can be verified from the text:**

| What | Its warrant in the verse | What it does not say |
|---|---|---|
| ***Fīhi*** — "in it" | Healing is named as something **inside** the drink | It does not say "it is a healing" |
| ***Shifāʾun*** — **indefinite** | Not the healing, but a healing | It does not say "a healing for every disease" |
| ***Li'l-nās*** — for people | The scope is mankind | Which disease, in what measure — **is not said** |

**These three notes are the textual barrier against turning the verse into a medical claim.** **The sentence is not a prescription; it is a nominal sentence with an indefinite noun.**

**And the classical exegetes too have differed over the scope of this expression. The disagreement must be set out in a table:**

| View | What the pronoun *fīhi* refers to | The scope of the *shifāʾ* |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Honey** (*sharāb*) | For certain diseases — the indefinite noun shows this |
| 2 | **Honey** | A general attribute of healing |
| 3 | **The Qurʾān** — or the whole account in the preceding sentence | A healing of the heart |

**The third view is a minority one and is weak in point of word order** — the nearest referent is *sharāb*. **But it is transmitted, and I record it. I do not impose a preference.**

**And no medical ruling is issued in this commentary.** The wording of the verse says that there is a benefit in a substance; **for which disease, in what measure, on what condition — none of this is in the verse, and none of it is written here.**

**As my own reading I record only this, its ground being the closing of the verse:** having said *fīhi shifāʾun li'l-nās*, the sentence does not stop — *inna fī dhālika la-āyatan li-qawmin **yatafakkarūn***. **The verse itself turns the attention from the honey to thinking.** **And the word used in the sixty-sixth verse should be recalled: *ʿibra* — the place where one crosses.**

### On the name of the sūra

**The sūra takes its name from this verse, and this is worth recording.**

**I give this as an observation:** the sūra has a hundred and twenty-eight verses, and within it are prophetic narratives, rulings, parables, history. **It takes its name from a passage about a bee two verses long.**

**And that passage stands in the very middle of the sūra's enumeration of favours.** **The naming thus points to the sūra's backbone: an item from within the enumeration has become the name of the whole.**

---

## 16/70 — وَٱللَّهُ خَلَقَكُمْ ثُمَّ يَتَوَفَّىٰكُمْ وَمِنكُم مَّن يُرَدُّ إِلَىٰٓ أَرْذَلِ ٱلْعُمُرِ لِكَىْ لَا يَعْلَمَ بَعْدَ عِلْمٍ شَيْـًٔا إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلِيمٌ قَدِيرٌ

*Wa'llāhu khalaqakum thumma yatawaffākum, wa minkum man yuraddu ilā ardhali'l-ʿumuri li-kay lā yaʿlama baʿda ʿilmin shayʾā, inna'llāha ʿalīmun qadīr*

"**God created you, then He takes you. And some of you are sent back to the most abject stage of life, so that after knowing they should know nothing.** God is knowing, all-powerful."

### أَرْذَلِ ٱلْعُمُر

**The root ر-ذ-ل was treated at `026-suara.md`, 26/111** (*wa'ttabaʿaka'l-ardhalūn*): **what is worthless, low, of no use in a thing; *ardhal* — the elative: the lowest.** **And this verse was already named there.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And at `095-tin.md`, 95/5 (*thumma radadnāhu asfala sāfilīn*), this verse was used in a table of disagreement as evidence for one of the readings:** the sense of the verb *radda* as "turning back, returning" and its use in this verse were recorded there. **I rely on that treatment.**

**What belongs here is that the sentence is built on the axis of *knowledge*, and this must be recorded:**

***Li-kay lā yaʿlama baʿda ʿilmin shayʾā*** — "so that **after knowing** he should know nothing."

**Decline, then, is described not as a weakness of the body but as *the taking back of knowledge*.**

**And this has a counterpart within the sūra. The seventy-eighth verse will build the same axis from the other end:** *wa'llāhu akhrajakum min buṭūni ummahātikum **lā taʿlamūna shayʾā***.

| Verse | Expression | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **70** | *Lā yaʿlama **baʿda ʿilmin** shayʾā* | **From knowledge to unknowing** — at the end of life |
| **78** | *Lā taʿlamūna shayʾā* | **From unknowing, the beginning** — at the start of life |

**This is an overlap verifiable within the sūra, and eight verses stand between the two.** **I record this as my own reading:** the sūra bounds a human life at both ends with the same word (*shayʾā* — nothing at all). **At the beginning nothing is known; at the end what was known is taken back.** **And what is given in between will be enumerated in the seventy-eighth verse: hearing, eyes, hearts.**

**At `030-rum.md`, 30/54, the three stages of life (weakness → strength → weakness and grey hair) were treated and compared with the six steps at `040-gafir.md`, 40/67.** **I rely on that treatment and record the difference here:** al-Rūm and Ghāfir measure the stages **by the body**; **al-Naḥl by knowledge.**

---

## 16/71 — وَٱللَّهُ فَضَّلَ بَعْضَكُمْ عَلَىٰ بَعْضٍ فِى ٱلرِّزْقِ فَمَا ٱلَّذِينَ فُضِّلُوا۟ بِرَآدِّى رِزْقِهِمْ عَلَىٰ مَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَٰنُهُمْ فَهُمْ فِيهِ سَوَآءٌ أَفَبِنِعْمَةِ ٱللَّهِ يَجْحَدُونَ

*Wa'llāhu faḍḍala baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍin fi'l-rizq, fa-ma'lladhīna fuḍḍilū bi-rāddī rizqihim ʿalā mā malakat aymānuhum fa-hum fīhi sawāʾ, a-fa-bi-niʿmati'llāhi yajḥadūn*

"**God has favoured some of you over others in provision. Those who are favoured do not hand their provision over to those their right hands possess, so as to be their equals in it. Do they then knowingly deny God's favour?**"

### How the parable is built

**The verse gives an example, and the example is taken from the addressee's own life.**

**And its logic must be recorded, because it is indirect:**

| Step | Expression | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Faḍḍala baʿḍakum ʿalā baʿḍin fi'l-rizq* | **The fact:** provision is not distributed equally |
| 2 | *Fa-ma'lladhīna fuḍḍilū bi-rāddī rizqihim…* | **The observation:** the favoured man does not share his advantage and level himself with the others |
| 3 | *A-fa-bi-niʿmati'llāhi yajḥadūn* | **The question** |

**The exegetes dwell on the work of the second step, and two readings are transmitted:**

| Reading | The work of the parable |
|---|---|
| 1 | **The contradiction of associating partners:** the man who accepts no partner in his own property accepts partners in God's dominion |
| 2 | **The denial of the favour:** the advantage given is not shared, and the giver is met with ingratitude besides |

**Both readings are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**The first reading has a ground outside the verse, and it must be recorded:** **at `030-rum.md`, 30/28, a parable of exactly the same logic occurs** (*hal lakum min mā malakat aymānukum min shurakāʾa fī mā razaqnākum fa-antum fīhi sawāʾ*) **and was treated there.** **The two verses use the same words: *mā malakat aymānukum*, *fī mā razaqnākum*, *fa-antum fīhi sawāʾ*.** **This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

**The difference must be recorded:**

| | **al-Rūm 30/28** | **al-Naḥl 16/71** |
|---|---|---|
| The kind of sentence | ***A question*** — *hal lakum … min shurakāʾa* | **A statement** — *fa-mā … bi-rāddī* |
| What is asked | **Would you accept partners?** | (no question is put; a fact is announced) |
| The closing | *Ka-dhālika nufaṣṣilu'l-āyāti li-qawmin **yaʿqilūn*** | *A-fa-bi-niʿmati'llāhi **yajḥadūn*** |

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the difference of the closings:** al-Rūm ends the example with a call to **reason**; **al-Naḥl ties the same example to an accounting of favour.** **The sūra draws a shared parable onto its own backbone.**

### يَجْحَدُونَ — root: ج-ح-د

**The root was treated at `046-ahkaf.md`, 46/26, and the connection the lexicographers record was given there: *arḍun juḥād* — barren, arid ground.** **Denial is thus described not as ignorance but as a ground that does not give back what it has taken.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**And I record that the word falls very much in its place here:** *juḥūd* is denial **against knowledge.** **And the subject of the verse is the denial of the source of an advantage that is known.** **The eighty-third verse will say this outright: *yaʿrifūna niʿmata'llāhi thumma yunkirūnahā* — "they recognise it, then they deny it."**

---

## 16/72 — وَٱللَّهُ جَعَلَ لَكُم مِّنْ أَنفُسِكُمْ أَزْوَٰجًا وَجَعَلَ لَكُم مِّنْ أَزْوَٰجِكُم بَنِينَ وَحَفَدَةً وَرَزَقَكُم مِّنَ ٱلطَّيِّبَٰتِ أَفَبِٱلْبَٰطِلِ يُؤْمِنُونَ وَبِنِعْمَتِ ٱللَّهِ هُمْ يَكْفُرُونَ

*Wa'llāhu jaʿala lakum min anfusikum azwājan wa jaʿala lakum min azwājikum banīna wa ḥafadatan wa razaqakum mina'l-ṭayyibāt, a-fa-bi'l-bāṭili yuʾminūna wa bi-niʿmati'llāhi hum yakfurūn*

"**God made for you spouses of your own kind, and from your spouses He gave you sons and grandsons, and He provided you with wholesome things. Do they then believe in what is false and deny God's favour?**"

### مِّنْ أَنفُسِكُمْ

**The construction was treated at `030-rum.md`, 30/21** (*an khalaqa lakum min anfusikum azwājan li-taskunū ilayhā*), **and the disagreement was tabulated there: "of the same kind, or of the same stock".** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference that belongs here must be recorded, and the two verses do not arrive at the same place:**

| | **al-Rūm 30/21** | **al-Naḥl 16/72** |
|---|---|---|
| What follows | *Li-taskunū ilayhā wa **jaʿala baynakum mawaddatan wa raḥma*** | *Wa jaʿala lakum min azwājikum **banīna wa ḥafada*** |
| What it is tied to | **The character of the relation** — rest, love, mercy | **The continuation of the line** — children, grandchildren |
| The closing | *La-āyātin li-qawmin **yatafakkarūn*** | *A-fa-bi'l-bāṭili **yuʾminūn*** |

**I record this as an observation:** al-Rūm names the pairing by way of **an inward state**; **al-Naḥl by way of a *continuation*.** **The two verses take hold of the same fact at two different points, and both are enumerated as favours.**

### حَفَدَة — root: ح-ف-د

**The concrete sense of the root: to serve swiftly, to bustle about, to hurry to another's help.** *Ḥafada* — he was quick in service.

**There is disagreement over the sense of the word, and it must be set out in a table:**

| View | Who the *ḥafada* are | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Grandchildren** | That it comes after *banīn*; the order of the generations |
| 2 | **Those who serve, helpers** | The primary sense of the root — service performed at a run |
| 3 | **Sons-in-law / a wife's earlier children** | A usage transmitted by some lexicographers |

**All three views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the sense of the root lends the second view a certain appeal — but the first is the more established in point of word order.** **I give this as an observation, not as a preference.**

### أَفَبِٱلْبَٰطِلِ يُؤْمِنُونَ

**And the question is built upon a reversal:**

| Verb | Its object |
|---|---|
| *Yuʾminūn* — **to believe** | *Bi'l-**bāṭil*** — in what is false |
| *Yakfurūn* — **to deny** | *Bi-niʿmati'**llāh*** — God's favour |

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the two verbs have changed places. **The verb of believing is turned towards what is empty, the verb of denying towards what is real.** **The sentence does not state this separately; it shows it by the arrangement.**

**بَٰطِل** — root **ب-ط-ل**: **to come to nothing, to be void, to vanish.** The lexicographers record the root's sense of "what is not fixed, what cannot keep its footing".

---

## 16/73-74 — وَيَعْبُدُونَ مِن دُونِ ٱللَّهِ مَا لَا يَمْلِكُ لَهُمْ رِزْقًا مِّنَ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ شَيْـًٔا وَلَا يَسْتَطِيعُونَ · فَلَا تَضْرِبُوا۟ لِلَّهِ ٱلْأَمْثَالَ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَعْلَمُ وَأَنتُمْ لَا تَعْلَمُونَ

*Wa yaʿbudūna min dūni'llāhi mā lā yamliku lahum rizqan mina'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi shayʾan wa lā yastaṭīʿūn · Fa-lā taḍribū li'llāhi'l-amthāl, inna'llāha yaʿlamu wa antum lā taʿlamūn*

"**And they serve, besides God, things that hold no provision for them from the heavens or the earth, and have no power to.** · **So do not coin likenesses for God. God knows, and you do not know.**"

### فَلَا تَضْرِبُوا۟ لِلَّهِ ٱلْأَمْثَال — and the parables two verses later

**This verse must be read with care, because immediately afterwards the sūra will give two parables (75-76).**

**The contradiction is only apparent, and its resolution lies in one word: *li'llāh* — "for God".**

**What is forbidden is not the making of parables; it is the coining of likenesses for God** — that is, the attempt to describe Him by a comparison, to fit Him into a mould.

**And the evidence for this is what comes two verses later: *ḍaraba'llāhu mathalan* — "God has coined a likeness."** **Coining a likeness is not forbidden; coining one *about God* is.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that these verses stand back to back:** the verse marks in advance by whom the parables of the seventy-fifth and seventy-sixth verses are framed. **When the agent changes, the ruling changes too.**

**The same principle was recorded at `030-rum.md`, 30/27** (*wa lahu'l-mathalu'l-aʿlā fi'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ*), **and the same construction occurred in the sixtieth verse of this sūra** (*wa li'llāhi'l-mathalu'l-aʿlā*). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/75 — ضَرَبَ ٱللَّهُ مَثَلًا عَبْدًا مَّمْلُوكًا لَّا يَقْدِرُ عَلَىٰ شَىْءٍ وَمَن رَّزَقْنَٰهُ مِنَّا رِزْقًا حَسَنًا فَهُوَ يُنفِقُ مِنْهُ سِرًّا وَجَهْرًا هَلْ يَسْتَوُۥنَ ٱلْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ بَلْ أَكْثَرُهُمْ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ

*Ḍaraba'llāhu mathalan ʿabdan mamlūkan lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾin wa man razaqnāhu minnā rizqan ḥasanan fa-huwa yunfiqu minhu sirran wa jahrā, hal yastawūn, al-ḥamdu li'llāh, bal aktharuhum lā yaʿlamūn*

"**God has coined a likeness: a slave owned by another, with power over nothing; and a man to whom We have given a good provision from Ourselves, and who spends of it in secret and openly. Are they equal?** Praise belongs to God. No, most of them do not know."

### How the parable is built

**The example is taken from the life of that day, and it has two sides:**

| Side | Attribute | His act |
|---|---|---|
| *ʿAbdan **mamlūkā*** | **Owned by another** | *Lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾ* — **he has power over nothing** |
| *Man razaqnāhu … rizqan **ḥasanā*** | **Given to him** | *Yunfiqu minhu sirran wa jahrā* — **he spends** |

**And at the axis of the contrast there is one word: *power*.** The first *lā yaqdiru*, the second is in fact spending. **The comparison is thus built not on ownership but on the power to dispose.**

**سِرًّا وَجَهْرًا** — **at `002-bakara.md`, 2/271, it was treated that both secret and open giving are counted lawful, and at `035-fatir.md`, 35/29, it was recorded that the two are named with a *wāw*, no distinction being drawn.** **I rely on those treatments.** **Here too they are named together with a *wāw*.**

---

## 16/76 — وَضَرَبَ ٱللَّهُ مَثَلًا رَّجُلَيْنِ أَحَدُهُمَآ أَبْكَمُ لَا يَقْدِرُ عَلَىٰ شَىْءٍ وَهُوَ كَلٌّ عَلَىٰ مَوْلَىٰهُ أَيْنَمَا يُوَجِّههُّ لَا يَأْتِ بِخَيْرٍ هَلْ يَسْتَوِى هُوَ وَمَن يَأْمُرُ بِٱلْعَدْلِ وَهُوَ عَلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ

*Wa ḍaraba'llāhu mathalan rajulayni aḥaduhumā abkamu lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾin wa huwa kallun ʿalā mawlāhu, aynamā yuwajjihhu lā yaʾti bi-khayr, hal yastawī huwa wa man yaʾmuru bi'l-ʿadli wa huwa ʿalā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm*

"**God has coined another likeness: two men. One of them is mute and has power over nothing; he is a burden to his master, and wherever he is sent he brings back nothing good. Is he equal to one who enjoins justice and is himself upon a straight path?**"

### The relation between the two parables

**In two verses the sūra gives two parables, and both open with the same verb: *ḍaraba'llāhu mathalan*.**

**And at `039-zumer.md`, 39/29, there was a paired parable too — two slaves. The three must be compared:**

| | **al-Zumar 39/29** | **al-Naḥl 16/75** | **al-Naḥl 16/76** |
|---|---|---|---|
| The two sides | *Rajulan fīhi shurakāʾu mutafākisūn* / *wa rajulan salaman li-rajul* | *ʿAbdan mamlūkā* / *man razaqnāh* | *Abkam* / *man yaʾmuru bi'l-ʿadl* |
| The axis of distinction | **How many owners** — many / one | **Power of disposal** — absent / present | **Speech and use** — absent / present |
| The shared question | *Hal yastawiyāni **mathalā*** | *Hal **yastawūn*** | *Hal **yastawī*** |
| What it tells | **The practical consequence of associating partners** — confusion of direction | **The use made of what is given** | **What is given turning towards another** |
| Whom it looks at | **The servant** — whose condition is the better | **The servant** | **The servant and those around him** |

**What was recorded in the al-Zumar section was this, and I take it as ground here:** *"The verse does not argue the theoretical falsity of plurality — it shows its practical consequence. A man with more than one owner cannot know whose measure to act by."* **The matter is thus taken out of a dispute about belief and made a question of *direction*.**

**al-Naḥl's two parables use the same method but arrive somewhere else. I record this as my own reading, and my ground is the axis of distinction in the three parables:**

**In al-Zumar the measure is *whom you belong to*. In al-Naḥl the measure is *what you are able to do*.**

**And al-Naḥl's two parables complete each other:**

| | **The first parable (75)** | **The second parable (76)** |
|---|---|---|
| The condition of the deficient side | *Lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾ* — **no power** | *Lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾ* — **the same expression** |
| The defect added | (none) | ***Abkam*** — mute; ***kallun ʿalā mawlāh*** — a burden |
| The act of the other side | *Yunfiqu* — **he spends** | *Yaʾmuru bi'l-ʿadl* — **he enjoins justice** |
| The attribute of the other side | *Rizqan ḥasanā* — **what has been given him** | *ʿAlā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm* — **the road he stands on** |

**The shared expression must be recorded: *lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾ* occurs in both parables.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**And the difference is this: in the first parable the other side *gives* (property), in the second he *speaks* (justice).** **In two verses the sūra thus enumerates two kinds of benefit — the material and the spoken.**

**And the closing of the second parable prepares the ninetieth verse:** *wa man yaʾmuru bi'l-**ʿadl***. **The ninetieth will open with the same word:** *inna'llāha yaʾmuru bi'l-**ʿadli** wa'l-iḥsān*. **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and it will be treated at that verse.**

### أَبْكَم — root: ب-ك-م

**The sense of the root: dumbness; and there is a distinction the lexicographers record.**

| Word | Root | The difference |
|---|---|---|
| **أَخْرَس (akhras)** | خ-ر-س | **Mute from birth** — the organ of speech is wanting |
| **أَبْكَم (abkam)** | ب-ك-م | The lexicographers mostly use it of **one who cannot speak, or does not know how to speak**; some say "both mute and without understanding" |

**I transmit this distinction as a tendency the lexicographers record, not as a settled ruling.**

**كَلّ** — root **ك-ل-ل**: **to grow weary, to grow blunt; and weight, a burden.** *Kalīl* — blunt (of a knife), weary (of an eye). **Here the word means "one who is a burden to another".**

**And the clause *aynamā yuwajjihhu lā yaʾti bi-khayr* must be recorded:** *tawjīh* — **to direct, to send somewhere.** **The verse says that the man can be directed, but that the directing yields no result.**

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`)

**This parable lays down no ruling about a disabled person.** **This must be written openly.**

**The evidence within the text is this:** the parable compares its two sides by **two attributes** — on the one side *lā yaqdiru ʿalā shayʾ* and *lā yaʾti bi-khayr*, on the other *yaʾmuru bi'l-ʿadl*. **And the subject of the comparison is fixed by the context coming from the preceding verse: the condition of the things that are served** (73: *mā lā yamliku lahum rizqan … wa lā yastaṭīʿūn*).

**The object of the parable, then, is not a group of human beings but the position described in the seventy-third verse.** **And the same care was kept at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/18:** there too it was recorded that a sentence built upon an attribute is not a ruling about those who bear that attribute. **The same line is kept here.**

---

## 16/77 — وَلِلَّهِ غَيْبُ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَمَآ أَمْرُ ٱلسَّاعَةِ إِلَّا كَلَمْحِ ٱلْبَصَرِ أَوْ هُوَ أَقْرَبُ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ قَدِيرٌ

*Wa li'llāhi ghaybu'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, wa mā amru'l-sāʿati illā ka-lamḥi'l-baṣari aw huwa aqrab, inna'llāha ʿalā kulli shayʾin qadīr*

"**To God belongs the unseen of the heavens and the earth. And the matter of the Hour is only as the blink of an eye, or nearer still.** God has power over everything."

### كَلَمْحِ ٱلْبَصَرِ

**لَمْح** — root **ل-م-ح**: **to glance quickly, to cast an eye, to flash.** *Lamḥa* — a glance; *lamaḥa'l-barq* — the lightning flashed.

**And the construction can be taken in two ways; the lexicographers record both:** **the blinking of an eye**, or **the time a glance takes.** **Both explanations are transmitted; in point of meaning there is no difference: the shortest span there is.**

**أَوْ هُوَ أَقْرَبُ** — "or nearer still."

**And the word must be recorded: *aqrab* — "nearer", not "shorter".** **The comparison is therefore made not by span of time but by distance.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the word itself:** the sentence measures not the **speed** of the Hour but its **distance.** **And this closes a ring with the sūra's first verse:** *atā amru'llāh* — it has come. **The seventy-seventh verse says the same nearness in another word.**

**The root ق-ر-ب and the form *iqtaraba* were treated at `054-kamer.md`, 54/1, and at `021-enbiya.md`, 21/1;** I rely on those treatments.

### أَمْرُ ٱلسَّاعَة — and the series of the root *amr* in the sūra

**The root أ-م-ر occurs several times in the sūra and in several different tasks, and this is a structural sign:**

| Verse | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Atā **amru**'llāh* | **The command that comes** |
| **2** | *Bi'l-rūḥi min **amrih*** | **The command that descends** |
| **12** | *Musakhkharātun bi-**amrih*** | **The command that holds the order** |
| **33** | *Aw yaʾtiya **amru** rabbik* | **The command that is awaited** |
| **50** | *Wa yafʿalūna mā **yuʾmarūn*** | **The command that is obeyed** |
| **77** | ***Amru**'l-sāʿa* | **The matter of the Hour** |
| **90** | *Inna'llāha **yaʾmuru** bi'l-ʿadl* | **The justice that is commanded** |

**All seven occurrences can be verified from the text. And I record this as my own reading:** in the sūra the root is used on the **cosmic** plane (12), on the **historical** (1, 33, 77) and on the **moral** (50, 90). **"Command" is thus the single word that binds three distinct layers of the sūra together.**

---

## 16/78 — وَٱللَّهُ أَخْرَجَكُم مِّنۢ بُطُونِ أُمَّهَٰتِكُمْ لَا تَعْلَمُونَ شَيْـًٔا وَجَعَلَ لَكُمُ ٱلسَّمْعَ وَٱلْأَبْصَٰرَ وَٱلْأَفْـِٔدَةَ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَشْكُرُونَ

*Wa'llāhu akhrajakum min buṭūni ummahātikum lā taʿlamūna shayʾan wa jaʿala lakumu'l-samʿa wa'l-abṣāra wa'l-afʾida, laʿallakum tashkurūn*

"**God brought you out of the bellies of your mothers knowing nothing, and He gave you hearing, eyes and hearts** — that you might give thanks."

### The triad hearing-eyes-hearts — a new link

**This triad was treated at many places in this commentary, and a table of four links was drawn up at `032-secde.md`, 32/9. I rely on that treatment and widen the table from here:**

| Place | Sentence | The work of the triad |
|---|---|---|
| al-Aḥqāf 46/26 | *Wa jaʿalnā **lahum** samʿan wa abṣāran wa afʾidatan **fa-mā aghnā ʿanhum…*** | **Given — it availed nothing** |
| al-Jāthiya 45/23 | *Wa **khatama** ʿalā samʿihī wa qalbihī wa jaʿala ʿalā baṣarihī ghishāwa* | **Closed off** — a seal and a veil |
| Fuṣṣilat 41/20 | *Shahida ʿalayhim samʿuhum wa abṣāruhum wa **julūduhum*** | **They bore witness** — the third is the *skin* |
| al-Sajda 32/9 | *Wa **jaʿala lakum** … **qalīlan mā tashkurūn*** | **Given — little thanks given** |
| al-Muʾminūn 23/78 | *Wa huwa'lladhī **anshaʾa** lakumu'l-samʿa wa'l-abṣāra wa'l-afʾida* | **Brought into being** — constructed |
| al-Mulk 67/23 | *Qul huwa'lladhī **anshaʾakum** wa jaʿala lakum … qalīlan mā tashkurūn* | **Given — little thanks given** |
| al-Isrāʾ 17/36 | *Inna'l-samʿa wa'l-baṣara wa'l-fuʾāda **kullu ulāʾika kāna ʿanhu masʾūlā*** | **Held to account** |
| **al-Naḥl 16/78** | *Wa jaʿala lakum … **laʿallakum tashkurūn*** | **Given — thanks is hoped for** |

**In `023-muminun.md` this verse was named in the table of the distribution of the root ن-ش-أ within that sūra, and in `017-isra.md` in the table of that sūra's structure;** **and at `067-mulk.md`, 67/23, the question of singular and plural within the triad was treated in detail.** **I rely on those treatments and do not repeat them.**

**The summary of what was recorded in the al-Mulk section is this:** *samʿ* is singular, *abṣār* and *afʾida* plural; **the soundest explanation is that *samʿ* stands in the verbal-noun form.** **The order runs from outside inward: two that gather, one that works upon what is gathered.** **And the choice of *afʾida* rather than *qulūb* suggests that what is given is not an organ but a centre of apprehension.**

### The difference here — *lā taʿlamūna shayʾā*

**al-Naḥl's link is set apart from the others by one expression, and this is the most striking thing in the verse.**

**In the other seven occurrences the triad is named alone, or together with a concluding sentence. In al-Naḥl it comes with a *note of state*: *lā taʿlamūna shayʾā* — "knowing nothing at all."**

| | The other occurrences | **al-Naḥl 16/78** |
|---|---|---|
| What precedes the triad | A verb (*jaʿala*, *anshaʾa*, *khatama*) | **A scene of coming out** — *akhrajakum min buṭūni ummahātikum* |
| A note of state | (none) | ***Lā taʿlamūna shayʾā*** |
| The closing | *Qalīlan mā tashkurūn* (32/9, 67/23) — **an accent on scarcity** | ***Laʿallakum tashkurūn*** — **an accent on hope** |

**All three differences can be verified from the text, and I record this as my own reading:**

**One — the triad is enumerated after a *starting point*.** In the other verses the faculties are named as given; **here the condition before the giving is stated as well: knowing nothing at all.** **The verse shows the zero.**

**Two — the consequence is this: the triad is enumerated not as *equipment* but as a *difference*.** Three doors are given to a being that comes out without knowledge. **Where knowledge comes from is shown by the distance between the two halves of the sentence.**

**Three — the difference of the closing.** al-Sajda and al-Mulk say *qalīlan mā tashkurūn* (little do you give thanks) — **a rebuke.** **al-Naḥl says *laʿallakum tashkurūn* — a hope.** **And this agrees with the sūra as a whole: al-Naḥl is a sūra of an enumeration of favours, and the purpose of the enumeration will be said outright in the eighty-first verse.**

### The ring formed by the seventy-eighth verse with the seventieth

**It was recorded above at the seventieth verse, and it is completed here:**

| Verse | Expression | Where in the life |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *Akhrajakum … **lā taʿlamūna shayʾā*** | **The beginning** — unknowing |
| — | *Wa jaʿala lakumu'l-samʿa wa'l-abṣāra wa'l-afʾida* | **The middle** — the faculties |
| **70** | *Lā yaʿlama **baʿda ʿilmin** shayʾā* | **The end** — knowledge taken back |

**All three steps can be verified from the text, and two of them use the same word: *shayʾā*.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the sūra describes a human life along the axis of knowledge and closes both its ends with the same word. **And the middle step — the three doors — is precisely the name of the difference between those two ends.**

---

## 16/79 — أَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ إِلَى ٱلطَّيْرِ مُسَخَّرَٰتٍ فِى جَوِّ ٱلسَّمَآءِ مَا يُمْسِكُهُنَّ إِلَّا ٱللَّهُ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَـَٔايَٰتٍ لِّقَوْمٍ يُؤْمِنُونَ

*A-lam yaraw ila'l-ṭayri musakhkharātin fī jawwi'l-samāʾ, mā yumsikuhunna illa'llāh, inna fī dhālika la-āyātin li-qawmin yuʾminūn*

"**Have they not seen the birds, made subservient in the open air of the sky? None holds them up but God.** In that there are signs for a people who believe."

### جَوّ — root: ج-و-و

**The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** **Its sense: the open space between earth and sky.** The lexicographers describe *jaww* as "a wide space that is empty within".

**And the construction must be recorded: *fī jawwi'l-samāʾ* — "in the open of the sky".** **The verse names the place where the bird flies an *emptiness*** — the place where there is nothing to take hold of.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the choice of word:** the rest of the sentence is *mā yumsikuhunna illa'llāh* — "none holds them up but God." **The word prepares the sentence's verb in advance:** where there is nothing to take hold of, one asks who is holding.

### The root م-س-ك and the link with Fāṭir

**The root of *imsāk* (holding) was the backbone of `035-fatir.md`** — it was tabulated there that at 35/2 (*fa-lā mumsika lahā*) and at 35/41 (*inna'llāha yumsiku'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa an tazūlā*) it bound the two ends of that sūra together. **I rely on that treatment.**

**Three verses can be set side by side:**

| Place | What is held | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| Fāṭir 35/2 | **Mercy** | *Fa-lā mumsika lahā* |
| Fāṭir 35/41 | **The heavens and the earth** | *Yumsiku'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa an tazūlā* |
| **al-Naḥl 16/79** | **Birds** | *Mā yumsikuhunna illa'llāh* |

**The difference of scale must be recorded, and I give it as an observation:** Fāṭir tells of the hand that holds the universe; **al-Naḥl uses the same verb of a bird.** **This suits the sūra's logic of enumeration: the evidence is chosen from close at hand.**

**And the same scene occurs at `067-mulk.md`, 67/19** (*a-wa-lam yaraw ila'l-ṭayri fawqahum ṣāffātin wa yaqbiḍna, mā yumsikuhunna illa'l-raḥmān*) **and was treated there.** **The two verses are almost identical, and the difference lies in a single word:**

| | **al-Mulk 67/19** | **al-Naḥl 16/79** |
|---|---|---|
| Who holds | ***al-Raḥmān*** | ***Allāh*** |
| The bird's condition | *Ṣāffātin wa **yaqbiḍna*** — spreading and folding the wings | *Musakhkharātin fī jawwi'l-samāʾ* — **made subservient** |
| The closing | *Innahū bi-kulli shayʾin **baṣīr*** | *La-āyātin li-qawmin **yuʾminūn*** |

**This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras. And the difference is worth recording:** al-Mulk describes the bird's **motion** (the spreading and folding); **al-Naḥl its *condition*** (made subservient). **al-Naḥl's word (*musakhkharāt*) is the very word used of the stars in the twelfth verse.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/80 — وَٱللَّهُ جَعَلَ لَكُم مِّنۢ بُيُوتِكُمْ سَكَنًا وَجَعَلَ لَكُم مِّن جُلُودِ ٱلْأَنْعَٰمِ بُيُوتًا تَسْتَخِفُّونَهَا يَوْمَ ظَعْنِكُمْ وَيَوْمَ إِقَامَتِكُمْ وَمِنْ أَصْوَافِهَا وَأَوْبَارِهَا وَأَشْعَارِهَآ أَثَٰثًا وَمَتَٰعًا إِلَىٰ حِينٍ

*Wa'llāhu jaʿala lakum min buyūtikum sakanan wa jaʿala lakum min julūdi'l-anʿāmi buyūtan tastakhiffūnahā yawma ẓaʿnikum wa yawma iqāmatikum, wa min aṣwāfihā wa awbārihā wa ashʿārihā athāthan wa matāʿan ilā ḥīn*

"**God made your houses a place of rest for you; and out of the hides of the livestock He made you houses light to carry on the day you strike camp and on the day you settle. And from their wool, their fur and their hair, furnishing and equipment for a time.**"

### Two houses

**The verse enumerates two shelters, and the two are of different kinds:**

| House | Material | Quality |
|---|---|---|
| *Min **buyūtikum** sakanā* | A permanent structure | ***Sakan*** — rest, coming to a stand |
| *Min **julūdi'l-anʿāmi** buyūtā* | **Of hide** | ***Tastakhiffūnahā*** — light to carry |

**سَكَن** — root **س-ك-ن**: to come to a stand, to find stillness, to settle. **The root was treated in detail at `030-rum.md`, 30/21 (*li-taskunū ilayhā*);** I rely on that treatment. **And what was recorded there was this: the word conveys not "the stopping of motion" but "finding a place to go and come to rest in".**

**And I give this as an observation:** in al-Rūm the object of the verb *sakana* was **a person** (the spouses); **in al-Naḥl it is a *place*.** **The same root, once in a human being, once in a place.**

**تَسْتَخِفُّونَهَا** — root **خ-ف-ف**: lightness. **In Form X (*istakhaffa*): to find light, to carry easily.** **The root was treated at `030-rum.md`, 30/60 (*wa lā yastakhiffannak*)** — there in the sense "to make light of, to unsettle". **Here the same form stands in its concrete sense: to carry easily.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the same form yields two different senses in two sūras — the one **moral** (to make light of), the other **physical** (to find light). **And both senses come out of the root's idea of "lightness".**

### ظَعْن — root: ظ-ع-ن

**The word occurs only here in the Qurʾān.** **Its sense: to migrate, to move from one place to another.** *Ẓaʿīna* — a woman travelling in a litter; **the word is the name for migration in the desert.**

**And the verse sets two days against each other:** *yawma **ẓaʿnikum** wa yawma **iqāmatikum*** — the day of striking camp and the day of settling.

**I give this as an observation:** in two words the verse sums up the addressee's whole order of life. **And the favour given fits both orders at once** — the tent is both carried and pitched.

### أَصْوَاف وَأَوْبَار وَأَشْعَار

**Three words, three different animal fibres, and the lexicographers record the distinction:**

| Word | Root | Which animal |
|---|---|---|
| **صُوف (ṣūf)** | ص-و-ف | **Sheep's wool** |
| **وَبَر (wabar)** | و-ب-ر | **Camel hair** |
| **شَعْر (shaʿr)** | ش-ع-ر | **Goat hair** |

**That the three are enumerated separately is worth recording.** **The verse does not say "from the hair of the animals"; it names all three apart.**

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being this threefold naming:** the enumeration divides its items instead of gathering them. **And this is the practical counterpart of the ruling *lā tuḥṣūhā* in the eighteenth verse:** if one item can be divided into three, counting is not a task that ends.

**أَثَاثًا وَمَتَاعًا إِلَىٰ حِينٍ** — *athāth*: household goods, bedding; root **أ-ث-ث**: a thing's being abundant and closely entangled. *Matāʿ*: what is made use of.

**And a qualification must be recorded: *ilā ḥīn* — "for a time".** **A limit of time is thus set upon this item of the enumeration.** **And it belongs to the same family as *ajalin musammā* in the sūra's sixty-first verse.**

---

## 16/81 — وَٱللَّهُ جَعَلَ لَكُم مِّمَّا خَلَقَ ظِلَٰلًا وَجَعَلَ لَكُم مِّنَ ٱلْجِبَالِ أَكْنَٰنًا وَجَعَلَ لَكُمْ سَرَٰبِيلَ تَقِيكُمُ ٱلْحَرَّ وَسَرَٰبِيلَ تَقِيكُم بَأْسَكُمْ كَذَٰلِكَ يُتِمُّ نِعْمَتَهُۥ عَلَيْكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُسْلِمُونَ

*Wa'llāhu jaʿala lakum mimmā khalaqa ẓilālan wa jaʿala lakum mina'l-jibāli aknānan wa jaʿala lakum sarābīla taqīkumu'l-ḥarra wa sarābīla taqīkum baʾsakum, ka-dhālika yutimmu niʿmatahū ʿalaykum laʿallakum tuslimūn*

"**God made shade for you out of what He created; He made you shelters in the mountains; and He gave you garments that guard you from the heat, and coats that guard you in your own violence. Thus He completes His favour upon you** — that you might submit."

### Four *jaʿala*s, one closing

**The verse says *jaʿala lakum* four times** (three in this verse, one in the eightieth) **and at the end closes the whole enumeration with a single sentence.**

| Order | What is given | Against what |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Ẓilālan* — **shade** | Against the sun |
| 2 | *Aknānan* — **shelters** | (not specified) |
| 3 | *Sarābīla taqīkumu'l-**ḥarr*** | **Against the heat** |
| 4 | *Sarābīla taqīkum **baʾsakum*** | **Against your own violence** |

**The fourth item must be recorded, and it is the most striking thing in the verse: *baʾsakum* — "your own violence".**

**The fourth protection, then, is not a protection from nature but from man himself.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the pronoun itself:** the first three items are against forces outside — the sun, the need for shelter, the heat. **In the fourth the pronoun turns back upon the one addressed.** **In the last item of its enumeration of favours the sūra counts protection from what man does to man as a favour too.**

**And this agrees with the sūra as a whole:** in the fourth verse man was named *khaṣīmun mubīn* (an open disputant). **In the eighty-first, protection from where that disputing arrives is enumerated.**

### ظِلَٰلًا — the shadow of the forty-eighth verse

**It must be recorded: in the forty-eighth verse the shadows were enumerated as a *proof*** (*yatafayyaʾu ẓilāluhū … sujjadan li'llāh*). **In the eighty-first the same word is enumerated as a *favour*.**

| Verse | *Ẓilāl* | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **48** | *Yatafayyaʾu **ẓilā**luhū … sujjadā* | **Proof** — the sign of submission |
| **81** | *Jaʿala lakum mimmā khalaqa **ẓilā**lā* | **Favour** — protection |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and it is the sūra's characteristic movement:** the same fact is enumerated twice, in two different columns. **The same was done with water (10 and 65).**

### أَكْنَان and سَرَابِيل

**أَكْنَان** — the plural of *kinn*, root **ك-ن-ن**: **to cover, to hide, to protect.** *Maknūn* — hidden (in the Qurʾān used of a pearl); *kināna* — a quiver. **The word means "a place one goes inside and is hidden in".**

**سَرَابِيل** — the plural of *sirbāl*: **a shirt, a garment worn over.** **The lexicographers record that the word may have come into Arabic from another language;** this is transmitted as a possibility, not as a settled ruling.

**And the expression used of the second *sarābīl* must be recorded: *taqīkum baʾsakum*.** **The exegetes generally explain it as armour** — indeed the making of armour is named elsewhere in the Qurʾān (al-Anbiyāʾ 21/80: *wa ʿallamnāhu ṣanʿata labūsin lakum li-tuḥṣinakum min baʾsikum*). **The overlap with that verse is worth recording, and both verses use the same word: *baʾsakum*.**

### كَذَٰلِكَ يُتِمُّ نِعْمَتَهُۥ عَلَيْكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُسْلِمُونَ — the closing of the enumeration

**And the sūra's second enumeration closes here, with a sentence of purpose.**

**Three words must be recorded:**

| Word | Root | What it says |
|---|---|---|
| ***Yutimmu*** | ت-م-م | **He is completing** — it goes on, it is not finished |
| ***Niʿmatahū*** | ن-ع-م | **Singular** — as in the eighteenth verse |
| ***Tuslimūn*** | س-ل-م | **Submitting** — the purpose |

**And the verb must be recorded: *yutimmu* — imperfect.** **The completing is thus not a finished work; it goes on.** **This is directly connected with the ruling *lā tuḥṣūhā* (you cannot count them) in the eighteenth verse, and I give it as my own reading:** the reason they cannot be counted is that the list is still lengthening.

**And the verb of purpose differs from the sūra's closing verbs. All seven of the closings tabulated above were verbs of cognition or of faith** (to think, to reason, to remember, to hear, to believe). **This eighth is a verb of *stance*: *tuslimūn* — to submit.**

**The same distinction was recorded at `030-rum.md`, 30/46:** the link that stood apart in the *wa min āyātihī* series ended with a verb of **response** (*tashkurūn*). **In al-Naḥl the enumeration ends with a verb of *submission*.**

**I record this as my own reading, my ground being the series of verbs:** the sūra begins its counting with **thinking** and ends it with **submission.** **And seven distinct verbs are enumerated in between.** **The enumeration thus moves from an operation of knowledge towards a change of stance.**

---

## 16/82-83 — فَإِن تَوَلَّوْا۟ فَإِنَّمَا عَلَيْكَ ٱلْبَلَٰغُ ٱلْمُبِينُ · يَعْرِفُونَ نِعْمَتَ ٱللَّهِ ثُمَّ يُنكِرُونَهَا وَأَكْثَرُهُمُ ٱلْكَٰفِرُونَ

*Fa-in tawallaw fa-innamā ʿalayka'l-balāghu'l-mubīn · Yaʿrifūna niʿmata'llāhi thumma yunkirūnahā wa aktharuhumu'l-kāfirūn*

"**If they turn away, nothing is laid upon you but plain delivery.** · **They recognise God's favour, then they deny it; and most of them are ungrateful.**"

### The sharpest diagnosis in the sūra

**The relation between the two verbs gathers the diagnosis of the whole sūra into a single sentence:**

| Verb | Root | What |
|---|---|---|
| ***Yaʿrifūn*** | ع-ر-ف | **To recognise** — to know, to be aware of |
| ***Yunkirūnahā*** | ن-ك-ر | **To deny** — to fail to recognise, to reject |

**And the lexicographers count the two roots as opposites: *maʿrifa* and *nakira*.** **In Arabic a noun that is definite is called *maʿrifa* and one that is indefinite *nakira* — the same two roots.**

**The sentence thus sets two opposed verbs one after the other upon a single object: *yaʿrifūna … thumma yunkirūnahā*.**

**And the particle *thumma* must be recorded.** In Arabic *thumma* conveys **sequence and an interval** — "then". **The denial does not take the place of the recognising; it comes *after* it.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that particle:** the verse does not describe a state of ignorance. **The recognising has taken place, and then been rejected.** **And this coincides with the word *yajḥadūn* (denial in the face of knowledge) in the seventy-first verse** — **as was recorded at `046-ahkaf.md`, 46/26, ج-ح-د is denial against knowledge.**

**The whole enumeration of the sūra arrives at one place in this sentence: the trouble is not a failure to see the list; it is the rejecting of it after seeing.**

**And it can be compared with the diagnosis at `030-rum.md`, 30/7:** there the fault was named *yaʿlamūna **ẓāhiran** mina'l-ḥayāti'l-dunyā* — **superficiality.** **Here something else is said.**

| Sūra | Diagnosis | Where the trouble lies |
|---|---|---|
| al-Rūm 30/7 | *Yaʿlamūna ẓāhiran* | **In the depth of the looking** — remaining on the surface |
| **al-Naḥl 16/83** | *Yaʿrifūna … thumma yunkirūnahā* | **After the looking** — refusing to accept |

**This is a difference verifiable across the sūras, and I give it as my own reading:** al-Rūm diagnoses the shallowness of the looking, al-Naḥl the step that comes after the looking. **The two sūras catch the same fact at two different stages.**

**وَأَكْثَرُهُمُ ٱلْكَٰفِرُونَ** — and the scope must be recorded. **The verse does not say "all of them": *aktharuhum* — most.** **Per the method (`USLUP.md`) the ruling is tied not to a group but to an attribute, and the verse's own word keeps this.**

---

## 16/84-86 — وَيَوْمَ نَبْعَثُ مِن كُلِّ أُمَّةٍ شَهِيدًا ثُمَّ لَا يُؤْذَنُ لِلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ وَلَا هُمْ يُسْتَعْتَبُونَ · وَإِذَا رَءَا ٱلَّذِينَ ظَلَمُوا۟ ٱلْعَذَابَ فَلَا يُخَفَّفُ عَنْهُمْ وَلَا هُمْ يُنظَرُونَ · وَإِذَا رَءَا ٱلَّذِينَ أَشْرَكُوا۟ شُرَكَآءَهُمْ قَالُوا۟ رَبَّنَا هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ شُرَكَآؤُنَا ٱلَّذِينَ كُنَّا نَدْعُوا۟ مِن دُونِكَ فَأَلْقَوْا۟ إِلَيْهِمُ ٱلْقَوْلَ إِنَّكُمْ لَكَٰذِبُونَ

*Wa yawma nabʿathu min kulli ummatin shahīdan thumma lā yuʾdhanu lilladhīna kafarū wa lā hum yustaʿtabūn · Wa idhā raʾa'lladhīna ẓalamu'l-ʿadhāba fa-lā yukhaffafu ʿanhum wa lā hum yunẓarūn · Wa idhā raʾa'lladhīna ashrakū shurakāʾahum qālū rabbanā hāʾulāʾi shurakāʾuna'lladhīna kunnā nadʿū min dūnik, fa-alqaw ilayhimu'l-qawla innakum la-kādhibūn*

"**On the day We raise up a witness from every community, no leave will be given to those who disbelieved, nor will they be asked to make amends.** · **When those who did wrong see the punishment, it will not be lightened for them, nor will they be granted respite.** · **When those who associated see the partners they associated, they will say: 'Our Lord, these are our partners, whom we used to call upon besides You.' And they will fling the word back at them: 'You are liars.'**"

### يُسْتَعْتَبُونَ — root: ع-ت-ب

**The word *istiʿtāb* was treated at `041-fussilet.md`, 41/24, and the same root occurred at `030-rum.md`, 30/57.** **I rely on those treatments.** **The sense of the root: to remove a grievance, to win back goodwill; in Form X, *to be asked to make amends*.**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: two doors close one after the other.**

| What closes | Expression |
|---|---|
| **Leave to speak** | *Lā yuʾdhanu lilladhīna kafarū* |
| **The chance to make amends** | *Wa lā hum yustaʿtabūn* |

**The sense of the second note is worth recording, and I give it as an observation:** the verse does not say "their excuses will not be accepted". **It says "they will not be asked to make amends".** **What closes, then, is the stage before the door of excuse: the stage at which amends are *called for*.**

### فَأَلْقَوْا۟ إِلَيْهِمُ ٱلْقَوْلَ

**And the verb must be recorded: *alqaw ilayhimu'l-qawl* — "they flung the word at them".**

**The same verb (ل-ق-ي, Form IV) occurs for the third time in the sūra:** in the fifteenth verse of the mountains (*wa alqā fi'l-arḍi rawāsiya*), in the twenty-eighth of surrender (*fa-alqawu'l-salam*), here of a word. **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**And the picture of *ilqāʾ* here is worth recording:** the word is not spoken, it is **thrown.** **Like a thing hurled at the other party.**

**This threefold refusal was treated at `035-fatir.md`, 35/13-14, and at `046-ahkaf.md`, 46/5-6;** I rely on those treatments.

**And the giving of the lie in the eighty-sixth verse must be recorded: *innakum la-kādhibūn* — to whom is it said?** The lexicographers record that it is said **by those who were served to those who served them.** **And what is given the lie is the claim of attribution in the preceding sentence** — the words "these were our partners".

---

## 16/87-88 — وَأَلْقَوْا۟ إِلَى ٱللَّهِ يَوْمَئِذٍ ٱلسَّلَمَ وَضَلَّ عَنْهُم مَّا كَانُوا۟ يَفْتَرُونَ · ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ وَصَدُّوا۟ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ زِدْنَٰهُمْ عَذَابًا فَوْقَ ٱلْعَذَابِ بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يُفْسِدُونَ

*Wa alqaw ila'llāhi yawmaʾidhini'l-salama wa ḍalla ʿanhum mā kānū yaftarūn · Alladhīna kafarū wa ṣaddū ʿan sabīli'llāhi zidnāhum ʿadhāban fawqa'l-ʿadhābi bimā kānū yufsidūn*

"**On that day they will offer their surrender to God, and what they used to invent will be lost to them.** · **Those who disbelieved and turned others from the way of God — We shall add punishment upon punishment for the corruption they wrought.**"

### Two surrenders

**The same expression occurred in the twenty-eighth verse: *fa-alqawu'l-salam*. In the eighty-seventh a word is added: *ila'llāh*.**

| Verse | Expression | To whom the surrender |
|---|---|---|
| **28** | *Fa-**alqawu'l-salam*** | **Not specified** — a defence follows it |
| **87** | *Wa **alqaw ila'llāhi** yawmaʾidhini'l-salam* | ***To God*** — no defence |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, its ground being the added word:** in the twenty-eighth verse a denial followed the surrender (*mā kunnā naʿmalu min sūʾ*). **In the eighty-seventh none follows.** **The scene shows the same act in its completed form.**

### عَذَابًا فَوْقَ ٱلْعَذَاب

**And the ground given for the increase must be recorded: *bimā kānū yufsidūn*.**

**The sentence had enumerated two acts — *kafarū* and *ṣaddū* — but as its ground it gives a third word: *ifsād*.**

**I give this as an observation:** the added punishment is tied not to the denial itself but to **the side of it that touches another.** *Ṣaddū ʿan sabīli'llāh* — "to turn others from the way of God" — is already an act that concerns someone else. **And the word *ifsād* names it.**

**The same distinction was made in the twenty-fifth verse** (*wa min awzāri'lladhīna yuḍillūnahum*): **a man's own burden and the burden of leading astray were enumerated separately.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/89 — وَيَوْمَ نَبْعَثُ فِى كُلِّ أُمَّةٍ شَهِيدًا عَلَيْهِم مِّنْ أَنفُسِهِمْ وَجِئْنَا بِكَ شَهِيدًا عَلَىٰ هَٰٓؤُلَآءِ وَنَزَّلْنَا عَلَيْكَ ٱلْكِتَٰبَ تِبْيَٰنًا لِّكُلِّ شَىْءٍ وَهُدًى وَرَحْمَةً وَبُشْرَىٰ لِلْمُسْلِمِينَ

*Wa yawma nabʿathu fī kulli ummatin shahīdan ʿalayhim min anfusihim wa jiʾnā bika shahīdan ʿalā hāʾulāʾ, wa nazzalnā ʿalayka'l-kitāba tibyānan li-kulli shayʾin wa hudan wa raḥmatan wa bushrā li'l-muslimīn*

"**On the day We raise up in every community a witness against them from among themselves, and bring you as a witness against these. And We have sent down to you the Book as an exposition of everything, a guidance, a mercy, and glad tidings for those who submit.**"

### The repetition of the eighty-fourth verse, and the difference

**The same sentence occurred five verses earlier. The two occurrences must be compared:**

| | **Verse 84** | **Verse 89** |
|---|---|---|
| The particle | *Nabʿathu **min** kulli ummatin* | *Nabʿathu **fī** kulli ummatin* |
| The qualification | (none) | *ʿAlayhim **min anfusihim*** |
| What follows | The scene of punishment | ***Wa jiʾnā bika shahīdan*** |
| The closing | *Lā hum yustaʿtabūn* | **The qualities of the Book** |

**The difference of particle must be recorded, and the lexicographers dwell on it:** *min* conveys "from among"; *fī* conveys "within". **Both particles give a sense close to the same thing, but the second stresses the witness's being inside the community.**

**And the eighty-ninth verse says this outright: *min anfusihim* — "from among themselves".** **The witness, then, is not someone brought in from outside.**

### تِبْيَٰنًا لِّكُلِّ شَىْءٍ

**The construction is the third and last link of the sūra's ب-ي-ن series** (39, 44, 89). **It was tabulated above.**

**And the form of the word must be recorded: *tibyān* — a verbal noun on the *tifʿāl* pattern.** **The lexicographers record that this pattern conveys intensification:** *tibyān* is **the fullest form of making clear.**

**The exegetes differ over the scope of the construction, and the disagreement must be set out in a table:**

| View | What *li-kulli shayʾ* covers |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Religious rulings and the knowledge that is needed** — the context requires it |
| 2 | **Everything that concerns guidance** — everything within the Book's own subject |
| 3 | **Absolute scope** — with no limit set |

**All three views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But a note must be entered in point of word order, and I give it as an observation:** the word *tibyān* does not stand alone in the sentence; **three further qualities follow it — *hudan*, *raḥmatan*, *bushrā*.** **And all three look towards a definite field: guiding, mercy, glad tidings.** **Read together with the three words beside it, the scope of the construction narrows.** **This is an observation; it is not a preference within the disagreement.**

**And the four qualities must be recorded:**

| Quality | What | To whom |
|---|---|---|
| *Tibyānan li-kulli shayʾ* | **Exposition** | (unqualified) |
| *Wa hudan* | **Guidance** | (unqualified) |
| *Wa raḥmatan* | **Mercy** | (unqualified) |
| *Wa bushrā* | **Glad tidings** | ***Li'l-muslimīn*** — **qualified** |

**Only the last has its addressee named.** **I give this as an observation:** exposition, guidance and mercy are unqualified; the glad tidings are for those who submit. **The first three are thus left open to everyone.**

**And the sixty-fourth verse had a shorter form of the same sequence: *wa hudan wa raḥmatan li-qawmin yuʾminūn*.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and here one further quality is added.**

---

## 16/90 — إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَأْمُرُ بِٱلْعَدْلِ وَٱلْإِحْسَٰنِ وَإِيتَآئِ ذِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَيَنْهَىٰ عَنِ ٱلْفَحْشَآءِ وَٱلْمُنكَرِ وَٱلْبَغْىِ يَعِظُكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ

*Inna'llāha yaʾmuru bi'l-ʿadli wa'l-iḥsāni wa ītāʾi dhi'l-qurbā wa yanhā ʿani'l-faḥshāʾi wa'l-munkari wa'l-baghy, yaʿiẓukum laʿallakum tadhakkarūn*

"**God commands justice, doing good, and giving to kinsfolk; and He forbids indecency, what is wrong, and oppression. He admonishes you — that you may take heed.**"

### Three commands, three prohibitions

**The structure of the verse is symmetrical and must be set out in a table:**

| | What is commanded | Root | What is forbidden | Root |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***al-ʿAdl*** — equivalence | ع-د-ل | ***al-Faḥshāʾ*** — indecency | ف-ح-ش |
| 2 | ***al-Iḥsān*** — the surplus | ح-س-ن | ***al-Munkar*** — what is not acknowledged | ن-ك-ر |
| 3 | ***Ītāʾu dhi'l-qurbā*** — giving to kinsfolk | أ-ت-ي / ق-ر-ب | ***al-Baghy*** — overstepping | ب-غ-ي |

**And the symmetry is not only in number but in order. I record this as my own reading, and my ground is the reading of the two lists against each other:**

**The commands run *from the general to the particular*:** justice (to everyone), *iḥsān* (to everyone, but with more than is due), giving to kinsfolk (to a definite circle).
**The prohibitions run *from the particular to the general*:** *faḥshāʾ* (concerning a man himself and those close to him), *munkar* (what the society does not acknowledge), *baghy* (spilling over into another's domain).

**The two lists thus run in opposite directions and meet in the middle.** **I do not rest this reading on a transmitted report; my ground is the scope of the words.**

### The difference between عَدْل and إِحْسَان

**I treat this difference in detail, because the relation between the verse's first two words carries the weight of the whole sentence.**

**عَدْل — root ع-د-ل.** **The root was treated at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/9, and compared there with *qisṭ*. I rely on that treatment and take over its note:**

> **"عَدْل — root ع-د-ل. Its primary sense is to make even, to equalise, to set straight. The two pans of a balance coming level. *Taʿdīl* — correcting, evening out."**

**And another derivative of the root bears out this picture: *ʿadl* also means "the equivalent of a thing"** — in the Qurʾān it has the sense of "an equal recompense" in usages such as *aw ʿadlu dhālika ṣiyāmā* (al-Māʾida 5/95). **At the centre of the word, then, stands equivalence.**

**إِحْسَان — root ح-س-ن.** **The root was treated at `067-mulk.md`, 67/2, and at `032-secde.md`, 32/7.** **The note there:**

> **"Beneath the root lies the idea of fitness and aptness: *ḥasan* is a thing's being as it ought to be. *Iḥsān* comes from the same root and means 'to do a work giving it its full due'."**

**Now I set the two words against each other:**

| | **عَدْل** | **إِحْسَان** |
|---|---|---|
| The picture in the root | **A balance** — the two pans level | **Beauty / aptness** — giving a thing its due |
| Its measure | **As much as is owed** — neither less nor more | **More than is owed**, or with nothing owed |
| Can it be demanded | **Yes** — it is claimed as a right | **No** — it is not demanded, it is given |
| Its absence | **Injustice** | **Not injustice** — only the absence of the surplus |
| Its form | Triliteral — *ʿadala* | **Form IV** — *aḥsana*; **transitive, directed at another** |

**The difference of form must be recorded, and it is an observation about the language:** *iḥsān* is of Form IV, and in Arabic this form conveys **the carrying of the act over to another.** *Aḥsana ilayhi* — he did him good. **Even in the pattern of the word, then, there is a movement "towards another".**

**And the order of the two words is worth recording: first *ʿadl*, then *iḥsān*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that order:** *iḥsān* does not take the place of justice — **it is laid on top of it.** **The surplus is meaningful only after the equivalence has been provided.** A surplus given without the equivalence becomes a cover for a right left unpaid.

**And at `042-sura.md`, 42/40-43, these two steps were treated by way of another pair** — *mithl* (an equal recompense) and *ʿafw* (pardon). **The pattern recorded there is the same: the right is measured, the surplus is left unmeasured.** **The two verses build the same structure with two different pairs of words:**

| Sūra | What is measured | What is unmeasured |
|---|---|---|
| al-Shūrā 42/40 | *Sayyiʾatun **mithluhā*** — an equal recompense | *Fa-**ajruhū** ʿala'llāh* — pardon |
| **al-Naḥl 16/90** | ***al-ʿAdl*** — equivalence | ***al-Iḥsān*** — the surplus |

**This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

### وَإِيتَآئِ ذِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ

**The third command differs from the first two:** it is not a concept but **an act and an object.**

**The construction was treated at `030-rum.md`, 30/38, and at `017-isra.md`, 17/26** (*fa-āti dha'l-qurbā ḥaqqah*), **and the word *ḥaqqahū* was dwelt on there: what is given is not a gift but a right.** **I rely on those treatments.**

**The difference here must be recorded:** al-Rūm and al-Isrāʾ say *ḥaqqahū*; **al-Naḥl does not.** **Here, then, the command is laid down not as the paying of a right but as the act of giving.**

### The three prohibitions

**ف-ح-ش** — root: **an ugliness that exceeds measure.** *Fāḥish* — excessive, going past the bound. **The root was treated in `024-nur.md` and at `017-isra.md`, 17/32;** I do not repeat it here.

**ن-ك-ر** — **what is not recognised, what is found strange.** **And the word has already occurred twice in the sūra:** *qulūbuhum munkira* (their hearts are in denial) in the twenty-second verse, *thumma yunkirūnahā* (then they deny it) in the eighty-third. **In the ninetieth the same root returns as a *moral category*: *al-munkar*.**

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and I record this as my own reading:** in the sūra the same root occurs first as **the state of a heart**, then as **the name of an act**, and finally as **a forbidden category.** **The act of "not recognising" thus widens from inside outwards through the sūra.**

**ب-غ-ي** — **to overstep the bound, to overflow, to pass into another's domain.** **The root was tabulated throughout `042-sura.md` and was treated at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/9 (*fa-in baghat iḥdāhumā*).** **I rely on those treatments.** **And the line recorded at al-Shūrā 42/42 holds here too: the ruling is tied not to a group but to an act.**

### يَعِظُكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ

**And the verse closes with a word of *admonition*: *yaʿiẓukum*.**

**The root و-ع-ظ:** speech that softens the heart and calls consequences to mind. **And the word will return once more in the hundred and twenty-fifth verse, as the second item of the method of calling: *wa'l-**mawʿiẓati**'l-ḥasana*.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**I record this as my own reading:** the ninetieth verse enumerates six rulings and at the end calls them not a **command** but an **admonition.** **And in the hundred and twenty-fifth the same word will be the name of the task given to the one addressed.** **The sūra thus names the work it is itself doing, and hands that same work over to the one addressed.**

---

## 16/91-92 — وَأَوْفُوا۟ بِعَهْدِ ٱللَّهِ إِذَا عَٰهَدتُّمْ وَلَا تَنقُضُوا۟ ٱلْأَيْمَٰنَ بَعْدَ تَوْكِيدِهَا وَقَدْ جَعَلْتُمُ ٱللَّهَ عَلَيْكُمْ كَفِيلًا إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يَعْلَمُ مَا تَفْعَلُونَ · وَلَا تَكُونُوا۟ كَٱلَّتِى نَقَضَتْ غَزْلَهَا مِنۢ بَعْدِ قُوَّةٍ أَنكَٰثًا تَتَّخِذُونَ أَيْمَٰنَكُمْ دَخَلًۢا بَيْنَكُمْ أَن تَكُونَ أُمَّةٌ هِىَ أَرْبَىٰ مِنْ أُمَّةٍ

*Wa awfū bi-ʿahdi'llāhi idhā ʿāhadtum wa lā tanquḍu'l-aymāna baʿda tawkīdihā wa qad jaʿaltumu'llāha ʿalaykum kafīlā, inna'llāha yaʿlamu mā tafʿalūn · Wa lā takūnū ka'llatī naqaḍat ghazlahā min baʿdi quwwatin ankāthā, tattakhidhūna aymānakum dakhalan baynakum an takūna ummatun hiya arbā min umma*

"**Fulfil God's covenant when you have made a covenant, and do not break your oaths after they have been made fast — you have made God a surety over you.** God knows what you do. · **And do not be like the woman who unravelled her yarn into loose shreds after it had been spun strong: you make your oaths a means of deceit among yourselves, because one community is more numerous than another.**"

### نَقَضَ — root: ن-ق-ض

**The concrete sense of the root: to undo something plaited, twisted, made firm.** *Naqaḍa'l-ḥabl* — he untwisted the rope; *naqīḍ* — undone. **The lexicographers record a sense of *sound* in the root as well: *naqīḍ*, the cracking of the joints** — that is, the sound of a bond giving way.

**And that the word is used together with *ʿahd* shows that the language carries within it an image of weaving.** **The same image is present in the root ن-ك-ث and was treated at `048-fetih.md`, 48/10. I rely on that treatment and take its note over here:**

> **"The concrete sense of the root: to open up again, to unravel a thread that has been spun and twisted. In the dictionaries *nikth* is used of wool or hair that has been unravelled: spun, then picked apart."**
>
> **"'Breaking a covenant', then, carries within the language an image of weaving. To give one's word is to spin a thread; to break one's word is to unpick the thread that was spun."**
>
> **"And this image is worth recording: an unpicked thread does not return to what it was before. The crimp stays, the strength goes. A broken undertaking is different from an undertaking never given — it leaves something behind."**

**In the al-Fatḥ section the image was recorded as an inference. In al-Naḥl the same image is built openly by the verse itself.**

### The parable of the woman who unravels her yarn

**I treat this parable in detail, because it is one of the most concrete comparisons in the Qurʾān and its whole weight is gathered into three words.**

**The sentence is this: *ka'llatī naqaḍat ghazlahā min baʿdi quwwatin ankāthā*.**

| Word | Root | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| ***Naqaḍat*** | ن-ق-ض | **She unravelled** — she unpicked what had been spun |
| ***Ghazl*** | غ-ز-ل | **Spun yarn** — wool twisted on the spindle |
| ***Min baʿdi quwwatin*** | ق-و-ي | **After it had been made strong** |
| ***Ankāthā*** | ن-ك-ث | **Unpicked shreds of fibre** — plural |

**The scene these four words build is this, and it must be read step by step:**

**One — the work has been done.** The wool has been spun, made into yarn. The word *ghazl* names it: the **product** of the spinning.

**Two — the work has been made firm.** *Min baʿdi quwwatin* — "after it was strengthened". **The yarn was not merely spun; it was tightened, made durable.** **And this note coincides with the word in the preceding verse:** *baʿda **tawkīdihā*** (after they had been made fast). **The two verses build the same structure: a thing is made firm, then broken.**

**Three — the work has been taken back.** *Naqaḍat* — she unravelled it.

**Four — and what is taken back does not return to its first condition.** ***Ankāthā*** — unpicked shreds of fibre. **The wool is no longer the wool it was before the spinning; nor is it yarn.** **A third thing has come to be in between: waste.**

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is that the word *ankāthā* is plural:** the verse does not say "she unravelled it" and stop. **It says besides what the unravelled thing became — and what it says is that there is no way back.**

**And the inference recorded in `048-fetih.md` is here borne out by the text's own word:** *"an unpicked thread does not return to what it was before."* **al-Naḥl says it with the word *ankāth*.**

### The taking back of labour

**The work the parable chooses is worth recording, and I give this as my own reading:**

**Spinning is work whose results accumulate slowly.** It is not finished in a day; it advances little by little, thread by thread. **And the force of the parable comes from the destruction being done by the same hand:** the one who unravels the yarn is the one who spun it.

**The verse, then, does not tell of a harm coming from outside. It tells of a hand turning back upon its own labour.**

**And the bearing of this on the spoken word is drawn directly:** an oath too rests upon a trust built up over time. **What is lost when it is broken is not that one oath — it is the whole accumulation behind it.** **And *ankāth* is indeed not a single thread but pieces.**

**I do not rest this reading on a transmitted report; my ground is the work the parable chooses and the plural of the word *ankāth*.**

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`)

**A woman is named in the parable, and in the classical sources it is transmitted that this comparison points to a particular woman who lived in Mecca.** **This commentary attaches no personal name to that report**, because I have no verified source for it. **And, more important: what the verse builds is not the description of a person but the description of a *work*.**

**The wording of the sentence shows it: *ka'llatī* — not "like that woman" but "like the one who does this work".** **The definite relative pronoun points not to the person but to the act.** **And the line kept at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/18, is kept here too: the verse speaks not about a sex but about a behaviour.**

### دَخَلًا بَيْنَكُمْ

**دَخَل** — root **د-خ-ل**: to enter. **The lexicographers describe the word thus: a corrupting element pushed inside a thing and not belonging to it; hidden corruption, deceit.** *Dakhal* is used also of a person who joins a community afterwards and spoils it.

**And the picture in the word works together with the parable:** the yarn looks sound from outside, but within it there is a faulty fibre. **An oath too looks sound from outside, but a *dakhal* has been mixed into it.**

**I record this as an observation about the language, and I rest it on the presence of the two words (*ghazl* and *dakhal*) in the same verse.**

**أَرْبَىٰ** — root **ر-ب-و**: **to increase, to swell, to grow more.** **The root was treated in detail at `030-rum.md`, 30/39, and at `002-bakara.md`, 2/275-276 (*ribā*);** I rely on those treatments.

**And the usage here is worth recording:** *an takūna ummatun hiya **arbā** min umma* — one community's being **more numerous, stronger** than another. **The reason given for breaking one's word, then, is *superiority in numbers*.**

**I give this as an observation:** the verse names the breaking of a covenant not as a debate about principle but as **a calculation of power.** **The reason for changing sides is which side is the more numerous.**

**إِنَّمَا يَبْلُوكُمُ ٱللَّهُ بِهِۦ وَلَيُبَيِّنَنَّ لَكُمْ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ مَا كُنتُمْ فِيهِ تَخْتَلِفُونَ** — and the rest of the verse says two things: that this is a **trial**, and that the disagreement will be **made clear** on the Day of Resurrection.

**The second clause must be recorded: *wa la-yubayyinanna lakum yawma'l-qiyāmati mā kuntum fīhi takhtalifūn*.** **This is a link in the sūra's ب-ي-ن series, and it is almost identical with the sentence in the thirty-ninth verse** (*li-yubayyina lahumu'lladhī yakhtalifūna fīh*). **The difference between them is the addressee: there the third person, here the second.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/93-94 — وَلَوْ شَآءَ ٱللَّهُ لَجَعَلَكُمْ أُمَّةً وَٰحِدَةً وَلَٰكِن يُضِلُّ مَن يَشَآءُ وَيَهْدِى مَن يَشَآءُ وَلَتُسْـَٔلُنَّ عَمَّا كُنتُمْ تَعْمَلُونَ · وَلَا تَتَّخِذُوٓا۟ أَيْمَٰنَكُمْ دَخَلًۢا بَيْنَكُمْ فَتَزِلَّ قَدَمٌۢ بَعْدَ ثُبُوتِهَا وَتَذُوقُوا۟ ٱلسُّوٓءَ بِمَا صَدَدتُّمْ عَن سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ وَلَكُمْ عَذَابٌ عَظِيمٌ

*Wa law shāʾa'llāhu la-jaʿalakum ummatan wāḥidatan wa lākin yuḍillu man yashāʾu wa yahdī man yashāʾ, wa la-tusʾalunna ʿammā kuntum taʿmalūn · Wa lā tattakhidhū aymānakum dakhalan baynakum fa-tazilla qadamun baʿda thubūtihā wa tadhūqu'l-sūʾa bimā ṣadadtum ʿan sabīli'llāhi wa lakum ʿadhābun ʿaẓīm*

"**Had God willed He would have made you one single community; but He lets go astray whom He wills and guides whom He wills. You will surely be questioned about what you were doing.** · **Do not make your oaths a means of deceit among yourselves, or a foot will slip after it had stood firm, and you will taste evil for having turned others from the way of God; and there will be a great punishment for you besides.**"

### فَتَزِلَّ قَدَمٌۢ بَعْدَ ثُبُوتِهَا — the second parable

**It must be recorded: the structure of the parable in the ninety-second verse is built here a second time, in another picture.**

| | **Verse 92** | **Verse 94** |
|---|---|---|
| Before | *Ghazlahā min baʿdi **quwwatin*** — yarn made strong | *Qadamun baʿda **thubūtihā*** — a foot that had stood firm |
| After | *Naqaḍat … **ankāthā*** — unravelled shreds | *Fa-**tazilla*** — it slips |
| The shared structure | **Firmness → undoing** | ***The same*** |

**Both parables are built on the same skeleton, and both use the particle *baʿda*.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**And the difference must be recorded:** in the first parable the one who undoes is **the person herself** (*naqaḍat*); in the second the foot **slips** (*tazilla*) — the verb is intransitive, there is no agent. **The first parable tells of the intent, the second of the outcome.**

**زَلَّ** — root **ز-ل-ل**: **to slip, to stumble.** *Zalla* — the foot slipped; *zalla* — a lapse. **And the lexicographers record the root's connection with slippery ground.**

**ثُبُوت** — root **ث-ب-ت**: **to be fixed, to stand in place.** **And the word is ascribed to the foot as *thubūtihā*: "after its standing firm".**

**قَدَمٌۢ** — and the noun comes **indefinite**: "a foot". **The lexicographers record that the indefiniteness here is not *taḥqīr* (belittling) but *ibhām* (leaving unspecified)** — whose foot it is, is not said.

**I give this as an observation:** the sentence does not say "your foot will slip". **It says "a foot will slip".** **The warning is left without an address.**

---

## 16/95-96 — وَلَا تَشْتَرُوا۟ بِعَهْدِ ٱللَّهِ ثَمَنًا قَلِيلًا إِنَّمَا عِندَ ٱللَّهِ هُوَ خَيْرٌ لَّكُمْ إِن كُنتُمْ تَعْلَمُونَ · مَا عِندَكُمْ يَنفَدُ وَمَا عِندَ ٱللَّهِ بَاقٍ وَلَنَجْزِيَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ صَبَرُوٓا۟ أَجْرَهُم بِأَحْسَنِ مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

*Wa lā tashtarū bi-ʿahdi'llāhi thamanan qalīlā, innamā ʿinda'llāhi huwa khayrun lakum in kuntum taʿlamūn · Mā ʿindakum yanfadu wa mā ʿinda'llāhi bāq, wa la-najziyanna'lladhīna ṣabarū ajrahum bi-aḥsani mā kānū yaʿmalūn*

"**Do not sell God's covenant for a small price. What is with God is better for you, if only you knew.** · **What you have runs out; what is with God endures. And We shall surely give those who were patient their reward according to the best of what they used to do.**"

### مَا عِندَكُمْ يَنفَدُ وَمَا عِندَ ٱللَّهِ بَاقٍ

**The sentence is built as two nominal clauses, in exact symmetry:**

| | What you have | What is with God |
|---|---|---|
| Expression | *Mā **ʿindakum*** | *Mā **ʿinda'llāh*** |
| Predicate | ***Yanfadu*** — **a verb**, imperfect | ***Bāqin*** — **a noun**, an active participle |

**And the difference of predicate must be recorded, and it is a point of word order:** the first predicate is **a verb**, the second **a noun**.

**In Arabic a verbal sentence conveys *becoming and renewal*, a nominal sentence *fixity and continuance*.** The lexicographers record this consistently.

**I give this as my own reading, its ground being the form of the two predicates:** the verse gives the running out as **an action in progress** and the enduring as **a state.** **The difference, then, is not in the quantity of the two things — it is that the one is at work and the other at rest.**

**The root ن-ف-د:** to come to the end of a thing, to give out. **The root was treated at `031-lokman.md`, 31/27 (*mā nafidat kalimātu'llāh*);** I rely on that treatment. **And there what does not run out was *kalimāt*; here what does not run out is *what is with God*.**

**بِأَحْسَنِ مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ** — and the measure of the recompense must be recorded: **according to the *best* of what they did.**

**I give this as an observation:** the measure is not the average of what was done but **the best of it.** **And this is from the same root as the word *iḥsān* in the ninetieth verse** (ح-س-ن). **Having commanded *iḥsān*, the sūra gives the recompense too in a word from the same root.**

---

## 16/97 — مَنْ عَمِلَ صَٰلِحًا مِّن ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنثَىٰ وَهُوَ مُؤْمِنٌ فَلَنُحْيِيَنَّهُۥ حَيَوٰةً طَيِّبَةً وَلَنَجْزِيَنَّهُمْ أَجْرَهُم بِأَحْسَنِ مَا كَانُوا۟ يَعْمَلُونَ

*Man ʿamila ṣāliḥan min dhakarin aw unthā wa huwa muʾminun fa-la-nuḥyiyannahū ḥayātan ṭayyiba, wa la-najziyannahum ajrahum bi-aḥsani mā kānū yaʿmalūn*

"**Whoever does righteous work, male or female, and is a believer — We shall surely give him a good life, and We shall surely give them their reward according to the best of what they used to do.**"

### مِّن ذَكَرٍ أَوْ أُنثَىٰ

**This verse has been named at many places in this commentary and used as the ground of a principle:** **in `056-vakia.md`, in `078-nebe.md` and in `043-zuhruf.md`** — **"throughout the Qurʾān it is stated openly that the recompense is not divided according to sex."** **I rely on those treatments and complete the ring here, from the verse's own place.**

**And the wording of the verse must be recorded, because it stands in a place where recording it looks unnecessary:**

**The sentence begins *man ʿamila ṣāliḥan*. *Man* is already inclusive — "whoever". Even so the verse adds: *min dhakarin aw unthā*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** grammatically the addition is not required. **That it has been put in shows it was put in for the sake of the record itself.**

**And the verse's place within the sūra must be recorded:** in verses fifty-seven to fifty-nine a society's stance towards a female child was described. **In the ninety-seventh verse it is said outright that the recompense does not depend on sex.**

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is that the two passages stand in the same sūra:** the sūra first diagnoses a stance, then lays down a principle that removes the ground of that stance. **The distance between them is thirty-eight verses, but both are within the same sūra.**

### حَيَوٰةً طَيِّبَةً

**The root ط-ي-ب occurred in the thirty-second verse as *ṭayyibīn* and in the seventy-second as *ṭayyibāt*. Here it is used a third time, of life.**

| Verse | Word | What it qualifies |
|---|---|---|
| **32** | *Ṭayyibīn* | **The state of the dying** |
| **72** | *Ṭayyibāt* | **Provisions** |
| **97** | *Ḥayātan **ṭayyiba*** | **Life** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And as was recorded at `061-saff.md`, 61/12, the shared idea of the root is this: what is acceptable, what sits well within.**

**And the exegetes differ over the content of the construction:**

| View | Where the *ḥayātan ṭayyiba* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **In this world** — contentment, peace, breadth of heart |
| 2 | **In the grave, in the interworld** |
| 3 | **In the hereafter** |

**All three views are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**But a note must be entered in point of word order, and I give it as an observation:** the sentence enumerates two recompenses and separates them with a *wāw* — *fa-la-**nuḥyiyannahū** ḥayātan ṭayyiba* **and** *la-**najziyannahum** ajrahum*. **Two distinct verbs, two distinct recompenses.** **This division suggests that the first recompense lies somewhere other than the second.** **This is an observation; it is not a preference within the disagreement.**

---

## 16/98-100 — فَإِذَا قَرَأْتَ ٱلْقُرْءَانَ فَٱسْتَعِذْ بِٱللَّهِ مِنَ ٱلشَّيْطَٰنِ ٱلرَّجِيمِ · إِنَّهُۥ لَيْسَ لَهُۥ سُلْطَٰنٌ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَعَلَىٰ رَبِّهِمْ يَتَوَكَّلُونَ · إِنَّمَا سُلْطَٰنُهُۥ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ يَتَوَلَّوْنَهُۥ وَٱلَّذِينَ هُم بِهِۦ مُشْرِكُونَ

*Fa-idhā qaraʾta'l-qurʾāna fa'staʿidh bi'llāhi mina'l-shayṭāni'l-rajīm · Innahū laysa lahū sulṭānun ʿala'lladhīna āmanū wa ʿalā rabbihim yatawakkalūn · Innamā sulṭānuhū ʿala'lladhīna yatawallawnahū wa'lladhīna hum bihī mushrikūn*

"**When you read the Qurʾān, seek refuge with God from the outcast Satan.** · **He has no power over those who believe and rely upon their Lord.** · **His power is only over those who take him as a patron and who associate him with God.**"

### فَٱسْتَعِذْ بِٱللَّهِ

**عَوْذ** — root **ع-و-ذ**: **to take refuge, to get behind something.** **The root was treated in detail in `113-felak.md` and `114-nas.md`, by way of those sūras' names;** I do not repeat it here.

**And the same command occurred at `041-fussilet.md`, 41/36** (*wa immā yanzaghannaka mina'l-shayṭāni nazghun fa'staʿidh bi'llāh*), **and it was recorded there:** the command came immediately after the hardest counsel of all (to meet evil with good) — **it was saying "where the counsel will crack first".** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The difference here must be recorded:**

| | **Fuṣṣilat 41/36** | **al-Naḥl 16/98** |
|---|---|---|
| When | *Immā **yanzaghannaka** … nazghun* — **when a prompting comes** | *Idhā **qaraʾta**'l-qurʾān* — **when you read** |
| The kind of condition | **Contingent** — if it happens | **Tied to an act** — at every reading |

**I give this as an observation:** in Fuṣṣilat the seeking of refuge stands in the face of an **event**; in al-Naḥl it stands before a **work.** **The second is a preparation made without waiting for the danger.**

**A note is needed on the tense as well: *idhā qaraʾta* — perfect.** **The lexicographers record that it has the sense "when you are about to read", and that *idhā* conveys the future.** **This is the widespread explanation.** **Some exegetes also transmit the reading "after you have read"; this view is a minority one.** **I transmit the disagreement, impose no preference, and issue no legal ruling.**

**رَجِيم** — root **ر-ج-م**: **to stone, to pelt; and from this, to drive off, to banish.** **The root was treated at `067-mulk.md`, 67/5 (*rujūman li'l-shayāṭīn*);** I rely on that treatment.

### سُلْطَان — the sense of the word

**Root: س-ل-ط.** The core the lexicographers give: **to have mastery over a thing, to hold the power of disposal over it.** *Sulṭa* — power; *salīṭ* — sharp, penetrating.

**And the word is used in the Qurʾān in two senses: *power, mastery* and *proof, argument*.** **In `040-gafir.md` the construction *bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum* was treated in the second sense** (disputation without ground). **Here the first sense is used.**

**And the construction of the sentence must be recorded: the verse lays down one condition in saying there is no power, and another in saying there is.**

| Over whom | Their attributes |
|---|---|
| **No power** | *Āmanū* **and** *ʿalā rabbihim yatawakkalūn* — **two conditions** |
| **There is power** | *Yatawallawnahū* **and** *bihī mushrikūn* — **two conditions** |

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** two attributes are enumerated on each side, and both are joined with a *wāw*. **Protection, then, is named not with faith alone but with a stance added to faith (*tawakkul*); and the other side not with association alone but with a relationship added to it (*tawallī* — taking as a patron).**

---

## 16/101-102 — وَإِذَا بَدَّلْنَآ ءَايَةً مَّكَانَ ءَايَةٍ وَٱللَّهُ أَعْلَمُ بِمَا يُنَزِّلُ قَالُوٓا۟ إِنَّمَآ أَنتَ مُفْتَرٍۭ بَلْ أَكْثَرُهُمْ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ · قُلْ نَزَّلَهُۥ رُوحُ ٱلْقُدُسِ مِن رَّبِّكَ بِٱلْحَقِّ لِيُثَبِّتَ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ وَهُدًى وَبُشْرَىٰ لِلْمُسْلِمِينَ
