*Wa idhā baddalnā āyatan makāna āyatin wa'llāhu aʿlamu bimā yunazzilu qālū innamā anta muftar, bal aktharuhum lā yaʿlamūn · Qul nazzalahū rūḥu'l-qudusi min rabbika bi'l-ḥaqqi li-yuthabbita'lladhīna āmanū wa hudan wa bushrā li'l-muslimīn*

"**When We put one verse in the place of another — and God knows best what He sends down — they say: 'You are only an inventor.' No, most of them do not know.** · **Say: *rūḥ al-qudus* brought it down from your Lord in truth, to make firm those who believe, and as guidance and glad tidings for those who submit.**"

### How the objection is framed

**The verse records an objection, and the logic of the objection is this: if a text changes, its source cannot be divine.**

**And the answer has two parts:**

| Part | Expression | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Wa'llāhu **aʿlamu bimā yunazzil*** | **A parenthesis** — it comes into the middle of the objection |
| 2 | *Qul **nazzalahū rūḥu'l-qudusi** min rabbika bi'l-ḥaqq* | **The naming of the source** |

**The place of the first part must be recorded: it comes into the middle of the sentence, before the objection has been reported.** **The lexicographers call this *iʿtirāḍiyya* (a parenthetical clause).**

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** in reporting the objection the verse has already put the answer in place beforehand. **The reader reads the objection knowing the answer.**

### رُوحُ ٱلْقُدُس

**In the second verse *bi'l-rūḥ* occurred, and the disagreement was tabulated there** (revelation / Gabriel / prophethood). **In the hundred and second verse the construction becomes definite: *rūḥ al-qudus*.**

**قُدُس** — root **ق-د-س**: **purity, being cleansed.** *Taqdīs* — to hold pure; *muqaddas* — made pure.

**The exegetes differ over whom the construction points to, and it must be set out in a table:**

| View | Who *rūḥ al-qudus* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Gabriel** — transmitted as the view of the majority |
| 2 | **Revelation itself** — the same as the use of the word *rūḥ* in the second verse |

**I do not impose a preference.**

**But an internal note must be entered:** in the second verse it was *yunazzilu'l-**malāʾikata** bi'l-rūḥ* — the angels and the *rūḥ* were named separately. **In the hundred and second the one who brings it down is *rūḥ al-qudus*.** **There is a tension between the two verses, and the exegetes resolve it in two ways.** **No resolution is imposed in this commentary.**

### لِيُثَبِّتَ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟

**The root ث-ب-ت occurred in the ninety-fourth verse** (*baʿda thubūtihā* — the foot's standing firm). **In the hundred and second the same root appears again, this time of the believers: *li-yuthabbita* — to make firm.**

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I give this as an observation:** in the ninety-fourth verse firmness was being **lost**; in the hundred and second it is **given.** **The same root, in two opposite directions.**

**And the closing is the same as that of the eighty-ninth verse: *wa hudan wa bushrā li'l-muslimīn*.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/103 — وَلَقَدْ نَعْلَمُ أَنَّهُمْ يَقُولُونَ إِنَّمَا يُعَلِّمُهُۥ بَشَرٌ لِّسَانُ ٱلَّذِى يُلْحِدُونَ إِلَيْهِ أَعْجَمِىٌّ وَهَٰذَا لِسَانٌ عَرَبِىٌّ مُّبِينٌ

*Wa laqad naʿlamu annahum yaqūlūna innamā yuʿallimuhū bashar, lisānu'lladhī yulḥidūna ilayhi aʿjamiyyun wa hādhā lisānun ʿarabiyyun mubīn*

"**We know well that they say: 'A human being is teaching him.' The tongue of the one they hint at is foreign; and this is a clear Arabic tongue.**"

### The form of the objection and of the answer

**This verse was named at `043-zuhruf.md`, 43/3, and it was recorded there:** *"At al-Naḥl 16/103 the word occurs as an answer to an objection: there *aʿjamī* is set as the opposite of *ʿarabī*."* **I rely on that treatment.**

**And the method of the answer must be recorded, and I give this as an observation:** the verse refutes the claim not morally but **linguistically.** **It does not say "such a thing cannot be"; it says "the tongue of the one you hint at is another tongue".**

**The answer, that is, is taken from inside the addressee's own claim.**

**يُلْحِدُونَ** — root **ل-ح-د**: **to swerve to one side, to bend aside.** *Laḥd* — the niche cut into the side of a grave (the Turkish *lahit* comes from this); *mulḥid* — one who swerves from the straight. **The root was treated at `041-fussilet.md`, 41/40 (*yulḥidūna fī āyātinā*);** I rely on that treatment.

**And the use of the verb here is worth recording:** *yulḥidūna **ilayhi*** — "they lean towards him", that is, they hint at him. **The root's picture of "swerving to the side" produces the sense of "pointing indirectly".**

**أَعْجَمِىّ** — root **ع-ج-م**: **not to be clear in speech, to be unintelligible.** *Aʿjam* — one who cannot state his meaning plainly; **the word names not a people but a *condition of intelligibility*.**

**I record this as an observation about the language, and per the method (`USLUP.md`) I state it separately:** the verse lays down no value-judgement about a language or a people. **The contrast does not lie on the axis *ʿarabī / aʿjamī* but on the axis *intelligible / unintelligible*** — indeed the verse adds the adjective *mubīn* (plain) to the word *ʿarabī*. **The stress falls not on the name of the tongue but on its clarity.**

---

## 16/104-105 — إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِـَٔايَٰتِ ٱللَّهِ لَا يَهْدِيهِمُ ٱللَّهُ وَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ · إِنَّمَا يَفْتَرِى ٱلْكَذِبَ ٱلَّذِينَ لَا يُؤْمِنُونَ بِـَٔايَٰتِ ٱللَّهِ وَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْكَٰذِبُونَ

*Inna'lladhīna lā yuʾminūna bi-āyāti'llāhi lā yahdīhimu'llāhu wa lahum ʿadhābun alīm · Innamā yaftari'l-kadhiba'lladhīna lā yuʾminūna bi-āyāti'llāh, wa ulāʾika humu'l-kādhibūn*

"**Those who do not believe in the signs of God — God does not guide them, and for them there is a painful punishment.** · **Only those who do not believe in the signs of God invent lies; and it is they who are the liars.**"

### The charge turned back

**In the hundred and first verse there was a charge: *innamā anta muftar* — "you are only an inventor."**

**The hundred and fifth gives the same root back in the same form: *innamā yaftari'l-kadhib…***

| Verse | Expression | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **101** | *Innamā anta **muftar*** | **The charge** — from the mouths of the addressees |
| **105** | ***Innamā yaftari'l-kadhib**a'lladhīna lā yuʾminūn* | **The answer** — the same particle, the same root |

**And the particle *innamā* is repeated too.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and the two sentences are built to the same pattern.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** in rejecting the charge the verse does not change the word. **It gives the same word back, with the same particle of restriction.** **The same movement was recorded in `046-ahkaf.md` for the word *ifk*** (what was said in verse 11 was given back in verse 28); **I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 16/106 — مَن كَفَرَ بِٱللَّهِ مِنۢ بَعْدِ إِيمَٰنِهِۦٓ إِلَّا مَنْ أُكْرِهَ وَقَلْبُهُۥ مُطْمَئِنٌّۢ بِٱلْإِيمَٰنِ وَلَٰكِن مَّن شَرَحَ بِٱلْكُفْرِ صَدْرًا فَعَلَيْهِمْ غَضَبٌ مِّنَ ٱللَّهِ وَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌ عَظِيمٌ

*Man kafara bi'llāhi min baʿdi īmānihī illā man ukriha wa qalbuhū muṭmaʾinnun bi'l-īmāni wa lākin man sharaḥa bi'l-kufri ṣadran fa-ʿalayhim ghaḍabun mina'llāhi wa lahum ʿadhābun ʿaẓīm*

"**Whoever denies God after having believed — except one who is compelled while his heart is at rest in faith — and whoever opens his breast to denial: upon them is anger from God, and for them there is a great punishment.**"

### The bringing forward of the exception

**In treating this verse a limit must be set at the outset, per the method (`USLUP.md`): no legal ruling is issued here.** In the classical sources this verse has been counted one of the chief grounds of the law of *ikrāh* (compulsion), and a broad juristic literature has formed around it. **This commentary does not enter that literature.**

**What is treated is what the verse does in its wording. And in point of word order the most striking thing in the verse is where the exception is placed.**

**The skeleton of the sentence is this:**

| Order | Part | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Man kafara bi'llāhi min baʿdi īmānihī* | **The condition** — the case a ruling is to be laid upon |
| 2 | ***Illā man ukriha wa qalbuhū muṭmaʾinnun bi'l-īmān*** | ***THE EXCEPTION*** |
| 3 | *Wa lākin man sharaḥa bi'l-kufri ṣadrā* | **The one the ruling truly falls on** |
| 4 | *Fa-ʿalayhim ghaḍabun mina'llāh…* | **The apodosis** |

**And what must be recorded is this: the exception comes *before* the ruling.**

**The sentence does not say "upon those who do such and such is anger; except those who are compelled". It says: "whoever denies — except one who is compelled — and whoever opens his breast…"**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that order:** the exception comes in before the sentence of judgement is complete. **The dispensation, that is, is given not as a consequence of the ruling but *within the ruling's own definition*.** **Before saying whom the anger is directed at, the verse says whom it is not directed at.**

### Two conditions, two verbs

**And within the exception itself there are two qualifications:**

| Qualification | Expression | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ***Ukriha*** — he was compelled | **Outside** — a passive verb; the compeller is not named |
| 2 | ***Qalbuhū muṭmaʾinnun bi'l-īmān*** | **Inside** — the state of the heart |

**That the verb is passive must be recorded: *ukriha*.** **The one who compels is not named.** **And this is a pattern recorded at many places in this commentary** — at `035-fatir.md`, 35/8, for *zuyyina*, and at `040-gafir.md`, 40/37, for the same verb.

**كَرِهَ / أُكْرِهَ** — root **ك-ر-ه**: **to dislike, not to want.** **In Form IV (*akraha*): to make someone do what he does not want.** **At the centre of the root, then, lies the idea of being *outside one's own wanting*.**

### مُطْمَئِنٌّ بِٱلْإِيمَٰنِ — *iṭmiʾnān*

**The root ط-م-ن was treated at `002-bakara.md`, 2/260, and I rely on that treatment.** What was recorded there:

> **"لِّيَطْمَئِنَّ قَلْبِى — root ط-م-ن: to grow calm, to settle, to come to rest in its place. The lexicographers record the picture of '**a thing settling into a hollow and being held fast**'."**
>
> **"Faith and *iṭmiʾnān* are not the same thing. The one is acceptance, the other a settling within."**

**And this distinction from al-Baqara works exactly in this verse.**

**It must be recorded: the verse does not say "if his heart believes". It says *qalbuhū muṭmaʾinnun bi'l-īmān*.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the distinction drawn in al-Baqara:** what is laid down as the condition of the dispensation is not the presence of faith but faith's **having settled into place.** **That is: the presence of something within which the words spoken outside cannot shift.**

**And the picture in the root gives this openly:** *iṭmiʾnān* is a thing's **settling into a hollow.** To move it, it has to be lifted out. **A pressure coming from outside does not shift what has settled.**

| Place | Whose *iṭmiʾnān* | Its work |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Baqara 2/260** | **Abraham's** — *li-yaṭmaʾinna qalbī* | **Asked for** — a request to be made firm by seeing |
| **al-Naḥl 16/106** | **The compelled man's** — *qalbuhū muṭmaʾinnun* | **Found present** — the condition of the dispensation |

**The same root: in the one a request, in the other a state.** **This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

### شَرَحَ بِٱلْكُفْرِ صَدْرًا — the opposite state

**شَرَحَ** — root **ش-ر-ح**: **to open, to split open and widen.** *Sharḥ* — to open out a text; *inshirāḥ* — the widening of the breast. **The root was treated in detail in `094-insirah.md`, by way of that sūra's name;** I do not repeat it here.

**And the contrast must be recorded, because the verse sets two states squarely against each other:**

| | The one compelled | The one who opens his breast |
|---|---|---|
| The heart | ***Muṭmaʾinn*** — settled, fixed | ***Sharaḥa ṣadrā*** — opened, widened |
| The direction | **Resistance from inside outwards** | **Acceptance from inside outwards** |
| The will | *Ukriha* — **passive**, outside his will | *Sharaḥa* — **active**, his own act |

**All three lines can be verified from the text, and the wording shows it: the verb of the first state is passive, that of the second active.**

**I record this as my own reading, its ground being the voices of the verbs:** in dividing the two cases the verse looks at **who did it.** **The measure is not the word spoken — it is where the word came from.**

### On the accounts of the occasion of revelation

**In the classical sources it is transmitted that this verse was revealed concerning a man who was tortured in the Meccan period, and an account is given.**

**This commentary attaches no personal name to that report**, because I have no verified chain of transmission. **The account itself is present in the sources; to whom it belongs, and its details, are another matter.**

**And I record this: whether the account of the occasion is known or not, the wording of the verse says the same thing.** **The scope of the exception is fixed not by the details of an event but by two words: *ukriha* and *muṭmaʾinn*.**

---

## 16/107-109 — ذَٰلِكَ بِأَنَّهُمُ ٱسْتَحَبُّوا۟ ٱلْحَيَوٰةَ ٱلدُّنْيَا عَلَى ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ وَأَنَّ ٱللَّهَ لَا يَهْدِى ٱلْقَوْمَ ٱلْكَٰفِرِينَ · أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ طَبَعَ ٱللَّهُ عَلَىٰ قُلُوبِهِمْ وَسَمْعِهِمْ وَأَبْصَٰرِهِمْ وَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْغَٰفِلُونَ · لَا جَرَمَ أَنَّهُمْ فِى ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ هُمُ ٱلْخَٰسِرُونَ

*Dhālika bi-annahumu'staḥabbu'l-ḥayāta'l-dunyā ʿala'l-ākhirati wa anna'llāha lā yahdi'l-qawma'l-kāfirīn · Ulāʾika'lladhīna ṭabaʿa'llāhu ʿalā qulūbihim wa samʿihim wa abṣārihim wa ulāʾika humu'l-ghāfilūn · Lā jarama annahum fi'l-ākhirati humu'l-khāsirūn*

"**That is because they preferred the life of this world to the hereafter, and because God does not guide a denying people.** · **Those are they whose hearts, hearing and eyes God has sealed; and it is they who are the heedless.** · **Beyond doubt, in the hereafter it is they who are the losers.**"

### ٱسْتَحَبُّوا۟ — root: ح-ب-ب

**In Form X (*istaḥabba*):** **to prefer, to choose one thing over another.** The primary sense of the root is love; **here Form X conveys the act of choosing.**

**And the ground must be recorded:** the verse names the cause of the ruling in the hundred and sixth verse not as **a question of belief** but as **a preference.** **The measure, then, is not what is believed — it is what is put first.**

### The sealing of the triad

**The sealing of the heart, the hearing and the eyes is the opposite of the triad given in the seventy-eighth verse. And the order has been reversed:**

| Verse | Order | Verb |
|---|---|---|
| **78** | *al-Samʿ → al-abṣār → **al-afʾida*** | *Jaʿala lakum* — **given** |
| **108** | ***Qulūbihim** → samʿihim → abṣārihim* | *Ṭabaʿa'llāhu* — **sealed** |

**The difference of order must be recorded, and I give this as my own reading:** in the giving they were enumerated from outside inward — hearing, sight, apprehension. **In the sealing they are enumerated from inside outward — the heart first.**

**The closing thus begins not at the organs of reception but at the centre of appraisal.** **This agrees with the sūra's own diagnosis: in the eighty-third verse the trouble was named *denial after recognition* — that is, not in the senses but at the stage of acceptance.**

**The root ط-ب-ع:** to stamp a seal, to set a mark. **The root was treated at `030-rum.md`, 30/58-59 (*ka-dhālika yaṭbaʿu'llāhu ʿalā qulūbi'lladhīna lā yaʿlamūn*);** I rely on that treatment. **And at `045-casiye.md`, 45/23, the same triad occurred with *khatm* and *ghishāwa*.**

**And the construction *lā jarama* occurs for the second and last time in the sūra** (23 and 109). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

---

## 16/110-111 — ثُمَّ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ لِلَّذِينَ هَاجَرُوا۟ مِنۢ بَعْدِ مَا فُتِنُوا۟ ثُمَّ جَٰهَدُوا۟ وَصَبَرُوٓا۟ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ مِنۢ بَعْدِهَا لَغَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ · يَوْمَ تَأْتِى كُلُّ نَفْسٍ تُجَٰدِلُ عَن نَّفْسِهَا وَتُوَفَّىٰ كُلُّ نَفْسٍ مَّا عَمِلَتْ وَهُمْ لَا يُظْلَمُونَ

*Thumma inna rabbaka lilladhīna hājarū min baʿdi mā futinū thumma jāhadū wa ṣabarū inna rabbaka min baʿdihā la-ghafūrun raḥīm · Yawma taʾtī kullu nafsin tujādilu ʿan nafsihā wa tuwaffā kullu nafsin mā ʿamilat wa hum lā yuẓlamūn*

"**Then your Lord — for those who emigrated after they had been put to the test, and then strove and were patient: your Lord after all that is surely most forgiving, most merciful.** · **On the day when every soul comes pleading for itself, and every soul is paid in full for what it did, and they are not wronged.**"

### Three verbs, one sequence

**The verse ranges three verbs with *thumma* and *wāw*:**

| Order | Verb | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Hājarū min baʿdi mā **futinū*** | **Emigration** — after the testing |
| 2 | *Thumma **jāhadū*** | **Striving** |
| 3 | *Wa **ṣabarū*** | **Patience** |

**And it must be compared with the forty-first verse:**

| | **Verses 41-42** | **Verse 110** |
|---|---|---|
| The cause of the emigration | *Min baʿdi mā **ẓulimū*** — after they had been wronged | *Min baʿdi mā **futinū*** — after they had been put to the test |
| The verb added | *Ṣabarū wa ʿalā rabbihim **yatawakkalūn*** | *Jāhadū wa **ṣabarū*** |
| The closing | *Wa la-ajru'l-ākhirati **akbar*** | *La-**ghafūrun raḥīm*** |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and I record this as my own reading, its ground being the difference of the closings:** the forty-first verse closes with a **reward**, the hundred and tenth with **forgiveness.**

**And the reason for this lies in the context:** the hundred and tenth verse comes directly after the passage on compulsion in the hundred and sixth. **The choice of the word "forgiving" is thus connected with the four verses before it.** **This is an observation, and it rests on the two passages standing back to back.**

### تُجَٰدِلُ عَن نَّفْسِهَا — a return to the fourth verse

**And one of the plainest rings in the sūra is completed here.**

**In the fourth verse man was named *khaṣīmun mubīn* (an open disputant).**
**In the hundred and eleventh the same verb is used for the last scene of disputation: *tujādilu ʿan nafsihā* — "it pleads for itself."**

| Verse | Word | Root | What it disputes for |
|---|---|---|---|
| **4** | *Khaṣīmun mubīn* | خ-ص-م | **Not specified** — as an attribute |
| **111** | *Tujādilu **ʿan nafsihā*** | ج-د-ل | ***For itself*** |
| **125** | *Wa **jādilhum** bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | ج-د-ل | **With another, in the best manner** |

**All three occurrences can be verified from the text, and they stand at three different places in the sūra.**

**I record this as my own reading, my ground being this threefold set:** the sūra first identifies disputation as **an attribute** (4), then shows that attribute in its most helpless state (111) — a soul pleads for itself and the plea avails nothing — **and finally gives that same tendency a direction (125).**

**The sūra, then, neither ignores nor forbids man's most conspicuous trait; it sets it to a task.**

**And the line recorded at `018-kehf.md`, 18/54 (*akthara shayʾin jadalā*) is completed here:** it was said there, "the tendency is identified, then a limit is set". **In al-Naḥl the tendency is identified, its outcome is shown, and a method is given.**

---

## 16/112-113 — وَضَرَبَ ٱللَّهُ مَثَلًا قَرْيَةً كَانَتْ ءَامِنَةً مُّطْمَئِنَّةً يَأْتِيهَا رِزْقُهَا رَغَدًا مِّن كُلِّ مَكَانٍ فَكَفَرَتْ بِأَنْعُمِ ٱللَّهِ فَأَذَٰقَهَا ٱللَّهُ لِبَاسَ ٱلْجُوعِ وَٱلْخَوْفِ بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَصْنَعُونَ · وَلَقَدْ جَآءَهُمْ رَسُولٌ مِّنْهُمْ فَكَذَّبُوهُ فَأَخَذَهُمُ ٱلْعَذَابُ وَهُمْ ظَٰلِمُونَ

*Wa ḍaraba'llāhu mathalan qaryatan kānat āminatan muṭmaʾinnatan yaʾtīhā rizquhā raghadan min kulli makānin fa-kafarat bi-anʿumi'llāhi fa-adhāqaha'llāhu libāsa'l-jūʿi wa'l-khawfi bimā kānū yaṣnaʿūn · Wa laqad jāʾahum rasūlun minhum fa-kadhdhabūhu fa-akhadhahumu'l-ʿadhābu wa hum ẓālimūn*

"**God has coined the likeness of a town: it was safe and at ease, its provision coming to it in plenty from every side. Then it was ungrateful for God's favours, and God made it taste the garment of hunger and fear for what they had wrought.** · **A messenger from among themselves had come to them, and they cried him a liar; so the punishment seized them while they were doing wrong.**"

### آمِنَة مُّطْمَئِنَّة — the secure town

**Both attributes were treated in this commentary, in two separate places. I rely on those treatments:**

**At `028-kasas.md`, 28/57** (*a-wa-lam numakkin lahum ḥaraman āminan yujbā ilayhi thamarātu kulli shayʾ*) **and at `029-ankebut.md`, 29/67** (*a-wa-lam yaraw annā jaʿalnā ḥaraman āminan wa yutakhaṭṭafu'l-nāsu min ḥawlihim*) **the same fact was treated and the two verses compared:**

| Place | Expression | Who is snatched away / what is said |
|---|---|---|
| al-ʿAnkabūt 29/67 | *Wa **yutakhaṭṭafu'l-nāsu** min ḥawlihim* | **Those around them** — they themselves are safe |
| al-Qaṣaṣ 28/57 | *Nutakhaṭṭaf min arḍinā* | **Themselves** — what they feared |
| **al-Naḥl 16/112** | ***Kānat** āminatan muṭmaʾinna* | **The past tense** — the safety has been **lost** |

**And what was recorded in the al-Qaṣaṣ section can be carried over here:** the objection was framed not upon belief but upon **anxiety about safety**, and the answer was given on the same plane — *who built the place where you are already safe?*

**al-Naḥl takes a step further, and I record this as my own reading, my ground being the tense of the verb:** al-Qaṣaṣ and al-ʿAnkabūt name the safety as **a state still holding.** **al-Naḥl says *kānat* — a perfect.** **The third verse thus shows that the condition the first two describe can be lost.**

**And the verse gives it as a *likeness* — the town is not named.** **This must be recorded:** the sentence begins with *ḍaraba'llāhu mathalan*. **What is told is set down not as a particular event of history but as an example.** **And the exegetes differ over which town it is; no name is given in this commentary.**

**مُّطْمَئِنَّة** — and the root ط-م-ن occurs for the second time in the sūra. **In the hundred and sixth verse it was *qalbuhū muṭmaʾinnun bi'l-īmān*.**

| Verse | Whose *iṭmiʾnān* | Its outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **106** | **The heart's** — by faith | **It held** — the condition of the dispensation |
| **112** | **The town's** — by provision | **It did not hold** — ingratitude came |

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and I give this as my own reading:** the same word, once inside and once outside. **The *iṭmiʾnān* within does not shift even under pressure; the *iṭmiʾnān* without is lost in the midst of ease.** **Six verses stand between the two.**

### لِبَاسَ ٱلْجُوعِ وَٱلْخَوْفِ — "the garment of hunger and fear"

**I treat this comparison in detail, because its construction is layered twice over.**

**The sentence is this: *fa-adhāqaha'llāhu libāsa'l-jūʿi wa'l-khawf* — "God made it taste the garment of hunger and fear."**

**There is a mismatch between the two verb-object relations, and it is deliberate:**

| Element | Root | To which sense it belongs |
|---|---|---|
| ***Adhāqa*** — made taste | ذ-و-ق | **Taste** — the mouth |
| ***Libās*** — garment | ل-ب-س | **Touch** — the skin |
| *al-Jūʿ* — hunger | ج-و-ع | **Within** — the stomach |
| *al-Khawf* — fear | خ-و-ف | **Within** — the mind |

**Four elements, four different domains. And the lexicographers call constructions of this kind *istiʿāra* (borrowing).**

**Now I open the construction of the comparison step by step, and I give it as my own reading, my ground being this mismatch of the words:**

**One — hunger and fear are normally things *felt*.** They happen inside; no one else sees them.

**Two — the verse makes them something *worn*.** *Libās* — a garment. **And a garment has two properties: it wraps the body, and it is seen from outside.**

**Three — the wrapping property: a garment is not on one part of the body but on all of it.** **Hunger and fear thus cease to be a condition of a person and become something that covers him entirely.**

**Four — the property of visibility: a garment is what a person shows to the outside.** **Two states that would be expected to stay within thus become the person's appearance.** **The identity of the town is now its hunger and its fear.**

**Five — and the verb turns this round once more: *adhāqa* — "made taste".** **The thing worn is tasted.** **It does not merely wrap from outside; it enters within as well.**

**The comparison as a whole gives this picture:** hunger and fear are first a condition within (the stomach and the mind), then a dress without (*libās*), then a taste within again (*adhāqa*). **The words draw a circle.**

**And this reading is an observation that can be drawn from the text; the mismatch of the sensory domains of the words is an observable fact.**

**And the other occurrences of the root ل-ب-س in the sūra must be recorded:**

| Verse | Expression | The garment of what |
|---|---|---|
| **14** | *Ḥilyatan **talbasūnahā*** | **Ornament** — brought out of the sea |
| **81** | ***Sarābīla** taqīkumu'l-ḥarr* | **Garments** — protection (a different word, but the same domain) |
| **112** | ***Libāsa**'l-jūʿi wa'l-khawf* | **Hunger and fear** |

**This is a weave verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading:** the sūra enumerated clothing twice as a **favour** (14, 81). **In the hundred and twelfth verse the same domain becomes the name of the loss of favour.** **An item from the list of the enumeration is turned over and made the name of the punishment.**

### بِأَنْعُمِ ٱللَّهِ — a plural of the plural

**It must be recorded: the word is not *niʿmat* but ***anʿum***.**

**The lexicographers count *anʿum* as a plural of *niʿma***, and some explain the difference between it and the plural *niʿam* by the distinction between **the plural of paucity and the plural of multitude.** **I transmit this distinction as a tendency the lexicographers record; I do not impose a preference.**

**And what matters is this: throughout the sūra *niʿma* was used in the singular** (18, 53, 71, 72, 83) **and occurred as *niʿmatahū* in the eighty-first verse.** **In the hundred and twelfth the plural comes for the first and only time.**

**I give this as an observation, and it is a countable fact:** in naming favour as a single whole the sūra never uses a plural. **It uses the plural only of favours that are denied, and only in one place.**

### بِمَا كَانُوا۟ يَصْنَعُونَ

**The root ص-ن-ع:** to do a thing with skill, in an ordered way. *Ṣunʿ* — a piece of craftsmanship; *ṣināʿa* — a craft. **The root occurred at `035-fatir.md`, 35/8 (*inna'llāha ʿalīmun bimā yaṣnaʿūn*);** I rely on that treatment.

**And the choice of the word is worth recording:** the verse does not say *bimā kānū **yaʿmalūn*** (what they did). **It says *yaṣnaʿūn*** — **what they made with skill, by repetition.**

**I give this as an observation about the language:** the word names ingratitude not as a stumble but as **a habit, a craft.**

---

## 16/114-115 — فَكُلُوا۟ مِمَّا رَزَقَكُمُ ٱللَّهُ حَلَٰلًا طَيِّبًا وَٱشْكُرُوا۟ نِعْمَتَ ٱللَّهِ إِن كُنتُمْ إِيَّاهُ تَعْبُدُونَ · إِنَّمَا حَرَّمَ عَلَيْكُمُ ٱلْمَيْتَةَ وَٱلدَّمَ وَلَحْمَ ٱلْخِنزِيرِ وَمَآ أُهِلَّ لِغَيْرِ ٱللَّهِ بِهِۦ فَمَنِ ٱضْطُرَّ غَيْرَ بَاغٍ وَلَا عَادٍ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*Fa-kulū mimmā razaqakumu'llāhu ḥalālan ṭayyibā, wa'shkurū niʿmata'llāhi in kuntum iyyāhu taʿbudūn · Innamā ḥarrama ʿalaykumu'l-maytata wa'l-dama wa laḥma'l-khinzīri wa mā uhilla li-ghayri'llāhi bih, fa-mani'ḍṭurra ghayra bāghin wa lā ʿādin fa-inna'llāha ghafūrun raḥīm*

"**Eat of what God has provided you, lawful and wholesome; and give thanks for God's favour, if it is Him you serve.** · **He has forbidden you only carrion, blood, the flesh of swine, and what has been consecrated to other than God. But whoever is driven by necessity — not desiring it and not going beyond the bound — God is most forgiving, most merciful.**"

### The context of the verse

**It must be recorded: these two verses come directly after the parable of the secure town and are connected with it.**

**In the parable the town had been ungrateful *bi-anʿumi'llāh*. The hundred and fourteenth verse answers with the same word: *wa'shkurū niʿmata'llāh*.**

**This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra. And I record this as my own reading, its ground being that the two passages stand back to back:** the sūra sets a passage on the lawful and the forbidden after an account of ingratitude. **The rulings, that is, come as the outcome of an accounting.**

### The same list, a fourth time

**This list of four items occurs in four places in the Qurʾān: al-Baqara 2/173, al-Māʾida 5/3, al-Anʿām 6/145, and here.**

**The list was treated at `002-bakara.md`, 2/173;** I rely on that treatment.

**And what must be recorded is that the list is given with a particle of restriction: *innamā* — "only, no more than".**

**I give this as an observation about the word order:** the sentence does not build a list of prohibitions; **it draws a boundary.** **And this is the subject of the next section (116): declaring things forbidden on one's own authority.**

### فَمَنِ ٱضْطُرَّ غَيْرَ بَاغٍ وَلَا عَادٍ

**And the dispensation has the same structure as the dispensation in the hundred and sixth verse, and this must be recorded:**

| | **Verse 106** | **Verse 115** |
|---|---|---|
| The dispensation | *Illā man **ukriha*** | *Fa-mani'**ḍṭurra*** |
| The voice of the verb | **Passive** — he was compelled | **Passive** — he was driven to necessity |
| The qualification | *Wa qalbuhū **muṭmaʾinn*** | ***Ghayra bāghin wa lā ʿādin*** |
| The closing | *Wa lākin man sharaḥa bi'l-kufri ṣadrā* | *Fa-inna'llāha **ghafūrun raḥīm*** |

**Both dispensations rest on the same skeleton: a passive verb plus an inward qualification.** **This is an overlap verifiable within the sūra.**

**اضْطُرَّ** — root **ض-ر-ر**: harm. **In Form VIII, passive: "he was forced into a strait, he was left with no choice."** **And that the verb is passive shows that the necessity is not the person's own choosing** — just as with *ukriha*.

**غَيْرَ بَاغٍ وَلَا عَادٍ** — two qualifications. **The root ب-غ-ي was enumerated in the ninetieth verse among the things forbidden** (*wa'l-baghy*). **Here it comes back as the limit of the dispensation.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**And the lexicographers dwell on the difference between the two words, and two explanations are transmitted:**

| Explanation | *Bāgh* | *ʿĀd* |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **One who seeks it out willingly** — when he has no need | **One who exceeds the measure** — eating more than he needs |
| 2 | **One bent on wrongdoing** | **One who crosses the bound** |

**Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference, and I issue no legal ruling.**

---

## 16/116-117 — وَلَا تَقُولُوا۟ لِمَا تَصِفُ أَلْسِنَتُكُمُ ٱلْكَذِبَ هَٰذَا حَلَٰلٌ وَهَٰذَا حَرَامٌ لِّتَفْتَرُوا۟ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ ٱلْكَذِبَ إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَفْتَرُونَ عَلَى ٱللَّهِ ٱلْكَذِبَ لَا يُفْلِحُونَ · مَتَٰعٌ قَلِيلٌ وَلَهُمْ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ

*Wa lā taqūlū li-mā taṣifu alsinatukumu'l-kadhiba hādhā ḥalālun wa hādhā ḥarāmun li-taftarū ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhib, inna'lladhīna yaftarūna ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhiba lā yufliḥūn · Matāʿun qalīlun wa lahum ʿadhābun alīm*

"**And do not say, of what your tongues falsely describe, 'this is lawful and this is forbidden', so as to invent lies against God. Those who invent lies against God do not prosper.** · **A brief enjoyment; and for them there is a painful punishment.**"

### تَصِفُ أَلْسِنَتُكُمُ ٱلْكَذِبَ

**The construction must be recorded, and the same one occurred in the sixty-second verse: *wa taṣifu alsinatuhumu'l-kadhib*.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra, and the person changes: there "their tongues", here "your tongues".**

**And the agent must be recorded: the subject of the verb is *alsinatukum* — the tongues, not the persons.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** the verse does not say "do not lie". **It says "your tongues describe a lie".** **The act is thus detached from the person and ascribed to the organ** — as though the tongue produced a ruling of its own.

**The root و-ص-ف:** to describe a thing by qualifying it. **And the work of the word here:** the ruling rests not upon a piece of knowledge but upon **a description.**

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`)

**This verse lays down a principle of method, and it bears directly on the limit this commentary sets itself.**

**What is forbidden is the pronouncing of a ruling of lawful and forbidden without ground.** **And the verse says what that amounts to: *li-taftarū ʿala'llāhi'l-kadhib* — to invent a lie against God.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that clause:** the verse names a ruling laid down on one's own authority not as an error but as **an attribution.** **The matter, then, is not the correctness of the ruling; it is to whom it is ascribed.**

**And this line was treated at `002-bakara.md`, 2/169** (*wa an taqūlū ʿala'llāhi mā lā taʿlamūn*); I rely on that treatment.

**One of the reasons no legal ruling is issued in this commentary is the clause written in the method. This verse is one of the textual grounds of that clause.**

---

## 16/118 — وَعَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ هَادُوا۟ حَرَّمْنَا مَا قَصَصْنَا عَلَيْكَ مِن قَبْلُ وَمَا ظَلَمْنَٰهُمْ وَلَٰكِن كَانُوٓا۟ أَنفُسَهُمْ يَظْلِمُونَ

*Wa ʿala'lladhīna hādū ḥarramnā mā qaṣaṣnā ʿalayka min qabl, wa mā ẓalamnāhum wa lākin kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn*

"**And to those who are Jews We forbade what We recounted to you before. We did not wrong them; it was themselves they were wronging.**"

### The context of the verse

**It must be recorded: the verse comes after the prohibition of the preceding verse (declaring things lawful or forbidden on one's own authority).**

**And it gives an example: prohibitions laid down for one community in particular.** **The verse thus shows by a historical example that the source of a ruling is God.**

**And the end of the sentence must be recorded: *wa mā ẓalamnāhum wa lākin kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn*.** **This sentence occurred word for word in the thirty-third verse.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

| Verse | Sentence | About whom |
|---|---|---|
| **33** | *Wa mā ẓalamahumu'llāhu wa lākin kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn* | **Peoples of the past** |
| **118** | *Wa mā **ẓalamnā**hum wa lākin kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn* | **A community** — those on whom a particular prohibition was laid |

**The difference is one word: in the thirty-third verse *Allāh* is named openly; in the hundred and eighteenth the first person plural is used.**

### A note required by the method (`USLUP.md`)

**This verse lays down no wholesale ruling about a community, and this needs to be written openly.**

**The evidence within the text is this:** the subject of the verse is **a list of prohibitions**, not an assessment of an identity. **And the second half of the sentence ties the ruling to an act: *kānū anfusahum yaẓlimūn*.**

**The clause of the method holds here exactly:** *"No wholesale ruling is laid down about an ethnic or religious group; what the verse describes are attributes, and whoever bears that attribute is included in it."* **And at `029-ankebut.md`, 29/46-47, it was treated in detail how this care is kept; I rely on that treatment.**

---

## 16/119 — ثُمَّ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ لِلَّذِينَ عَمِلُوا۟ ٱلسُّوٓءَ بِجَهَٰلَةٍ ثُمَّ تَابُوا۟ مِنۢ بَعْدِ ذَٰلِكَ وَأَصْلَحُوٓا۟ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ مِنۢ بَعْدِهَا لَغَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*Thumma inna rabbaka lilladhīna ʿamilu'l-sūʾa bi-jahālatin thumma tābū min baʿdi dhālika wa aṣlaḥū inna rabbaka min baʿdihā la-ghafūrun raḥīm*

"**Then your Lord — for those who did evil in ignorance, then repented after that and set things right: your Lord after all that is surely most forgiving, most merciful.**"

### The pattern of the hundred and tenth verse

**The sentence stands on the same skeleton as the hundred and tenth verse, and this is a repetition verifiable within the sūra:**

| | **Verse 110** | **Verse 119** |
|---|---|---|
| The opening | *Thumma inna rabbaka lilladhīna…* | ***The same*** |
| Who | *Hājarū … jāhadū wa ṣabarū* | *ʿAmilu'l-sūʾa bi-jahālatin … tābū wa **aṣlaḥū*** |
| The closing | *Inna rabbaka min baʿdihā la-ghafūrun raḥīm* | ***The same*** |

**The same pattern, for two different groups.** **And I record this as my own reading, my ground being this symmetry:** the first group are those who were wronged and emigrated, the second those who did evil and repented. **The sūra meets both with the same sentence.**

### تَابُوا۟ وَأَصْلَحُوا۟ — two verbs

**And repentance is not named alone; a second verb is added beside it: *wa aṣlaḥū*.**

**This structure was treated at `042-sura.md`, 42/40, and it was recorded there:**

> **"Pardon on its own may be a *withdrawal* — a man does not take his right, he cuts the relation, he goes away. Technically that is pardon, but it does not repair what was broken. The condition of *iṣlāḥ* ties pardon to a repair."**

**The same structure works here for repentance, and I give it as an observation:** repentance too may be a withdrawal on its own. **The condition of *iṣlāḥ* ties it to a repair.**

**And the root ص-ل-ح was treated at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/9-10:** to make what has been spoiled **fit for use again.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**بِجَهَٰلَة** — two explanations of the word are transmitted:

| Explanation | What *jahāla* is |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Ignorance** — doing a thing without knowing the ruling |
| 2 | **Thoughtlessness, anger, excess** — knowingly, but without reflection |

**The second explanation rests on the root's use in Arabic: *jahl* is the opposite of forbearance (*ḥilm*) as much as it is the opposite of knowledge.** **Both explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

---

## 16/120-123 — إِنَّ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ كَانَ أُمَّةً قَانِتًا لِّلَّهِ حَنِيفًا وَلَمْ يَكُ مِنَ ٱلْمُشْرِكِينَ · شَاكِرًا لِّأَنْعُمِهِ ٱجْتَبَىٰهُ وَهَدَىٰهُ إِلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ · وَءَاتَيْنَٰهُ فِى ٱلدُّنْيَا حَسَنَةً وَإِنَّهُۥ فِى ٱلْـَٔاخِرَةِ لَمِنَ ٱلصَّٰلِحِينَ · ثُمَّ أَوْحَيْنَآ إِلَيْكَ أَنِ ٱتَّبِعْ مِلَّةَ إِبْرَٰهِيمَ حَنِيفًا وَمَا كَانَ مِنَ ٱلْمُشْرِكِينَ

*Inna Ibrāhīma kāna ummatan qānitan li'llāhi ḥanīfā, wa lam yaku mina'l-mushrikīn · Shākiran li-anʿumih, ijtabāhu wa hadāhu ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm · Wa ātaynāhu fi'l-dunyā ḥasana, wa innahū fi'l-ākhirati la-mina'l-ṣāliḥīn · Thumma awḥaynā ilayka ani'ttabiʿ millata Ibrāhīma ḥanīfā, wa mā kāna mina'l-mushrikīn*

"**Abraham was a community, devoutly obedient to God, *ḥanīf*; and he was not one of those who associate.** · **He was thankful for His favours. God chose him and guided him to a straight path.** · **We gave him good in this world; and in the hereafter he is among the righteous.** · **Then We revealed to you: 'Follow the way of Abraham, as a *ḥanīf*.' He was not one of those who associate.**"

### كَانَ أُمَّةً — "*umma*" used of a single man

**The word is *umma*, and it is used of a single man. This is a place the lexicographers dwell on, and the explanations must be set out in a table:**

| Explanation | What *umma* is here |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Worth a whole community by himself** — an intensification |
| 2 | **A leader, an *imām*** — one who is followed |
| 3 | **A man in whom good is gathered** — *umma* in the sense of "what is gathered" |

**All three explanations are transmitted; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the sense of the root must be recorded: أ-م-م — to head towards, to intend; *amma* — he headed for; *imām* — one who goes in front; *umm* — the origin, the source.** **In the root, then, there is both the idea of *direction* and the idea of *source*.**

### شَاكِرًا لِّأَنْعُمِهِ — the link with the sūra's backbone

**This is one of the most striking connections in the sūra, and it must be recorded.**

**The word *anʿum* occurs twice in the sūra, and the two stand against each other:**

| Verse | Expression | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **112** | *Fa-**kafarat** bi-**anʿum**i'llāh* | **The town** — it was ungrateful |
| **121** | ***Shākiran** li-**anʿum**ih* | **Abraham** — he gave thanks |

**The same plural word, with two opposite verbs.** **And nine verses stand between them.** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra and a countable fact — the word occurs in these two places only.**

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is these two occurrences:** the sūra ties its enumeration of favours to a town as its negative case and to a man as its positive one. **The hundred and twenty-first verse stands like an answer given to the whole enumeration of the sūra: what the one gives thanks for and the other denies is the same thing.**

**And the purpose of the sūra's enumeration in the eighty-first verse was *laʿallakum tuslimūn*.** **And Abraham's attribute is *qānitan li'llāhi ḥanīfā* — bowed in obedience.** **The two expressions look towards the same place.**

### حَنِيف — root: ح-ن-ف

**The root was treated in detail at `030-rum.md`, 30/30;** I rely on that treatment. **The word's sense of "inclining in one direction" and its relation to *fiṭra* were analysed there.** **I do not repeat it here.**

**What belongs here: the word occurs twice, in two verses (120 and 123), and in both together with the same sentence: *wa mā kāna mina'l-mushrikīn*.**

**This repetition must be recorded, and I give it as an observation about the word order:** *ḥanīf* is a positive description, *wa mā kāna mina'l-mushrikīn* a negative one. **The verse uses the two together, twice.** **The description is thus completed by saying both what he was and what he was not.**

**قَانِت** — root **ق-ن-ت**: **unbroken obedience, uninterrupted submission.** **The root occurred at `030-rum.md`, 30/26 (*kullun lahū qānitūn*).**

**ٱجْتَبَىٰهُ** — root **ج-ب-ي**: **to gather, to bring together, to pick out and take.** *Jibāya* — the collecting of a tax. **In Form VIII: to choose — that is, to single out and gather from among many.**

### ثُمَّ أَوْحَيْنَآ إِلَيْكَ

**And the particle *thumma* must be recorded.** In Arabic *thumma* conveys sequence and **an interval.** **The verse thus marks with a particle the span of time between Abraham and the one addressed.**

**And the content of the command must be recorded: *ittabiʿ millata Ibrāhīm* — "follow the way of Abraham."**

**The root م-ل-ل:** *milla*, the way held to, the manner followed. **The lexicographers record its relation to the sense "dictated, written down at another's word" (*imlāʾ*)** — that is, a way taken from another and followed.

**I give this as an observation:** the command conveys not the following of a person but the following of **a way.** **And the attributes of that way had already been enumerated over four verses: obedience, being *ḥanīf*, thankfulness, not associating.**

---

## 16/124 — إِنَّمَا جُعِلَ ٱلسَّبْتُ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ ٱخْتَلَفُوا۟ فِيهِ وَإِنَّ رَبَّكَ لَيَحْكُمُ بَيْنَهُمْ يَوْمَ ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ فِيمَا كَانُوا۟ فِيهِ يَخْتَلِفُونَ

*Innamā juʿila'l-sabtu ʿala'lladhīna'khtalafū fīh, wa inna rabbaka la-yaḥkumu baynahum yawma'l-qiyāmati fīmā kānū fīhi yakhtalifūn*

"**The Sabbath was laid down only upon those who differed about it. And your Lord will judge between them on the Day of Resurrection concerning that over which they differed.**"

### The root of disagreement once more

**The root خ-ل-ف occurs three times in this one verse: *ikhtalafū*, *yakhtalifūn*, and the disagreement to which *fīmā kānū fīhi* attaches.**

**And the root had occurred earlier in the sūra: in the thirty-ninth verse (*yakhtalifūna fīh*), in the ninety-second (*mā kuntum fīhi takhtalifūn*), in the sixty-fourth (*alladhi'khtalafū fīh*).** **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

**And the work of this verse must be recorded:** the verse ties the **cause** of a ruling to a disagreement, and leaves the settling of the disagreement to the hereafter. **It thus arrives at the same place as the sūra's ب-ي-ن series.**

**سَبْت** — root **س-ب-ت**: **to cut off, to stop, to break off.** *Subāt* occurs in the Qurʾān as a description of sleep (al-Nabaʾ 78/9; al-Furqān 25/47) **and was treated at `025-furkan.md`, 25/47.** **The same root is here the name of the weekly day of stopping.**

**I record this as an observation about the language:** the picture in the root is the same in both — **the cutting off of work.** The one daily, the other weekly.

**The Qurʾān touches on this subject in several places (al-Baqara 2/65, al-Aʿrāf 7/163 and al-Nisāʾ 4/154).** **No detail is entered into, because al-Naḥl's verse does not enter into it either: *innamā juʿila'l-sabtu ʿala'lladhīna'khtalafū fīh* — a single sentence.**

---

## 16/125 — ٱدْعُ إِلَىٰ سَبِيلِ رَبِّكَ بِٱلْحِكْمَةِ وَٱلْمَوْعِظَةِ ٱلْحَسَنَةِ وَجَٰدِلْهُم بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ إِنَّ رَبَّكَ هُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِمَن ضَلَّ عَن سَبِيلِهِۦ وَهُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِٱلْمُهْتَدِينَ

*Udʿu ilā sabīli rabbika bi'l-ḥikmati wa'l-mawʿiẓati'l-ḥasanati wa jādilhum bi'llatī hiya aḥsan, inna rabbaka huwa aʿlamu bi-man ḍalla ʿan sabīlihī wa huwa aʿlamu bi'l-muhtadīn*

"**Call to the way of your Lord with wisdom and with fair admonition; and dispute with them in the way that is best.** Your Lord knows best who has strayed from His way, and He knows best those who are guided."

### Three instruments

**The verse enumerates three instruments for the calling, and each is given in a different form:**

| Order | Instrument | Form | Is there an adjective |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | *Bi'l-**ḥikma*** | *Bāʾ* + noun | **None** |
| 2 | *Wa'l-**mawʿiẓa**ti'l-**ḥasana*** | Coordinated with the preceding | **Yes** — *ḥasana* |
| 3 | *Wa **jādilhum** bi'llatī hiya **aḥsan*** | **An imperative verb** — a separate clause | **Yes** — *aḥsan*, the elative |

**Three things must be recorded, and all three can be verified from the text:**

**One — *ḥikma* is given no adjective.** The other two have one. **The lexicographers put this down to aptness being already present in wisdom itself** — the root of *ḥikma* is **ح-ك-م**: to put a thing in its proper place, to make it fast; *ḥakama* — to bridle an animal and so control it. **The word means "what is in its place"; no adjective is needed.**

**Two — the adjectives rise: *ḥasana* → *aḥsan*.** The second is the elative: **better, best.**

**Three — and the third item is given as a separate clause, with an imperative verb.** The first two are joined with a *bāʾ*, while the third is a command of its own: *jādilhum*.

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is these three differences of structure:** the first two are **the form of the calling**; the third is a separate work that comes into play **if the other side resists.** **Indeed the first two carry no pronoun for the addressee, while the third does: *jādil**hum***.**

**And the rising of the adjectives fits this:** as the work grows harder, the measure rises. **What is asked in disputation is more than what is asked in admonition.**

### بِٱلَّتِى هِىَ أَحْسَنُ — the fourth link

**This construction was treated at three places in this commentary, and a table of three links was drawn up. At `028-kasas.md`, 28/54, the table stood thus, and I rely on it:**

| Place | Command | Domain |
|---|---|---|
| Fuṣṣilat 41/34 | *Idfaʿ bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | **Answering evil** |
| al-ʿAnkabūt 29/46 | *Lā tujādilū illā bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | **Disputation** |
| al-Qaṣaṣ 28/54 | *Yadraʾūn* — د-ر-أ | **Evil** |

**This is the fourth link, and I complete the table:**

| # | Place | Expression | Domain | The kind of clause |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fuṣṣilat 41/34 | *Idfaʿ bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | **Answering evil** | **A command** |
| 2 | al-ʿAnkabūt 29/46 | *Lā tujādilū **illā** bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | **Disputing with the People of the Book** | ***A prohibition*** — with an exception |
| 3 | al-Qaṣaṣ 28/54 | *Yadraʾūna bi'l-ḥasanati'l-sayyiʾa* | **Warding off evil** | **A statement** — in praise |
| **4** | **al-Naḥl 16/125** | ***Wa jādilhum** bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | **The calling in general** | ***A command*** |

**And the difference of the fourth link must be recorded, because it is the contrary form of al-ʿAnkabūt's:**

| | **al-ʿAnkabūt 29/46** | **al-Naḥl 16/125** |
|---|---|---|
| The clause | *Wa **lā tujādilū** … **illā** bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* | ***Wa jādilhum** bi'llatī hiya aḥsan* |
| The form | ***A prohibition*** + an exception | ***A command*** |
| The addressee | *Ahla'l-kitāb* — definite | **Indefinite** — *hum* |
| The exception | *Illa'lladhīna ẓalamū minhum* | **None** |

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is the grammatical form of the two clauses:** al-ʿAnkabūt **forbids disputation and opens it again by an exception**; al-Naḥl **commands it outright.**

**The outcome is the same — a method is fixed — but the route differs.** **In al-ʿAnkabūt the default position is "disputing", in al-Naḥl the default position is "dispute in the best way".**

**And what was recorded in the al-ʿAnkabūt section holds here too, and I repeat it: the command is framed not as a prohibition but as a *condition of manner*. Disputation is not forbidden; its method is fixed.**

**And it was tabulated that the whole backbone of `040-gafir.md` is *jidāl*, and that what is condemned there is disputation without ground** (*bi-ghayri sulṭānin atāhum*). **At al-Naḥl 16/4 man's contentiousness was identified as an attribute; at 16/125 that attribute is given a form.** **This is a ring verifiable within the sūra, and it was treated above at 16/4 and 16/111.**

### إِنَّ رَبَّكَ هُوَ أَعْلَمُ بِمَن ضَلَّ

**And having given the command, the verse sets a limit.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order:** having enumerated three instruments, the sentence says **in whose hand the outcome lies.** **The method belongs to the one addressed; the outcome does not.**

**This limit was treated at many places in this commentary: `035-fatir.md` 35/8 and 35/19-23, `039-zumer.md` 39/41, `030-rum.md` 30/52-53, `046-ahkaf.md` 46/35.** **And it had already occurred in the thirty-fifth and eighty-second verses of this sūra as *balāgh*.** **I do not repeat it here.**

---

## 16/126-127 — وَإِنْ عَاقَبْتُمْ فَعَاقِبُوا۟ بِمِثْلِ مَا عُوقِبْتُم بِهِۦ وَلَئِن صَبَرْتُمْ لَهُوَ خَيْرٌ لِّلصَّٰبِرِينَ · وَٱصْبِرْ وَمَا صَبْرُكَ إِلَّا بِٱللَّهِ وَلَا تَحْزَنْ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا تَكُ فِى ضَيْقٍ مِّمَّا يَمْكُرُونَ

*Wa in ʿāqabtum fa-ʿāqibū bi-mithli mā ʿūqibtum bih, wa la-in ṣabartum la-huwa khayrun li'l-ṣābirīn · Wa'ṣbir wa mā ṣabruka illā bi'llāh, wa lā taḥzan ʿalayhim wa lā taku fī ḍayqin mimmā yamkurūn*

"**If you punish, punish with the like of what you were punished with. But if you are patient, that is better for the patient.** · **So be patient. And your patience is only by God. Do not grieve over them, and do not be in distress at what they plot.**"

### A comparison with the balance of al-Shūrā

**These two verses build the balance treated in detail at `042-sura.md`, 42/40-43, and I rely on that treatment. What was recorded there was this:**

> **"The sentence calls the recompense that is a man's right *sayyiʾa* as well… That the recompense should be called an 'evil' shows that using one's right and virtue are not the same thing. A man takes his right and is not blamed for it — the forty-first verse will say this outright. But what he takes is not named a good thing."**
>
> **"*Mithl* — equal, matching. And this word is the sentence's most important limit: the recompense must be equal. More than that is wrongdoing."**
>
> **"Immediately after the fortieth verse praises pardon, the forty-first defends the one who does not pardon. This order matters: had pardon been praised and left there, the man who takes his right would have been blamed by implication. The verse closes that door."**

**Now I compare the two places:**

| | **al-Shūrā 42/40-43** | **al-Naḥl 16/126-127** |
|---|---|---|
| The right of recompense | *Jazāʾu sayyiʾatin sayyiʾatun **mithluhā*** (40) | *Fa-ʿāqibū **bi-mithli** mā ʿūqibtum bih* (126) |
| The word of measure | ***Mithl*** | ***Mithl*** — **the same word** |
| The defence of the right | *Wa la-mani'ntaṣara baʿda ẓulmihī fa-ulāʾika **mā ʿalayhim min sabīl*** (41) | **No separate clause** — the conditional clause is itself the dispensation |
| What is held higher | *Fa-man **ʿafā wa aṣlaḥa** fa-ajruhū ʿala'llāh* (40) | *Wa la-in **ṣabartum** la-huwa khayr* (126) |
| The word for what is higher | ***ʿAfw*** — pardon | ***Ṣabr*** — endurance |
| The closing | *Wa la-man **ṣabara wa ghafara** inna dhālika la-min ʿazmi'l-umūr* (43) | *Wa'**ṣbir** wa mā ṣabruka illā bi'llāh* (127) |

**All six lines can be verified from the text. And three things must be recorded:**

**One — the word of measure is the same: *mithl*.** Both sūras limit the right of recompense by **equivalence.** **And what was recorded in al-Shūrā holds here too: more than that is wrongdoing.**

**Two — the name of what is held higher changes.** **al-Shūrā says *ʿafw* (pardon); al-Naḥl says *ṣabr* (endurance).**

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is the difference between the two words:** *ʿafw* is the **relinquishing** of a right — an act turned towards the other party. *Ṣabr* is **endurance** — a state turned towards oneself. **al-Shūrā, then, puts the relation at the centre, al-Naḥl the person.**

**And in al-Shūrā *ʿafw* was not counted sufficient on its own; *iṣlāḥ* was added beside it.** **In al-Naḥl *ṣabr* comes alone — but with a qualification: *wa mā ṣabruka illā bi'llāh*.**

**Three — and the two sūras meet at the same place.** **al-Shūrā 42/43 says: *wa la-man ṣabara wa ghafara inna dhālika la-min ʿazmi'l-umūr*.** **al-Shūrā too, then, arrives in the end at the word *ṣabr*.**

**It was recorded in the al-Shūrā section: the construction *ʿazmu'l-umūr* occurs in three places in the Qurʾān, and in all three it comes together with *ṣabr*.** **al-Naḥl's closing stands like a fourth textual counterpart to that set of three — without using the construction, but arriving at the same place.**

### وَمَا صَبْرُكَ إِلَّا بِٱللَّهِ

**I record this sentence separately, because it is a limitation that comes with the command itself.**

**The wording of the sentence is this: a command (*wa'ṣbir*) → immediately after it a limitation (*wa mā ṣabruka illā bi'llāh*).**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is that juxtaposition:** the verse commands patience and in the same breath says that the patience does not issue from the addressee's own source. **In giving the command it also states where the strength to carry it out will come from.**

**And the same structure was recorded at `041-fussilet.md`, 41/35:** *wa mā yulaqqāhā illa'lladhīna ṣabarū wa mā yulaqqāhā illā dhū ḥaẓẓin ʿaẓīm* — **the verb was passive: "it is granted".** **It was recorded there: "this stance is named not as a temperament that comes of itself but as something given."** **al-Naḥl says the same thing outright: *illā bi'llāh*.**

**This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

### وَلَا تَحْزَنْ عَلَيْهِمْ وَلَا تَكُ فِى ضَيْقٍ

**Two further prohibitions are added, and both are turned inward:**

| Prohibition | Root | What |
|---|---|---|
| *Lā **taḥzan** ʿalayhim* | ح-ز-ن | **Do not grieve** |
| *Wa lā taku fī **ḍayqin*** | ض-ي-ق | **Do not fall into distress** — a narrowing |

**The root ح-ز-ن:** the concrete sense the lexicographers give is **hard, broken, difficult ground** (*al-ḥazn*). **Grief thus carries in its root the picture of "ground that is hard to walk on".**

**The root ض-ي-ق:** narrowness, being pressed. **And at `027-neml.md`, 27/70, the same two prohibitions occur together** (*wa lā taḥzan ʿalayhim wa lā takun fī ḍayqin mimmā yamkurūn*) — **almost word for word the same sentence.** **This verse was treated in the al-Naml section, and the return of the root م-ك-ر within that sūra was recorded there.** **I rely on that treatment.**

**The exact overlap of the two verses must be recorded, and it is a verifiable fact.**

**And it was recorded in `057-hadid.md`: the Qurʾān records the grief of the prophets and does not condemn it** — and this verse (*"do not grieve over them"*) was named there as evidence. **I rely on that treatment and take the note over here:** the prohibition shows that the grief **exists.** **What does not exist is not forbidden.**

**مِّمَّا يَمْكُرُونَ** — and the root م-ك-ر occurs for the third and last time in the sūra (26, 45, 127). **This is a repetition verifiable within the sūra.**

| Verse | Expression | Who / when |
|---|---|---|
| **26** | *Qad **makara**'lladhīna min qablihim* | **Peoples of the past** — concluded |
| **45** | *A-fa-amina'lladhīna **makaru'l-sayyiʾāt*** | **Those of the present** — a warning |
| **127** | *Mimmā **yamkurūn*** | **Those of the present** — the addressee's situation |

**I record this as my own reading:** in the sūra the root occurs first as **a finished history**, then as **a threat**, and finally as **a reason for consolation.** **The same fact is named from three distances, and in the last the addressee is told "do not be pressed by this"** — because at the first occurrence its outcome had already been shown.

---

## 16/128 — إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ مَعَ ٱلَّذِينَ ٱتَّقَوا۟ وَّٱلَّذِينَ هُم مُّحْسِنُونَ

*Inna'llāha maʿa'lladhīna'ttaqaw wa'lladhīna hum muḥsinūn*

"**God is with those who guard themselves and with those who do good.**"

### The last sentence of the sūra

**The sūra closes with two attributes, and both had occurred earlier within it.**

| Attribute | Root | Its other occurrence in the sūra |
|---|---|---|
| ***Ittaqaw*** | و-ق-ي | **Verse 30** — *wa qīla lilladhīna'**ttaqaw*** |
| ***Muḥsinūn*** | ح-س-ن | **Verse 90** — *yaʾmuru bi'l-ʿadli wa'l-**iḥsān*** |

**Both occurrences can be verified from the text.**

**And the link with the ninetieth verse must be recorded, and I give it as my own reading:** the ninetieth verse commanded three things, and the second was *iḥsān*. **The hundred and twenty-eighth uses the same root as the sūra's last word: *muḥsinūn*.**

**The sūra, then, gives back at the end, as an attribute, what it had commanded.** **The command turns, at the close, into an identity.**

### The order of the two attributes

**And the order is worth recording: first *taqwā*, then *iḥsān*.**

***Taqwā* — root و-ق-ي: to guard oneself, to keep clear.** At the centre of the word stands **avoidance** — what is not to be done.
***Iḥsān* — root ح-س-ن.** At the centre of the word stands **the surplus** — what is to be done.

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is the direction of the words themselves:** the two attributes look in two opposite directions — the one towards drawing back, the other towards going forward. **And the sūra's last sentence names them together.**

**And this is the same structure as in the ninetieth verse:** there too three commands (forward) and three prohibitions (back) were given together. **The sūra keeps its account of the moral life two-directional to the very end.**

**مَعَ** — and the last word conveys a being-with: *maʿa'llāh*. **At `029-ankebut.md`, 29/69 (*wa inna'llāha la-maʿa'l-muḥsinīn*), almost the same sentence occurs.** **The two sūras close with the same attribute and the same particle.** **This is an overlap verifiable across the sūras.**

---

## The sūra as a whole — looking back

### The enumeration of favours — how it was cut off

**The sūra's backbone was tabulated above, in the introduction. Here only what the points of interruption do is gathered:**

| Interruption | Verse | Kind | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **11, 12, 13** | **A qualification** — *li-qawmin yatafakkarūn / yaʿqilūn / yadhdhakkarūn* | It ties the list to a capacity |
| 2 | **17** | **A question** — *a-fa-man yakhluqu ka-man lā yakhluq* | It turns the list into a comparison |
| 3 | **18** | **A ruling** — *lā tuḥṣūhā* | It declares the enumeration impossible |
| 4 | **65, 67, 69, 79** | **A qualification** — *yasmaʿūn / yaʿqilūn / yatafakkarūn / yuʾminūn* | The sections of the second enumeration |
| 5 | **71, 72** | **A question** — *a-fa-bi-niʿmati'llāhi yajḥadūn / yakfurūn* | Two questions one after the other |
| 6 | **81** | **A purpose** — *laʿallakum tuslimūn* | Why the enumeration is made |
| 7 | **83** | **A diagnosis** — *yaʿrifūna … thumma yunkirūnahā* | The enumeration closes |

**Seven points of interruption and four distinct kinds: qualification, question, ruling, diagnosis.**

**And the sequence itself builds a movement, and I record this as my own reading:** the sūra begins with **a qualification** (who understands), passes to **a question** (are they equal), arrives at **a ruling** (you cannot count them), ties it to **a purpose** (submit), and closes with **a diagnosis** (they recognise, then deny). **The enumeration thus advances from an operation of knowledge to an accounting.**

### Limits set per the method (`USLUP.md`)

| Verse | Subject | The limit set |

| **4** | *Nuṭfa* | Not equated with the terms of modern embryology (the limit set in `036-yasin.md` was repeated) |
| **8** | *Mā lā taʿlamūn* | The item left empty was not filled |
| **15** | The mountains | Not turned into a geological or physical claim |
| **43** | *Fa'sʾalū ahla'l-dhikr* | Not torn from its context and presented as a general principle of authority |
| **57-59** | The female child | The subject treated was not woman but the stance of a society |
| **66** | Milk | Not read as a description of physiology |
| **69** | Honey and healing | Not turned into a medical claim; kept within the three notes of the wording |
| **76** | The parable of the mute man | No ruling was laid down about a disabled person |
| **103** | *Aʿjamī* | No value-judgement was laid down about a language or a people |
| **106** | Compulsion | No legal ruling was issued; no personal name was attached to the account of the occasion |
| **116** | Lawful and forbidden | The verse itself was recorded as the ground of this commentary's limit against issuing legal rulings |
| **118** | A community | No wholesale ruling was laid down; the ruling was tied to an attribute |

### And where the sūra leaves us

**The sūra opened with news of an arrival: *atā amru'llāh* — it has come.** **And immediately a prohibition was laid down: do not seek to hasten it.**

**In the fourth verse a diagnosis was entered: man, the moment he comes out of a drop, is *khaṣīmun mubīn* — an open disputant.**

**Then the enumeration began and ran for thirteen verses, was cut off, opened again at the sixty-fifth verse and closed at the eighty-first. Twenty items were enumerated in between.**

**And in the middle of the enumeration there is a passage about a bee, two verses long. The sūra takes its name from there.**

**I record this as my own reading, and my ground is the sūra's own structure:** the bee is the smallest item on the list. **And the reason it was chosen is that it is the item that shows the logic of the enumeration most plainly** — for there a creature does what is communicated to it, and something of use to man comes out. **The favour, then, is enumerated not as a ready-made object but as the product of an order at work.**

**And the sūra's diagnosis is given in the eighty-third verse: *yaʿrifūna niʿmata'llāhi thumma yunkirūnahā*.** **The trouble is not a failure to see the list — it is the rejecting of it after seeing.** **And the ruling of the eighteenth verse had prepared this in advance: the list cannot be counted in any case; the point is not to arrive at a number.**

**The sūra's last four verses give a work to the man whose condition it diagnosed:** call (125), dispute — but in the best manner (125), if you answer back, answer with the like (126), be patient (127), and your patience is not of yourself (127).

**And the sūra closes with two attributes: *ittaqaw* and *muḥsinūn*.** **The *iḥsān* commanded in the ninetieth verse comes back in the hundred and twenty-eighth as an identity.**

**In the fourth verse man was named a disputant; in the hundred and twenty-fifth that same man is taught the method of disputation.** **The sūra neither ignores nor forbids the tendency it diagnoses.** **It binds it to a direction — just as the ways were shown to the bee.**
